Lena’s heart hammers inside her chest, discordant to the music, as she readjusts her mask back onto her face. “Wait for the song to end,” Kara finally says. Her body tenses as Lena doesn't meet her eyes. She looks down at her feet and swallows around the impossibility of them that has lodged itself inside her throat. Kara’s grip slackens and Lena wonders if she’ll be strong enough to last till the end of the song.

 

Everything is so damn complicated. Muddled and absurd. Lena can only sigh as they resume their swaying. She presses her face against Kara’s shoulder and feels her mask digs into her skin. The laces of her dress tighten against her chest. All Lena thinks about as Kara’s shuddering breath hits the side of her neck, is that she doesn’t want the song to end.

 

Screwing her eyes shut, all Lena thinks about is how much she wants…

 

She wants to keep dancing until her feet hurt. She wants them to keep dancing until they are so tired they fudge up the steps and do nothing more than sway to the music. She wants to keep dancing until the lights fade out and the guests leave and lights peek out of the horizon. She wants so much right now her chest feels too tight and her hands are not big enough to grasp onto Kara and hold her close – closer; till the fabric of her suit gets all wrinkled and distorted inside her fists. Lena wants so much right now. So much more than the need to hide in Lian Yu to escape those who want to harm her and her crew. So much more than the aimless wandering and solitude of the deep blue sea. She wants more. More. And it terrifies her. It terrifies her so much that Kara must sense it because she’s holding on to her a little bit tighter. As if they could stop time and the destruction and chaos awaiting them by holding on a little bit tighter… Lena grits her teeth. She wants so much more than to keep running away, afraid of her own shadow, petrified by what the future holds and by the Silver Banshee’s prophecy and the curse burning her forehead.

 

They could be so much more… So much more…

 

But the music is on its last note. Its last breath. It’s wheezing in the air like a man’s dying wish, and Lena knows she can’t have what she wants. She never had and she never will.

 

“Don’t leave,” Kara whispers, still holding her tight despite the dancers all breaking away around them.

 

“I have to.”

 

“No. Wait…” But Kara’s protests get lost within the sound of clinking glass, laughter, and stomping feet. Lena presses her hands against the unbending frame of the Girl of Steel and tears herself away from the embrace.

 

It hurts. It hurts so much Lena wants to scream, but the pain and confusion clouding Kara’s eyes only strengthens her resolve. Lena takes a step back. And then another. Kara is still among the bumbling crowd; her jaw is clenched and her eyes burn with something Lena is too afraid to name.

 

Turning around, Lena takes a deep, steadying breath as she walks away. Her chin trembles with every step she takes but she is too stubborn to cry.

 

She hears Kara call after her. She hears Kara ask her to wait, but Lena runs. She runs out of the ballroom and her eyes begin to burn.

 

Damn, they burn.

 

*

 

A tear rolls down her cheek. Lena tears her mask away and flings it against the wall. She doesn’t stop to watch it fall. She can’t keep doing this. She can’t let this pull, this unexplainable force, draw her to Kara anymore. She can’t keep cutting threads and say goodbyes and push her away when it feels like her heart breaks a little more each time. One day there will be nothing left. One day Lena will be as hollow and cold as the Silver Banshee’s soulless eyes and-

 

Another tear rolls down her cheek and Lena wipes it away. She clenches her jaw in the hope her tears would be intimidated enough to stop falling, but her eyes burn. And they won’t stop burning no matter what she does…

 

Lena takes off her shoes and hurls them at a plant. She misses and it angers her so much she stomps further into the dark corridor. Silence crawls on the floor and slithers on the walls. It creeps ever so closely, no longer disturbed by the tapping of her shoes on the marble floor. All Lena hears is the sound of her breathing, the rustling of her dress, and the pounding of her heart.

 

She can’t keep doing this.

 

One day, the shadows will swallow her whole and she won’t be able to escape it. It’s already there. So visible and she can’t escape it no matter how hard she latches onto a flicker of light, a glimmer of hope.

 

Lena rubs her forehead, feeling the raised flesh of her scar taunting her fingertips.

 

She is cursed. Her future has been set in stone. She has spent tears, blood, and thousand of prayers trying to prevent the inevitable. But she will be her brother’s downfall and Lena is not foolish enough to think it will be the only life she’ll ruin. She’s a danger to the people she loves and there is no guarantee that her curse will stop after claiming one life. She already has such a hard time keeping her crew alive. Lena has no guarantee that the curse hasn’t staked a claim on them all already. It would be irresponsible and insane to add one more life to that sinking ship. She can’t involve Kara in this. She won’t take the risk. And if any entities are out there in the sky, pitying their mortal soul, Lena hopes they hear her plea. She’ll pray. She’ll beg them if she has to… She’ll promise not to want too much, not to hope for more… Or love… She’ll stay away.

 

It might be already too late for her crew but it might not be for Kara…

 

Walking with that resolve, the coolness of the marble floor numbs the sole of her feet. It helps her think about the matter at hand: Queen Rhea’s ship has been spotted by the Reign an hour away from Almerac’s port. It doesn’t leave Lena a lot of time to prepare. She should’ve gotten to Luke as soon as she’s got words of their arrival. She should’ve already made her move but she got distracted.

 

Damn Kara and her blue eyes!

 

There’s a twinge in her chest that makes it hard for her to breathe. If only she could stop her heart from beating. It would make her life so much easier if she could shut out that part of herself. To not care. To be the heartless monster the Federation has painted her to be…

 

A set of boots squeaks against the floor. Lena freezes and looks over her shoulder.

 

Kara, stripped off of her mask, locks eyes with her – chest heaving from running. The pain Lena feels is sudden and unexpected, but Lena relishes in it - in the hammering of her heart stealing her breath away, in the slight trembling of her fingers dying to reach out for the woman at the other end of the hallway. It hurts to feel so much at once. It hurts to feel this alive, but right at this moment, Lena wouldn’t trade that sweet ache for anything in the world because Kara is smiling. She is looking relieved, like she’s finally found what she has been looking for, and it’s terrifying. No one has ever looked at her like that before - like the world made sense even when faced with the risk of an inevitable collision. But Lena can’t take that first step. She’s frozen where she is with disbelief and fear, with years and years of disillusions and disappointments, and with the absolute certainty that she isn’t deserving of all the terrifying promises Kara is so willing to offer.

 

But Kara is stubborn – maybe more than Lena is. Pressing her hand against her ribs, her mouth twisting with discomfort, Kara steps forward.

 

Lena doesn’t move. The thought of running away flashes through her mind before it is squashed into nothingness as Kara takes another step. Lena still doesn’t move. She doesn’t want to. Standing on the edge of such monumental transformation, Lena faces Kara head-on instead of running away and it might be the bravest thing she’s ever done…

 

“Run away again and I might begin to think you enjoy making me chase after you,” Kara says with a roguish smile. Lena lets out a shaky laugh as Kara closes the distance, a wince flashing across her face.

 

“It does flatter my ego.”

 

“Knowing I’ll catch you?”

 

Lena rolls her eyes.

 

“You were crying…” Kara trails off with a frown, hand outstretched toward her cheek, but Lena slaps it away and pokes her ribs instead. Kara hisses with a barely contained flinch. Lena immediately ruffles through her clothes, unbuttons her coat, and lifts her shirt mid-way, hoping her insight might be wrong.

 

A deep yellowish bruise mar Kara’s right side.

 

Lena pinches her lips.

 

“What happened?”

 

“Depends. Are you going to run away again if I answer your question?”

 

“I have things I need to get to.”

 

“So do I. But I think we also ought to talk.”

 

“What happened, Kara?” Lena repeats a little bit more forcefully this time but Kara still doesn’t look like she is willing to give her an answer. Lena huffs, her fingertips skimming over a puckered wound that hasn’t completely closed yet. “It’s recent.” And it bothers Lena because she had made sure to thwart the attack Queen Rhea had planned on New Kandor. She had called in a few favors and she had led Jack and Sam’s crews – as well as her own – as close to the Federation as she could without being detected. She had made them wait at sea for days on end just to have a chance to cut off the Daxamites and Dominators’ path to the city of Kryptonian refugees. She had done it so that Kara wouldn’t have to see more of her people die. She had done it to lessen the weariness she had seen weighing down Kara’s shoulders. She had done it so that Kara wouldn’t get hurt and Lena doesn’t understand why Kara looks so bruised or why there are new scars etched onto her skin when she has moved heaven and earth to protect her.

 

It makes Lena wonder if they might be more alike than she first thought. Lena doesn’t know if Kara would consider it a curse or a blessing to survive even when everything she has known and loved has gone. To be the only one standing. The only one remaining. Over and over again.

 

“There were spies amidst the Armed Forces,” Kara says, making Lena lock eyes with her and stop breathing. “They attacked while I was escorting Princess Xalia to a meeting with Councilwoman Marsdin.”

 

From what Lena can see and feel – her palms can’t help but run over Kara’s skin, inspecting, memorizing, reassuring herself – it had been a violent and brutal encounter. Kara, usually so good at concealing any pain and discomfort her body is subjected to, can’t even maintain the façade long enough for Lena not to worry.

 

“I… I tried to stop them,” Kara says, hushed and small. She takes Lena’s hand and presses it against her heart. “I tried, but I couldn’t save her.”

 

“You can’t save everyone,” Lena whispers, fighting the urge to cry; repeating what Luke has told her countless of times to reduce the guilt searing her heart.

 

Kara smiles, full of sorrow. “I can try.”

 

In the silence of the empty corridor, Lena’s soft and relieved gasp might as well as sounded like a lightning bolt. All they can do is try… It might hurt. They might fail. But Kara is still trying. Kara has never stopped trying. Feeling the soft beating of Kara’s heart against her hand, her skin burning hers, Lena stares into Kara’s eyes and is amazed by the woman standing before her. Resilient. Unrelenting. And so heartbreakingly kind.

 

“I feel bad for making you run after me,” Lena apologizes. She cups Kara’s cheeks and notices the still healing bruises on her cheekbone and at the edge of her hairline now that they are standing so close. No longer wearing their mask.

 

“Don’t,” Kara responds. She leans into the touch and Lena’s heart beats harder. “It was worth it.”

 

Tongue-tied, Lena doesn’t know what else to say. If someone had told her months ago that she would have this secret affair with one of the most preeminent figures of the Federation, Lena would have laughed in their face. It’s just so… unbelievable.

 

And yet, here they are.

 

“Careful, Commodore Zor-El. I might begin to believe you like chasing after me.”

 

Kara shrugs, her eyes shining with mirth and affection. “It has grown on me.”

 

It is inevitable. The collision. The shattering of past-selves and misconceptions.

 

In the empty hallway of the Palace, so far away from the festivities, Lena rises on her toes, her nose brunching against Kara’s, and swallows the soft sigh escaping from Kara’s parted lips.

 

It was inevitable.

 

And Lena has fooled herself for so long, believing she could run away from this. She has fooled herself into thinking she couldn't care less for a blue-coat. A blue-coat who happens to be one of the most powerful defenders of the Federation. A living legend who had passed through fire and had risen from the ashes. It feels inevitable and Lena is terrified. But before she can let her mind stop her from wanting this, Kara – the woman who stutter and blush when embarrassed, who had clung to her so hard in her sleep that she tore her stitches and had been game for any challenges Lena had thrown her way – presses her against the wall and ignites fires with brushes of her lips and Lena feels so much more than fear. In that delicious moment where she feels Kara’s tongue touch her lip, Lena wants to be brave.

 

Even if it goes against reason, Lena hooks her leg over Kara’s hip and draws her in, needing her to be closer. Even if it goes against every hard life-lessons Lena has learned. It’s so far from the cynicism and emptiness she has grown accustomed to that being with Kara – her hands cupping her ass and making Lena move her hips with intent against her thigh – feels like jumping off of a cliff and expecting to fly.

 

Maybe they can. If they stick together they might make the impossible possible… But between harsh breaths and barely concealed moans, all Lena hears are Kara’s insistent encouragements to keep going; hips grinding against thigh. As if they ought to make up for the months they’ve been apart, for the hurt they felt, for the love that had built and found no release.

 

It’s ridiculous and reckless and mind-numbingly good. Lena’s toes curl and her arms wrap around Kara’s shoulders while her body shakes and shivers. Her hips keep moving as she ekes out a bit more pleasure from Kara’s hard thigh while Kara smiles against her neck. It's ridiculous and exhilarating and decadent. Lena shifts her leg and it doesn't take long for Kara to grind against her thigh and come with a soft moan burning the crook of her neck.

 

The candlelight flickers in the hallway. The shadows are silent and still and the fear is still there but it is muffled. Held so tightly in Kara’s embrace, Lena doesn’t let herself think too much on what this might mean for them. She doesn’t let the fear creep back in. For once, Lena lets herself feel and Kara makes her feel like she might be able to let herself fall entirely.

 

“Good talk,” Lena finally says after having found her breath again. It makes Kara shake with laughter. Lena cards her fingers through blonde hair and hums contentedly as Kara peppers her collarbone with kisses. “If I had known it meant sex…”

 

Kara chuckles before meeting her eyes, looking hesitant. “I don’t regret it. Do you?”

“Okay.” Kara lets out a breath and smiles. “Good.”

Kara’s coat is wrinkled and bunched so hard in places that it is hard for them to make it look presentable enough and not make their coupling obvious. But they manage it, with quiet laughter and stolen kisses. Lena wipes away at the smudge of red stains she had left on Kara’s face and restyle the hairdo she had taken so long to fashion.

 

A loud clatter echoes against the wall. The shadows creep back in. It reminds Lena, her heart skipping a beat, that there is a war to be stopped, a mad queen to arrest, and a world to save.

 

They have lingered long enough and indulged themselves more than they should have. Lena feels the fear rush back into her veins like an old friend.

 

“So…” Lena exhales, hiding the trembling of her fingers by smoothing her dress. She inspects her appearance one last time through the reflection of the window and sees Kara glance at her. “What’s our plan?”

 

“Our plan?” Kara asks with a delighted smile.

 

Lena rolls her eyes and walks away. “Well, better work together since it seems like you have a knack for finding me no matter what I do…” She doesn’t see Kara’s reaction but she can picture it: twinkling blue eyes and an even bigger smile. Somehow, it makes it easier for Lena to breathe around the panic pulsing inside of her. It makes it easier for Lena to fight against the irrepressible desire to flee.

 

A warm hand settles at the small of her back and Lena hides a smile as Kara falls into step with her.

 

“I don’t know,” Kara muses with a shrug, “it’s just… something tells me we’re stronger together.”

Lena has always likened Kara to fire. From the mesmerizing dance of a flame to the destruction of a wildfire. Months ago, trapped in Roulette’s brig and watching Kara pace in their cell, Lena remembers toying with the Commodore and push at her buttons until she’d snapped. It had felt like playing with fire, stoking up the flame till Kara had been nothing but fury and had glared at her with so much intensity Lena had felt like she was going to burst into flames. She had played with fire and Kara had burned her so bad with hard-hitting truths that Lena still remembers doing everything she could to create some distance, some space, between them. She still remembers thinking that, despite her curiosity and desire to peak at the cracks in the carefully constructed Girl of Steel persona, Kara Zor-El wouldn’t be worth the pain. She has had enough of that to last a lifetime.

 

But Lena doesn’t know what to think anymore. Kara is leading her down halls and corridors while holding her hand, fingers interlocked, and Lena can’t find any reason not to let her. If anything, her traitorous heart is making her hold on tighter, soaking up the heat of Kara’s skin like a seed that needs fire to sprout.

 

“How many are you?” Kara asks but Lena is so distracted that Kara glances at her from over her shoulder and squeezes her hand. “How many are you in the Palace, Lena?”

 

It’s embarrassing how Lena has to think about it before she can answer. “Five.” She feels the tension travel into Kara’s arm before she sees her clench her jaw. “What?”

 

Kara huffs. “Five? Lena, really?”

 

“What? It’s not like I can march in here with a full pirate crew, Kar-” Her eyes widen as Kara presses her hand against her mouth and pushes her until her back hits a wall.

 

Footsteps echo against the walls. Lena stiffens as she hears voices and laughter. Body tensed, Kara is breathing heavily. Their chests are fused together and Lena can feel the erratic beating of Kara's heart against hers. The voices get closer and closer. Kara becomes steel. Lena threads her fingers inside blonde hair and scratches the tension away. It doesn't happen right away but Kara does loosen up just as the voices stop a few feet from them. “The party’s that way, folks.” A male voice tells them before another one snicker, prompting hollers and lewd comments from others.

 

“Let them be,” a voice cuts in, making Lena heart stop and the hair on arms rise. “We have more pressing matters, gentlemen.”

 

The footsteps fade away.

 

“That was close.” Kara looks over her shoulder, grabs her hand, and leads her down a flight of stairs. But Lena barely registers any of it as the voice she’s heard minutes ago still echoes inside her mind.

 

“What was the plan? Kill Rhea in the middle of the crowd?”

 

“Yeah,” Lena replies, distracted. Still trying to find why that voice is still echoing inside her mind like a warning.

 

“And how would you have gotten out?”

 

“I don’t know!” Lena grits out, stopping in the middle of the hallway. “I would have cooked up something.” Kara looks at her unimpressed. Lena sighs, rubbing at her forehead and feeling her scar burn her fingertips. “It’s called improvising, love. I’m really good at that.”

 

“You are,” Kara concedes. Lena looks up and sees Kara staring at her and at the way she’s nearly clawing at her scar…

 

Lena presses her palm against her forehead and wills herself to stop.

 

“What’s wrong?”

 

“Nothing.” Lena takes a steadying breath and walks away. She doesn’t know what is wrong with her or why her inside seems to be twisting into knots. How can she try to explain it when she doesn’t know what’s even happening? The voice had felt familiar and…

 

The sound of clacking pots and sizzling grease on hot pans expels that feeling out of her mind. “This way,” Lena says, breathing in the aroma of grilled meat and spices floating in the air.

 

Cooks and maids pay them no mind as Lena guides Kara past the kitchen to the cellar. Luke should still be there, waiting and securing them a way out.

 

Lena knocks once. She opens the door and stops short at the sight before her. Kara bumps into her back.

 

“What-”

 

“Finally!” Captain Danvers grabs them both by the arm and pulls them into the room. The door closes and Captain Danvers presses her back against it, pistol in hand. “These two have been at each other’s throat for the past ten minutes!”

 

Lena frowns, staring at Jess staring down the barrel of a gun and aiming her rifle at a woman with short dark hair.

 

“Stand down, Lieutenant Vasquez!”

 

Lieutenant Vasquez doesn't seem to hear the order. Her arm is steady and her eyes blare with fury. Lena can understand why Lieutenant Vasquez doesn’t even spare Kara a glance. The last time they had seen her, they had thrown her overboard The Intrepid. So it doesn't surprise her when Lieutenant Vasquez spits out, “Commodore, she was planning on blowing up the ballroom.”

 

“I was setting up a distraction!” Jess growls, glaring at the Lieutenant.

 

“By killing hundreds of innocents?”

 

Jess huffs, rolling her eyes, but her aim is steady. “I wasn’t going to do it in the middle of the crowd!”

 

“The plan was to set the explosion inside the fountain,” Luke says, trying to placate both women. But there is a dagger in his hand and the tension increases. “The blast would have forced the guests to back away from the windows-”

Lena doesn’t know if Kara might be cursed but Lena knows that she is. And the realization is so heavy, so disheartening that Lena cannot move. There is already so much blood on her hands and there will be more blood to come. With the blood of your blood on your hands, you will be the last sunrise he’ll see, the Silver Banshee had said. Fear courses through her veins and all Lena hears echoing inside her mind is that she is cursed. Cursed. Cursed. She is like a spectator: unable to move, to speak, or stop the disaster from happening.

 

No matter what she does, people die around her.

 

“Enough!” A voice roars from behind her and her heart gets lodged inside her throat at seeing Kara jump into the fray. A warning barely gets past her lips before Lena sees Jess’s finger twitch on the trigger, surprised.

 

Two gunshots ring in the air. A howl echoes against the stones, full of horror and agony. Lena presses her hands against her mouth, her fingers trembling against her parted lips as silence befalls onto the cellar.

 

The smoke clears. Kara is alive. She has veered both guns upward and she is unharmed.

 

And yet Lena feels like she is the one bleeding.

 

“Luke,” she croaks out. Her Quartermaster wrenches the rifle from Jess’ hand and pushes her behind his back.

 

From the corner of her eyes, Lena can see Captain Danvers doing the same with Lieutenant Vasquez who is looking back at Kara with wide, shocked eyes.

 

“Kara,” Lieutenant Vasquez whispers, “I’m sorry.”

 

Lena doesn’t hear Kara’s replies. All she hears is the Silver Banshee’s chilling laughter and Lena fears that if she were to look at Kara, to stubbornly want her, to... Lena fears that all she would see would be blood: Kara lying dead on the ground, the sound of her blood still gurgling inside her throat. They have been tempting fate. Lena had known it from the beginning and she can’t look at Kara and not think that they are being arrogant and reckless. They’ve been painting giant targets onto their back and it is only a matter of time before their luck runs out.

 

It’s too much. The pain is too much. And the fire Lena can feel licking her inside isn’t welcome anymore. The seed of warmth she feels every time Kara smiles turns into ice onto barren lands.

 

“We can’t stay here,” Lena grits out. Looking around the cellar, she spots her bag leaning against a crate. Her knees wobble. Lena clenches her jaw so hard that blood fills her mouth and she is able to make it to her bag without crumbling. “Someone must have heard the gunshots. We have to go.”

“I know,” Luke whispers, his voice warm and comforting.

 

Lena doesn't find any comfort in that knowledge. It only makes her grief grow and sob harder.

“The Commodore can handle it,” Luke replies. “They came prepared.”

“Tell them to backup the Commodore’s fleet. We’re going in.”

All eyes are on her.

“What are you doing here?” The woman wheezes out with blood running down her temple and crashes against a beam. “We thought you left.”

 

“I did,” Lena grunts, pushing a Daxamite away before Captain Danvers cuts him down. “I came back. I-”

 

“It doesn’t matter,” Captain Danvers grits out and picks up a hammer lying around and throws it at the back of a Daxamite. Jess whirls around as he falls to her feet. “You need to find Kara. “Get them out of here,” Lena orders.

 

“Aye. Follow me,” Victor calls out to the remaining officers.

 

“You go with them.” Jess tries to protest but Lena shakes her head, watching them make their way to the bow of the ship while Daxamites try to stop them. “Someone needs to protect them.”

 

“What about you?”

 

Lena gives Jess a trembling smile and reload her pistols. “Go.”

 

Jess hesitates, about to say something, but she only heaves a sigh. “I don’t know if I want to hug you or slap some sense into you.”

 

“Let’s do both when I’ll come back.”

 

“Sure thing, Captain.” Jess squeezes her hand before running back to Victor and the retreating officers.

Chaos is reigning above deck. The flames are eating at the flesh of the ship and Lena knows she doesn’t have a lot of time before the flooring begins to cave in from beneath her feet. Beyond the smoke and the flames, Lena sees Daxamites carrying chests back toward their ship. Up near the Captain’s Cabin, Lena sees Rhea make her retreat back to the ship.

 

Lena fires. Once. Twice.

 

Rhea stumbles onto her feet. The queen looks at her wounds and then around her. Her gaze lands on Lena, eyes cold but full of rage. Her guards haul her to her feet and carry her back to the ship. Lena tries to stop them but the wood cracks beneath her feet. A burning beam crashes through the deck, making the wood splinter.

 

Lena whips around at the sound of gunshots. Squinting through the smoke, Lena can only make out shadows standing on the quarterdeck. She makes her way there, pulled by some inevitable force.

 

Standing on the stairs, Lena sees Kara trying to fight a man off of another man lying on the ground. Even among the chaos Lena recognizes the uniform easily. The crest embroidered on his cape is a dead giveaway.

 

Admiral Kal-El.

 

Taking one more step, Lena's heart stills as there are no more doubts about whom Kara is fighting against.

 

Lex taunts the Girl of Steel, wiping away the blood at the corner of his lips, while watching her drag her cousin away. But Admiral Kal-El is practically dead weight. His feet beat against the wood uselessly as Kara tries to get him to stand up.

 

“Two heirs of the House of El for the price of one,” Lex jeers. “Seems like fate has spoken Kal-El!”

 

The ship tilts to the side. The wood groans beneath their feet. Barrels upon barrels come crashing against the masts and splinter on impact. Lena holds on to the railing. Lex holds on to the helm while Kara and Admiral Kal-El skid down to the edge.

 

Despite the smoke, Lena sees her brother load his pistol. Kara is trying to protect her cousin from the fire; unaware of the pressing danger at her back. Lena runs the last set of stairs and barrels into her brother.

 

Eyes wide, Lex stares at her. It makes her chest wither and crumble, as recognition illuminates his features. He utters her name, his voice clear despite the crackling of burning wood.

 

“Is that you?” he asks in disbelief. “What- What are you doing here?”

 

Lena opens her mouth but no sound comes out. She stares at her brother, uncomprehending. Strangely, Lex doesn’t die from looking at her. His body isn't stricken by lightning or crumpling from a horrible disease or turning to ashes at the sight of her. No. Lex is fine. Lex is breathing and still full of arrogance. Her scar burns but Lena doesn't know if it is because of her curse or the heat of the burning ship. Lena doesn't know what to think anymore as Lex scrambles to his feet.

 

The fire keeps burning and Lex pushes past her.

 

“I’d love to chit-chat, but you have the worst timing, little sister.” Lex aims his pistol toward the last heirs of the House of El. Lena scrambles to her feet. Nothing makes sense, but Lena realizes that she doesn't care. She grabs her brother by the back of his coat and tugs him backward. She uses the momentum to stand in his line of fire.

 

“I’m here for her,” she says, holding her brother’s gaze. “I won’t let you kill her.”

 

The fire surrounds them, spitting embers and smoke and burning holes through the floor. It doesn’t matter if Kara is worth the pain or not, Lena realizes. It doesn’t matter that she is terrified out of her mind. It doesn’t matter if they are cursed or not. None of it matters. Hearing Kara suddenly scream her name while she stares at the barrel of Lex’s gun, Lena finally understands that maybe she was bound to burn.

14. Part III : 2

Notes for the Chapter:

Hey! A new chapter to entertain you while in quarantine. I hope you are all safe and healthy. I wanna thanks Rebellion Bear for the help and all of you who are still reading and commenting and leaving kudos. It feels like catching up with friends :)
Anyway, enjoy the new chapter.

 

The universe hangs off the barrel of Lex’s gun.

 

Lena stares at her brother. For a strange second, images rush through her mind like they should mean something. As if they were intimately hers– had been hers if she had lived through several lives and more. Lena sees her reflection standing up to her brother over and over again. Like she is now. Standing in Lex’s line of fire feels less like a choice and more like the echo of a promise she has failed to keep. In this strange second, dozens of images of Lex bathed in red, of his eyes becoming glassy, and of his lips turning purple, flashes inside her mind. It’s gory. It is horrifying. Her vision blurs with tears. Lena feels helpless and she feels like screaming, but then they are gone. In a blink. As if they never existed in the first place. And Lena is back. Back on a burning ship with Kara screaming her name and with Lex pointing his gun at her, his eyes filled with blood lust.

 

Kara screams her name. Again. Her voice, strong and piercing, dissolves into coughs.

 

Lex’s attention keeps shifting. His eyes dart from her to Kara and he hesitates. It’s not something Lena ever expected to see from her brother. If there is one thing Lena never forgot about Lex it’s how stubborn he is. Even when they were young, Lex had always been unyielding, hard-set on how the world should be. It had been comforting to be in the shadow of someone who seemed to know everything about everything. It had made Lionel’s absence easier to bear and Lillian’s constant disapproval matter a bit less when Lex used to tell Lena how great she was meant to be. But more than ten years have passed and a lot has changed. Lena would be foolish to think that Lex is the same person he used to be.

 

The echo of something inexplicable contorts his face. It’s monumental and brief, but it is enough to make him lower his gun and sigh– uncharacteristically.

 

“You owe me one,” he says and looks around. The grimace Lena sees on his face is telling. The ship is burning. It’s crumbling from beneath their feet, and Lex takes a step back. “A favor for a favor, Lena. I’ll be waiting onboard the Alexander.”

 

Lena startles as he runs to the edge of the ship. “Lex! Wait!”

 

He winks at her and jumps in the water. Lena is left befuddled and disoriented. She stares at the spot where her brother had been and cannot comprehend why he hasn’t dropped dead. Lex had seen her, but the reunion she has dreaded for years has been underwhelming. Confusion and anger swell inside of her. The Silver Banshee’s words echo inside her head with the poignancy of an ill-fated prophecy, and yet her brother is fine. He even winked at her before leaving. It’s mind-boggling. Had she been lied to? Had this been a hoax? Had she been driven away from home for nothing?

 

A shocked sob crawls out of her throat.

 

What kind of life could she have had if she’d never left?

 

Lena has never allowed herself to think about it. She couldn’t have because if her self-banishment was the price to pay for her brother’s life then it would have been worth it, but Lex is fine. Lex is fine, and Lena is left to wonder if the past ten years have been for nothing. The wandering, the hurt, the loneliness?

 

Lena hisses in pain as something prickles at her skin.

 

She is on fire.

 

Flames are licking at her clothes, her skin feels like it is melting off of her bones, and tears blur her vision so much she only sees a spectrum of red. Lena tries to beat the flame off her sleeve, but her breathing is becoming labored. The fire is all-encompassing. It is closing in on her faster now that she is aware of her surroundings. It’s like the fire has become sentient and it knows how faint she is becoming.

 

A coughing fit brings her to her knees.

 

Fire is cleansing, the Infernians had said. Some plants need fire for their seeds to sprout; we need fire to burn away our sins. To begin anew. To show us the way, even when it hurts.

 

Lena thinks of fire and it’s Kara who appears in her mind: candlelight illuminating blonde hair, the swoosh of a cape, the lingering scent of honey and mint, arms holding her tight, a warm lip against the tip of her tongue... She should have told Kara. She should have stayed and fought. Fought against the Daxamites, and against the fear and doubts raging inside of her. She should have stayed by Kara’s side. She should have-

 

Her mind is dizzy and her body is weak. For a split second, an inexplicable feeling overwhelms her…

 

She is floating in the air, held in a familiar embrace…

 

“Lena!”

 

Relief isn’t the word. Lena doesn’t know how to describe what she feels at hearing Kara scream her name, at feeling her footsteps thunder on the wooden boards, at the inkling –as crazy as it may sound –that Kara has found her and always will.

 

Hoisted back onto her feet, Lena can only stare at her disheveled guardian angel. “You’ve found me,” Lena says, feeling the words scrape the inside of her throat. Infernians may have found mercy in fire; Lena thinks she’s found it in the blue eyes staring right back at her. She touches Kara’s soot-stained cheek to make sure she isn’t hallucinating. She isn’t. Kara’s cheek is scorching the inside of her palm.

 

“We have to go.”

 

Kara doesn’t wait for a reply to pick her up. Lena tightens her grip over Kara’s shoulders. She breathes against the skin of her neck, but the smoke has left its mark everywhere. Lena can smell it on Kara’s skin, on the fabric of her clothes, on her hair… It’s smothering. It is making her wonder how Kara can even move, or run and jump on this hellish ship while also carrying her. It’s nothing short of extrao-

 

“Hold your breath!”

 

Lena takes a huge breath as Kara jumps off.

 

For a split second, a sensation of déjà-vu overwhelms Lena. Suspended in the air, the ridiculous belief that Kara can fly emerges like some universal truth.

 

They hit water.

 

The current is taking them under.

 

Kara’s arms become slack. The feeling is so jarring that Lena’s instinct kicks in. She tries to grab Kara before the current can separate them, but the water is turbulent and Kara slips through her fingers.

 

Lena reaches the surface. She gulps for air, her eyes frantic, as she scans her surroundings.

 

Kara is nowhere to be seen.

 

“Kar-” Lena ducks under the water to avoid the blast of an explosion. She fights the current and coughs up seawater. Scanning the surface, Lena shouts again. “Kara!”

 

Bobbing with the rise and fall of the sea, Lena tries to quell the panic overtaking her mind. The fire has engulfed the ship and it is now acting as a torch feeding the warm light of dawn.

 

If she doesn’t find Kara now, she might never find her.

 

Lena dives deep and looks up, using the light from the fire to make out shapes underwater. There’s wood, cordage, and cloth everywhere, but out of the corner of her eyes Lena sees something akin to a body. She shoots off in that direction, disregarding the terrible thought that it might not be Kara. She doesn’t want to think about the possibility of her making a mistake. Of her swimming toward a body that has been long dead. She doesn’t want to picture Kara, adrift at the bottom of the sea, on her own, paralyzed and powerless.

 

Getting closer to the floating body, her anxiety gives way to certainty. She grabs Kara under the arms and pushes her to the surface. Lena kicks the water, her lungs burning with the lack of air, and pushes, but it feels like she can’t swim fast enough—

 

Until they break to the surface.

 

Wide blue eyes stare at her, but Lena finds none of the intensity or soulfulness she is used to seeing. Her eyes are open but her gaze is lost, and if it weren’t for Kara’s shallow breathing, Lena might have feared the absolute worst. But Kara is breathing, and for having already experienced this, Lena knows Kara can get out of that state. She just needs time. She needs Lena to keep her afloat and that’s what she is going to do. She is going to keep Kara afloat until Kara can come back to her.

 

“I’ve got you,” Lena repeats, over and over again. For Kara. For her own peace of mind. She doesn’t really know… She drags Kara toward a large, floating piece of wood, and hauls her onto it.

 

Kara’s pulse is strong. It’s like she’s fighting an invisible battle, a battle happening inside her mind, her heart. And though Lena can hear voices in the distance, her gaze doesn’t stray from the woman who has turned her life upside down.

 

Worse. Commodore Kara Zor-El has completely destroyed her.

 

She has broken through every wall Lena had created. All the doubts Lena had about them trying to make it work were merely attempts to raise those walls back up. To minimize Kara’s influence on her life. To pretend her heart hasn’t been starving for something more than the life she’s been living… And it had been so easy. It’s what terrified Lena the most, how easy it had been to fall into Kara’s gravity. To want to challenge her and all she represents only to find herself changing. To follow Kara’s example and share all the broken pieces she’s been trying to hide for so long.

 

Kara has destroyed her. But Lena doesn’t feel grief. She isn’t in pieces. The ashes left in the wake of Kara’s destruction –those she had been so afraid of— have given her a new breadth, a new understanding. Kara has destroyed her and Lena has never felt more alive.

 

“Come back.” She slides her fingers through Kara’s hair and kisses her temple. “Come back to me…”

 

Kara blinks. Once. Twice. Her body tenses and Lena immediately releases her hold as Kara comes to. Violently. Her breathing turns harsh and her eyes are panicked, but she is back. Lena smiles, relieved, and watches Kara take in her surroundings as she whispers I’m fine over and over again. Coughing and spitting a bit of water with every breath, her forehead finally thumps against the wood. Lena gets closer only to stop as Kara’s grip whitens on the broken plank and their eyes meet.

 

“You left.”

 

Lena holds on to the broken plank to keep herself afloat.

 

“Why? You said—” Kara presses her forehead deeper against the wood as her eyes flutter closed with exhaustion or defeat, Lena doesn’t know.

 

“Kara, I—” She pushes a lock of blonde hair back behind Kara’s ear, and feels her heart lurch as Kara shies away from her touch.

 

Voices call out their names, sounding closer than they were before. The rescue is imminent, and yet all Lena hears is Kara’s lingering silence.

 

***

 

It is strange how… numb she feels. Every existing sound has been reduced to a shrill ringing inside her ears. The sea is voiceless and the debris littering the surface makes no sound when they move and bump and sink. Lena knows that her arm falling into water should make a sound —some kind of splash. The droplets falling on the wooden board Kara is lying on should mimic the pattering of rain. But they don't. The world is soundless. Kara is shouting and waving her arm in the air, and still Lena doesn’t hear a thing –only the sound of her heartbeat. Boats appear in the distance. People are rowing and pointing and shouting. Lena should feel relieved at the sight of them. She should feel relieved at seeing Luke and her crew among the Federation’s rescue party. Luke looks like he’s exhorting their crew to row faster. His tensed, worried frame is so familiar Lena feels like she can hear his voice echoing inside her head.

 

Kara slides off of the plank and nudges her shoulder. Her mouth is moving. Lena realizes that Kara might have been speaking to her, maybe for a while, judging by the furrowing of her brow.

 

“Are you well enough to swim?” Lena reads on Kara’s lips. No, Lena wants to say. The inside of her chest is one giant open wound and the saltwater seems to rub the pain in. Without walls or defenses, the uncertainty Lena feels when she looks at the woman who has brought them down is crippling.

 

“Yes,” Lena manages to say, relieved that Kara doesn’t comment on how disembodied her voice sounds. But Kara has noticed, of course she has. Her blue eyes are appraising. Her brows furrow even more, so much so that the urge to soothe them overtakes her, but she resists it. Kara has made it clear that signs of affection weren’t welcome, and underneath the hurt Lena feels at being rejected, she understands that Kara is setting a boundary.

 

Lena can’t help but think back on their time inside Lord’s ship. Of Kara telling her about the girl she used to be. She had been so open back then when she hadn’t needed to be. She had shared pieces of herself; unafraid of the pains it brought forth, and it had reminded Lena how to feel again. To remember not to lose herself in anger and despair. She had been so used to keeping herself in check, to not letting the waves of emotion rise to an unsupportable height and destroy everything she had worked so hard for, that she had been surprised to see the waves fall in a breath. With a whispered conversation.

 

This situation could have been avoided if she had talked to Kara instead of leaving. But she hadn’t. She had let the anxiety rise in a monstrous wave and she had closed herself off trying to hold it in. By leaving, she has forced Kara to do the same. And Lena can see it. The rising tension Kara is holding in feels like an impenetrable wall.

 

I need to talk to her… Lena realizes. It might not fix the trust she has broken or make Kara forgive her, but she owes it to Kara to try. To do what Kara had done for her.

 

Breathing deeply through her nose, sounds begin to trickle in again. The sea sings again amidst the chaotic rhythm of drifting wood. Lena hears the oars slicing the water to the rhythm of Luke’s urgings, and Kara is calling her name. Softly. Repeatedly. Oscillating between concern and forced detachment.

 

“Yeah… Sorry. I’m fine. Are you?”

 

“I’m fine,” Kara replies with a thin-lipped smile. “Let’s go.”

 

Lena follows after the Girl of Steel.

 

Despite being afraid of water, Kara really is a strong swimmer. Her strokes are powerful and precise. She’s swimming like she can’t get out of the water fast enough. Like her first instinct is to swim, swim, swim, and not drown. I used to love the water, Kara had said, inching the door to her soul open for her to see, and in retrospect that had been it. Lena had fallen with her whole heart without knowing. She had wanted to know more. She had drunk in Kara’s words. And she had added up all the parts. The legend the world created about the Girl of Steel. The hard choices Lena saw Kara Zor-El make. The woman Kara had let her have a glimpse of. All those facets had created a whole Lena found breathtaking. It had been fascinating and humbling to see someone of Kara’s stature so willing to be… vulnerable. Mind-blowing to realize Kara had trusted her not to use it against her.

 

I’d never seen waves that could reach the sky.

 

Kara lifts herself out of the water and climbs onto a boat, smiling and thanking those rushing to help her. She stands tall, squeezes the water out of her hair, and ties it back with a string provided by one of her officers.

 

Lena can hear her give orders and ask for status reports and—

 

A strong grip lifts her out of the water by the back of her coat and drops her in warm fabric and too many pairs of arms. Looking around, Lena recognizes her crew, all bereft of their coats, trying to get her to warm up. Luke steps in, his sleeves wet from fishing her out of the water, and hugs her so tight Lena loses her breath.

 

“Stop,” Lena wheezes out, laughing. “It’s worse than being trapped on that ship.”

 

It’s not funny, but her crew breaks in laughter. Lena hears the fear lingering in their guffaws. She sees the stress lines around Luke’s eyes as he rolls his eyes and flicks her forehead. She sees Kara, looking at her from the other boat with a question in her eyes.

 

Lena shrugs with a small smile before her attention is turned back to her crew.

 

“That was crazy!”

 

“We didn’t see you jump off the ship!”

 

“For sure we thought we’d find you looking like a roasted pig!”

 

Lena snorts at the ribbing. “Keep laughing. I’ll make you all scrub the hull for a year at the very least.”

 

The threat falls into deaf ears as they keep on with the ribbing but Lena doesn’t mind. Looking at all the coats laying at her feet, Lena knows they know it had been a close one. Lena doesn’t think she would have still been alive it hadn’t been for Kara.

 

“Quiet you lot!” Lena sags under the weight of another coat as Luke orders the crew around. “Pick up your oars and row!”

 

The crew goes back to work sheepishly. It gives Lena a bit of time to take a breath, to look around her, and observe. Their rowing boat is flanked by the Federation. Lena doesn’t see the Intrepid or the Reign, only looming Federation ships.

 

Luke forces her to sit and picks up the coats lying at the bottom to drape them over her. “They are leading us to the Kryptonian ship and—” his voice turns gruff, “they took Jess and Hector with them already. Captain Danvers didn’t give us a choice.”

 

Lena sighs.

 

Applause is heard as they get closer to the Federation fleet. Kara is waving to those cheering her on, looking proud and bashful at the same time.

 

“How are the Reign and the Intrepid? Have you seen them?”

 

Luke points west. The two ships are at a fair bit of distance, but Lena can see their sails being hit by the first hints of sun. They look all right, huddled close together.

 

“The blue-coats are tensed as fuck. They tried to chase after the Daxamites but see? The Luthorian armada is right there—” he points to several shapes encased in darkness, still obscured by the coastline. “A Luthorian ship came close but they didn’t attack. The ship retreated back to the armada… We’re trapped inside the bay, for now…”

 

Lena hums, musing over this new bit of information. They must have gotten close to get Lex back… Lena can’t imagine the panic the Federation must have felt at seeing an enemy ship converge toward the wreckage where they knew their Commodore was still trapped. It’s a wonder the Luthorian ship hadn’t been attacked knowing how trigger-happy some Federation captains can be. Lena holds the coats tighter around her body. “I saw Lex—” her voice breaks. She clears her throat. “I even talked to him, Luke. He was there.”

 

His shoulder knocks gently against her and Lena lets out a breath.

 

“What did he want?”

 

“I don’t know but he’s waiting for me…” Luke tenses at that. Lena looks at the boat in front of them. “As unbelievable as this sound, he spared Kara for me. I owe him a favor.”

 

“Will he attack if you don’t go?”

 

“He might. I don’t know…” She hunches over her seat and presses her forehead against her knees. She tries to stop her body from breathing too quickly. She tries not to succumb to the sudden vertigo overtaking her. Lena tries to remain calm but her scar burns, and she doesn’t know what to do. “I really have no idea…”

 

Luke rubs her back. Lena smiles at the comforting gesture. She tries to focus on the weight of his hand, on its warmth, but all she can smell or taste is saltwater and smoke.

 

“Look up.”

 

Lena stares, speechless, as the El Mayarah looms over them.

 

The morning mist seems to part to reveal a ship three times the size of the Singing Nightmare. Colors of blue, red, and gold shine under the rising sun. Like a beacon in the dark. Lena has only seen Kara’s ship from afar or from the lenses of her spyglass. Each time, she has been more focused on the Commodore than the ship herself. But now that they are getting closer, Lena feels like she is standing at a giant’s mercy.

 

It’s not the three rows of cannons that Lena finds intimidating. Humbling. There’s something else. It’s like the air around them is affected by the vessel. As if the wind was cognizant of the legacy of the El Mayarah and was carrying her pains and hopes on its shoulders. It’s more than just a ship… Lena can see it in the way the tension in Kara’s shoulders seems to loosen. In the way she seems to gain in confidence the closer they get. It’s hard not to feel Kara’s spirit exuding from the El Mayarah. Lena doesn’t know who has influenced the other. Did Kara mirror the gravitas and intensity she found in the red wood of her people’s ship? Or are Lena’s feelings toward the ship influenced by her knowledge of the woman who commands her?

 

Kara waves at the crowd of officers peering over the railing. Lena sees Captain Danvers among them, bruised and bloody, but wearing a relieved smile on her face. Kara’s responding grin is as bright as the sun. It makes Lena smile to see Kara so happy. It nearly makes her forget the tense moment they had in the water.

 

“Captain!”

 

Lena looks up and lets out a chuckle at seeing Jess and Hector at the railings. Jess shrugs Hector’s arm off of her shoulders with a roll of her eyes.

 

The welcoming party onboard the El Mayarah is as nice as Lena expected. She is used to the stares. The fear. The contempt. It is a testament to her skills and influence. Blue-coats have been indoctrinated to think of them as a menace, and Lena, in her whole pirating career, has done nothing to make them think otherwise. If anything, she might even have given fuel to some of the rumors heard among blue-coats. It’s funny to see Hector puff up his chest whenever an officer gets too close. It is even funnier to witness Jess make officers look away with a glare. With them flanking her sides, Luke standing at her back, and a small part of her crew huddled close behind her; Lena isn’t worried about being pestered. The El Mayarah, even at first glance, seems to run like a well-oiled machine, and no officers linger long enough to instigate any sort of confrontation. Not seeing Kara around, Lena tries not to let it dim her amusement…

 

That is until Lieutenant Vasquez approaches with two officers to ask them to relinquish their weapons.

 

“They already have ours,” Jess says with a huff. “It’s like saving them for the second time isn’t enough…”

 

Lieutenant Vasquez doesn’t react to the criticism but her reply does sound like an apology. “It is a security measure. Not all Captains of the Commodore’s fleet think as highly of you as Commodore Zor-El seems to.”

 

Feeling a tap on her shoulder, Lena leans back just enough for Luke to whisper in her ear.

 

“We give them our weapons and then what? Are we prisoners here?”

 

“Just play along,” Lena whispers back. She relinquishes her weapons. Her crew does the same and once they are out of earshot Lena gives them a pointed look. “They are destabilized. Look at them. They are trying to hide it but they are panicking.” All around deck, blue-coats are carrying orders and relaying messages between ships. A restless energy permeates the air, mixing with the smell of gunpowder and smoke. Lena wouldn’t be surprised to see the tension crank up a notch from a wrong look or some misguided comment. “Luke, from what you told me I don’t think they were expecting an attack of this scale so soon. Admiral El was in a bad shape when I got there. And from what I understood of the Commodore’s plan, this foray was meant to ambush the Daxamites. Not the other way around.”

 

“A few of Captain Danvers’ officers were injured during the fight…” Hector says. “We saw them be taken to the infirmary. Only Captain Danvers has come out yet…”

 

“And the Commodore?” Luke voices, trying to catch a glimpse of the elusive figure.

 

Lena looks around as well and bites the inside of her cheek when her search isn’t fruitful.

 

“She isn’t doing so well either.” Lena doesn’t speak about the bruised skin and bones Kara hides underneath her clothes. She doesn’t speak of Kara’s vacant look, of Kara clinging to the driftwood as a lifeline… Lena doesn’t say any of it but it is heavy on her mind. “I think that whoever is in charge right now is trying to make it seem like they still have the situation under control.”

 

A man burst out of the Captain’s cabin. His gaze lands on her and Lena tries not to smile at the distrust and scorn she sees in his eyes. Clearly, he is someone of importance for officers straighten at the sight of him. And faintly, as if carried by the wind, Lena hears them refer to him as Captain Olsen.

 

A second figure comes out of the Captain’s cabin. Lieutenant Vasquez approaches them again. Alone this time.

 

“Your weapons are under Commodore Zor-El’s supervision. They will be given back to you when you leave the ship or if an outside threat arises. These are the conditions the Captains have managed to settle on.”

 

“Well, thank you.”

 

Lieutenant Vasquez gives her a nod, impassive as always. Her gaze lands on Jess.

 

“I’m sorry for what happened in the Palace,” she says with a frown as if the idea of apologizing was foreign to her. “You were trying to help and I was too stubborn and biased to listen. It won’t happen again.” Lieutenant Vasquez holds her hand out and Jess stares at the gesture, bewildered. The pause stretches on so much Lena has to nudge Jess toward the Lieutenant to make her shake her hand. “If there is anything I can do to make your stay more comfortable, please let me know.”

 

“I… uh… Sure,” Jess stammers with a surprising blush coloring her cheeks. “Thanks.”

 

Lena looks on with interest as the handshake turns into some kind of handholding… The spell is broken when Hector clears his throat, loudly. Lena hears Luke muffle a laugh behind her.

 

Lieutenant Vasquez releases Jess’ hand with a deepening frown, trying to scowl away the red Lena can see blossoming on each of her cheeks. “I am to escort you all down to the infirmary. Follow me.” And she walks away.

 

“Wait!” Lena says, making the woman glance at them from over her shoulder, perplexed. “We’re fine. We don’t need to be checked on. We just want to go back to our ship.”

 

“It’s an order from the Commodore,” Lieutenant Vasquez replies with a sigh, as if she had expected them to refuse. “Please, don’t make this difficult.”

 

Lena suppresses a huff and clenches her jaw instead. There is some petty part of her that wants to make this difficult. Kara’s cold dismissal has hurt more than Lena is willing to admit and if being difficult is enough to draw the Commodore’s attention then—

 

Lena startles as Luke pats her on the shoulder.

 

“Just play along, hmm?”

 

Lena rolls her eyes at him, but looking at the still-closed door of the Captain’s cabin Lena gives in and follows the Lieutenant down to the infirmary.

 

“Commodore Zor-El has arranged a room for you to rest if you wish to.” Lieutenant Vasquez opens a door, revealing a small room filled with supplies and a few hammocks. “I’m going to notify the surgeons…”

 

Lieutenant Vasquez leaves only to stop short, turn around, and eye them. “Is there someone called Luke, here?”

 

Luke glances at Lena before replying. “That would be me.”

 

“We have a lot of officers injured. Commodore Zor-El has informed me of your skill set. If you’re feeling up to it, we’d appreciate your help. Your work will be compensated.”

 

Lena goes to lie down in a hammock, already knowing Luke’s answer is going to be.

 

“I’m parched. I take my payment in rum.”

 

“That can be arranged,” the lieutenant says with a laugh. “Come on.”

 

*

 

Hours pass.

 

She is bone tired. Weary. Lena doesn’t remember the last time she’s had a full night sleep. Nights without worrying when Queen Rhea will act on her threat or wondering if it wouldn’t be best to hide her crew from the world. At least till the end of the conflict. Lena doesn’t think she’s had a restful sleep since she met Kara. Lena doesn’t think she’d ever have a restful sleep knowing Kara will always, always, stand in the line of fire. She has spent hours thinking about it. Thinking of Kara and of the sacrifices she is willing to make. Lena doesn’t know if it is out of duty, or plain guilt, but Kara’s bravery so many times resembles self-destruction.

 

The Girl of Steel sparks hope, Lena can see that. Kara is admired. Revered because she stands between the Federation and all who threaten its citizens when so many others would have backed down in fear for their life. She has stepped up when no one else had. There have been rumors of her never giving up, even when all had seemed lost. But what they don’t seem to realize is how heavy the burden can be. Lena had seen it. Her fight against Eli had made her see Kara in a new light.

 

Going into the fight, she had been prepared to help Eli rebuild his confidence and self-esteem. She had been prepared to pick up the broken pieces, but Eli had thrived after the fight. He has become more confident, stronger... Lena hadn’t been prepared for Kara to be the most affected by the outcome of the fight. She hadn’t been prepared for Kara to be so… broken. Facing Eli had awakened something in Kara that Lena wouldn’t have noticed if it hadn’t been for the night they spent together. Kara had been desperate to hold on to someone. To find meaning in being the Girl of Steel. The persona Kara had created had looked like a burden on what used to be steady shoulders. Eli’s willing sacrifice seemed to have made Kara question what she’s been willing to sacrifice to be the Girl of Steel.

 

If we weren’t who we are… Would we have had a chance?

 

That question haunts Lena every night. Kara had been begging for a life where the Girl of Steel wasn’t all she had to be, and Lena had denied her that. Out of fear. She had made Kara choose between being the Girl of Steel and being with her when the choice wouldn't have changed what Lena felt anyway. Lena has never cared for a peaceful life, for a cottage near the beach, or quiet nights around a bonfire. If being with Kara meant getting thrown in the thick of danger then Lena wouldn’t have minded it one bit.

 

But she had made Kara choose. And not hours ago, she had left Kara on her own with the choice she had forced her to make.

 

A choice Lena fears will cost Kara her life.

 

But Lena doesn’t know how to even broach the subject. She doesn’t even know how to begin to apologize. There have been full on speeches going on in her mind, but knowing she has pushed Kara away –knowing how unsteady she had felt— for her own self-preservation, makes them seem ridiculous. Kara’s earlier rejection is more proof to how much she fucked up.

 

A surgeon appears at the door to check on them and give them more food. But most of her crew is fast asleep –some sharing hammocks and others leaning against crates.

 

She should be sleeping. Lena doesn’t remember the last time she’s had a good night sleep.

 

The surgeon leaves and Lena follows after him.

 

Officers note her presence but they don’t stop her from wandering the halls. Lena loses her way a few times. Kryptonian ships seem to be built with a different layout than most. The corridors are more circular; more connected to other parts of the ship, and Lena has a hard time finding the stairs marking the different levels of the ship. It’s confusing, and yet soothing. Lena grazes the walls, feeling the beautiful carving with the tip of her fingers. It looks like Kryptonese. Some carvings are paired with symbols –obvious sign of direction, but others don’t seem to perform any kind of function. They look like texts. Some even have a few ornaments…

 

Illuminated by lanterns, some letters seem to glow with gold. Lena runs the tip of her finger on one of the letters and startles at hearing someone speak from behind her.

 

“Those are prayers,” Kara says with her hands behind her back. Looking impassive. “This one protects the ship from oncoming storms.”

 

“I…” Cheeks getting warm from being caught unaware, Lena smiles, relieved. “I’ve been looking for you.”

 

Kara raises an eyebrow at that.

 

“I clearly wasn’t successful, but… You have a beautiful ship.”

 

“Thank you.”

 

The conversation withers just as it had started –abruptly.

 

The light flickers around them. Waves knock against the hull. Kara is silent. Stock-still. Looking at her with the kind of wariness one would expect from someone who has been duped too many times.

 

“I got lost…” Lena whispers, tongue-tied and thrown off. It makes Lena wonder if Kara can shrug off the hurt she feels like she can shrug off any physical injury.

 

“Would you like me to show you the way back to your crew?” Kara doesn’t wait for an answer and walks away. “It’s this way.”

 

“Wait! Kara. Just wait.”

 

She is stalling. Lena isn’t proud of it, but Kara is so… unreachable. It’s worse than the time they were locked in Roulette’s brig. It’s worse because the fire Lena is used to seeing in Kara’s eyes is not there. Kara looks so dispassionate. So distant. She looks like she can’t wait to get this over with. Kara hasn’t even turned around and all Lena can see is the tightness in her shoulders.

 

“I shouldn’t have left. I got—” Lena smiles at how painful it is. She doesn’t know how Kara does it. How she can be vulnerable and open without breaking, but she has to try. Kara has made her step away from the edge, from completely losing herself once. Lena will try to bring back the woman she loves by bringing down the walls she helped her build. “I was scared. Scared of you.”

 

Kara shifts at that, but still doesn’t face her.

 

“I don’t think you realize the impact you’ve had on me,” Lena says after a deep breath. “I didn’t realize it either. I have been going through the motions for years. Years.”

 

Two officers round the corner. They salute as they pass by Commodore Zor-El, and walk away. Lena clenches her jaw and rubs her sternum.

 

“Is there somewhere we can talk?”

 

She hates how faint her voice has become. She hates the way Kara sighs and walks away, only to open a door and tell her to get in with a motion of her chin.

 

The door closes and they are in what looks like a room full of broken things. Yellowed, musty maps are stocked in shelves. Rusty tools and broken parts are lying on a desk… If Lena wasn’t feeling so uneasy, she would have laughed at the irony of it.

 

Kara glances at the door and then at her, crossing her arms over her chest.

 

“I don’t have much time. We are monitoring the Luthorian armada.”

 

Lena nods, wetting her lips as they have become dry. “Have they showed any sign of movements?”

 

“None.”

 

Lena wonders if she should tell Kara that Lex isn’t likely to move since he is waiting for her. She hasn’t even had time to process that. Lena doesn’t think she wants to. Thinking about it is enough to make her heart race and her legs weak and—

 

“What is it?” Kara asks her with insistence. The wood creaks with the sudden step she takes and Lena instinctively steps back. Their eyes meet and Lena sees remorse in warm, blue eyes.

 

“Lex is waiting for me.” Kara looks surprised and Lena smiles humorlessly. “He told me so before he jumped off the ship.”

 

Kara nods, once, digesting the news. She begins to pace, her brow furrowed, and her fingers pinching at the bridge of her nose.

 

“What does he want from you?”

 

“I don’t know.”

 

“Will you be in danger if you go?”

 

“I don’t know.”

 

“Will you go?”

 

“I don’t know.”

 

If the room wasn’t so small and the silence so suffocating, Lena wouldn’t have heard the growl of frustration Kara lets out. But it is, and the rising tension emanating out of the Girl of Steel is all Lena can see.

 

“He spared you for me. You were in his line of fire and-”

 

Kara stops pacing and whirls around to face her with a snarl. “Did you make a deal with him to save me? You shouldn’t have! You shouldn’t have been there in the first place! You left! You were gone and I was handling it! But now Rhea is gone! She left with barrels of kryptonite powder that your brother gave to her! My cousin is unconscious! He was supposed to guard the coast of National City in my absence but he is here and injured and his fleet is nowhere to be seen!”

 

Kara slams her lips shut as she seems to realize she has been screaming at her. Her body is so close Lena can feel the heat radiating from her skin. Kara takes a step back, pressing her fist against her mouth as she turns away from her.

 

A few hours earlier, Lena wouldn’t have hesitated in taking Kara in her arms. Kara had told her of being afraid of things spiraling out of control, of feeling powerless and unprepared, and that is what is happening. There have been new bruises Lena has noticed on Kara’s face, to the side of her jaw and her eyebrow. Lena doesn’t even want to imagine what new scars Kara is hiding underneath her coat. She seems beyond exhausted, at her wit ends, and Lena wants nothing more than carry a bit of that burden for her. But Kara has resumed her pacing. Her lips are stretched in a thin line and...

 

She can deal with Kara’s anger. She can weather the storm.

 

“I’m sorry. I shouldn’t have left.”

 

“Why did you come back?”

 

The speech Lena has spent hours rehearsing inside her head becomes ludicrous. Kara is staring at her, expectant, and yet looking like any answer Lena will give her won’t be enough. Like her mind has been made up and she can’t be swayed. It feels like fighting a losing battle. It feels like Kara has already given up on them…

 

“I came back for you,” Lena says in a whisper. “I thought I could go back to the life I used to have and rid myself of the feelings I have for you. It would have been so much easier…” A tear rolls down her cheek and Lena wipes it, looking at Kara’s profile whose gaze has turned toward the wall. “You get so used to feeling alone, to feeling hurt, to feeling like the world is against you… you learn to love your misery. It took me a while to realize I wanted more than that. Meeting you changed everything. I’ve been fighting this change with my whole being when all I wanted was to embrace it.”

 

She takes a step closer, Kara doesn’t move but the tension seems to have bled out of her shoulders. Their eyes meet and Lena smiles.

 

“I’m not the person I used to be. I don’t want to be that person again. I’m sorry for hurting you while I figured that out.”

 

Kara exhales. It’s drawn out and heavy.

 

A hole opens up in the pit of her stomach.

 

“I’ve changed too—” Kara smiles. Sad. Resolute. “Ever since I met you, I have ignored direct orders. I let you escape from detainment, knowing full well I was putting those I left behind in danger. I let you enact your revenge against Lord and so many people died, Lena. I bore the weight of those deaths because you being safe mattered more to me.”

 

Lena feels like she can’t breathe, as if she had ingested some poison and she can feel it spread faster because of her beating heart…

 

“I chose you instead of doing the right thing. And if given the same choice now I would still choose you,” Kara continues, still sounding so sad and resolute Lena doesn’t find solace in her words. “I chose you and it had felt so right I convinced myself we were meant to be. You and I. Written in the stars…” Kara laughs, looking like she is chastising herself for having such thoughts.

 

“I was ready to chase after you, you know… If Alex hadn’t knocked some sense into me. I would have run after you and tried to convince you to stay,” Kara says with a self-deprecating shrug. “I craved this so much”—she gestures to the both of them, only accentuating the distance between them when there used to be none–“you and your wit and your companionship. I was ready to let them all down.”

 

Kara bites her lip, holding back tears.

 

“Because I wanted to be selfish”—a tear rolls down her cheek—“because I wanted you more than they needed me.”

 

The silence is overwhelming.

 

She has made Kara cry. That alone is enough to make Lena feel weak and dizzy.

 

“What are you saying?”

 

“I’m changing, Lena.” Kara inhales deeply. Lena feels like the person facing her is not the woman she has fallen in love with anymore but the legend people love her for. “I’m changing and I don’t like the person I’m becoming.”

 

Kara walks out the door, and Lena can’t find the strength to stop her.

 

***

 

The afternoon sun beats on her face as she waits, leaning against the railing of the deck. Lieutenant Vasquez told her a meeting was about to take place, and still Lena waits. From her position, she can see Lex’s armada –glaringly still on the horizon. Deck-hands and officers are all at their posts, waiting for one of their leaders to step out of the Captain’s cabin where Admiral El has been recovering.

 

She waits, stuck in a daze, wondering if she should leave. Kara doesn’t want her, and all Lena feels right now is the sting of rejection.

 

She closes her eyes, soaking in the sun, and wonders if she can revert back to the person she used to be, if it is possible to go back in time and not cross Kara’s path. Would it be possible? What would it change? Would she be in Lian Yu right now? Would she be drinking, only to wake up in Jack’s bed like she used to? Would she still slip out of his room to wash away the taste of rum and regret before the day even begins?

 

If she hadn’t crossed Kara’s path, would she even be alive?

 

The more she thinks on it, the less sense it makes. As much as she hates to admit it, their life has been inextricably linked from the moment Rhea chose her to carry out her vengeance… Or was it before? When Lex and Kal-El had become enemies? Or did Krypton’s destruction send them on this ineluctable path? Lena can only marvel at the likelihood of meeting Kara after everything they have been through. If she wasn’t so cynical, she would think it was fated.

 

But Lena doesn’t want to believe in fate. Because if she does then everything the Silver Banshee has told her will become true, and she has spent a decade fighting against it. She has run away from home and took to pirating and killed so many people in trying to cheat fate. She cannot believe it, can she?

 

Lena opens her eyes, and her vision whitens, blinded by the sun.

 

If she had to go back in time, how far in the past would she have to go to make sure she’d never cross paths with Kara Zor-El?

 

Decades. Lifetimes maybe.

 

“Lena…”

 

Lena looks over the railing and sees Maggie climbing up the ladder.

 

“How have you been?”

 

“Better.”

 

Maggie snorts at that. “Haven’t we all?”

 

They share a smile. Lena is glad to see Maggie looking so much better than the last time she’d seen her. It feels like so long ago. Years instead of months. So much has changed since then.

 

“With everything going on, I would have thought Marsdin would’ve kept you in Durla.”

 

“Ah,” Maggie shrugs and leans against the railings as well. “It is in her interest that Commodore Zor-El wins this war.”

 

Lena hums.

 

“And I had a personal interest here as well,” Maggie adds, quietly.

 

“Oh?”

 

“It’s complicated.”

 

Lena doesn’t pry. Maggie has always been fiercely independent; keeping things close to the chest to the point of making Lena wonder what her plans were. That’s why she is so good at what she does. At blending in with pirates and aristocrats alike. But for all the shifty things Maggie does behind the scenes, she is constant to a fault. She is loyal and fiercely protective, and seeing her so uncertain and unsettled, is surprising.

 

The door of the Captain’s cabin opens. Captain Danvers walks out and immediately gives out orders. She glances their way and gives them a short nod before going up the quarterdeck.

 

The soft exhale Lena hears from Maggie is a clue. Added to the lingering glance Maggie gives Captain Danvers, Lena thinks she knows what interest Maggie had in working with Kara.

 

“We are friends, aren’t we?” Maggie asks.

 

Lena wonders what Captain Danvers did to unsettle her so much.

 

“I’d like to think so…”

 

Maggie gives her a smile, looking grateful that the confirmation of their friendship is a constant that hasn’t changed. “Have you ever had the feeling of meeting someone and knowing you could be happy just having them in your life?”

 

The rhetorical question hangs in the air. Lena bites her lip, glancing at the closed door of the Captain’s cabin.

 

“I felt that… for the first time…”

 

Lena glances up the stairs of the quarterdeck. Captain Danvers is talking to the helmsman, oblivious to their conversation. “What happened?”

 

“I wasn’t…” Maggie sighs. “I tried. I asked myself if I could… I wasn’t ready for what she wanted out of what we could be.”

 

Maggie shifts. She turns away from the quarterdeck. Lena mirrors her position, digging her forearms into the railing, and looks to the horizon.

 

“Change is daunting,” Lena smiles as Maggie glances her way, and pauses, thinking on her words. Even though she entertained the fantasy of never crossing paths with Kara, of being as drastic as going back in time, Lena wouldn’t change a thing. Deep down, even with Kara’s rejecting her and all, Lena cannot deny that the change she has been experiencing –now that she has embraced it— has been quite freeing. Fighting against it had been more exhausting and soul-consuming than the pain of realizing what she had always known to be true: her love for Kara. She wished she would have handled a few things differently. She wished she’d never hurt Kara in the process. She wished such deep transformation were easier to go through, but sometimes… “Sometimes, it is a risk worth taking.”

 

Maggie gives her a tight-lipped smile and looks away. “Not if I come to resent her for it.”

 

Sound of boots thumps against the wood around them. Seagulls fly over their head. A door opens and closes.

 

Maggie nudges her shoulder with her own and gives her a more confident smile. “I’ll be ready… One day. In another life maybe.”

 

They are being called to the meeting. The door of the Captain’s cabin is wide open and Lena stares as Maggie walks away, frozen by her words.

 

*

 

Lena doesn’t know what to expect when she crosses the threshold of the Captain’s cabin. She was surprised to even be invited to the meeting, but Captain Danvers held the door open for her, and Lena had no choice but to step inside.

 

The room is breathtaking in its beauty. It is spacious and warm and delightfully foreign to her. It is so different from the Reign that Lena wonders if the El Mayarah might be a ship in a league of its own.

 

The writing on the walls glows in the light. It accentuates the rich red wood of the ship and blue fabric in the room. Lena wonders what the writings might be about -what protections they might be bestowing.

 

A large table stands at the center of the room. It is old and beautifully carved. Walking up to it –the back of her hand brushing against the wood –Lena can only imagine what that table must have been a witness to. The fall of empires. The destruction of a country. The rise of a phoenix…

 

Among all these beautiful things, her eyes find Kara’s. The Commodore is at the other end of the table, standing next to... Admiral Kal-El. The resemblance is striking. It is in the set of their jaw, in the eyes... There is something so uniquely them that Lena realizes she is in the presence of the last known descendants of one of Krypton’s most esteemed Houses. She looks around the table, recognizing most of its occupants. The others don’t step forward to offer their name and Lena doesn’t ask, but they all stare at her.

 

Despite his injuries, the Admiral stands up from his seat and greets her with a friendly smile Lena does not expect.

 

“You were this tall,” Kal-El says, his hand lowering to the height of the table, “the last time I saw you. It is peculiar the way in which we cross paths again.”

 

“It wasn’t of my own volition,” Lena says, trying not to squirm under the scrutiny. She tries not to glance toward Kara, but the Commodore’s glare is distracting. “To be involved in all this. Not at first.”

 

“So I’ve heard…” Kal-El motions for them to sit, but Lena remains standing.

 

Chairs scrape against the wood and Lena is the only one standing. Maggie gives her a reassuring smile but her body is locked tight, ready to fight, ready for… anything.

 

Admiral Kal-El opens the floor. Of all the captains seated at the table, only one seems to be wary of her. Lena, for once, doesn’t blame him. Crossing the threshold, she had been expecting a full-on interrogation. She had been prepared to fight for herself and stand her ground. But nothing. There wasn’t a fight to be had, and seeing everyone in their seat, sipping at drinks an officer is pouring for them, Lena gingerly sits down. Captain Danvers gives a detailed report of their current situation. Maggie follows suit and all the other Captains. There is no talk of the situation in the Federation. Of why Admiral Kal-El is here and injured and without the presence of his fleet. Lena realizes that despite their transparency, there are still things she isn’t privy to.

 

Sounds drift away. Lena knows she should listen to what they are saying, but her eyes are drawn to Kara. The Commodore is acting differently in the presence of her cousin. It’s in the way she speaks to the officers around them and to her Captains. The familiarity and playfulness Lena has been accustomed to seeing when she interacts with them is gone. Kara seems more reverent and withdrawn. She commands respect in a way Lena had never seen before, at least not from her. More than ever, it feels like Kara is playing a role, and Lena wonders if it is the person she is seeing right now who Kara aspires to be.
