Maggie clears her throat, making Lena glance her way and frown at the insistent look she is giving her. Looking around the table, Lena realizes that everyone is staring at her. Kara raises an eyebrow at her lack of attention.

 

“I’ll repeat,” Captain Olsen says with a smile that is meant to be mocking. “It has been reported by many officers that you and your crew have been a great help in evacuating the burning ship. I was wondering… What do you have to gain from helping us?”

 

“Well,” Lena bites the inside of her cheek as she feels more than sees the smugness radiating off of Captain Danvers, seated next to her. Lena clears her throat. She is stalling and she knows it. Does she tell them she’s been motivated by the only desire to protect one of their own? Because somehow, in all this, she has fallen in love, and her heart won’t find rest until that said love is safe and sound? It is too much. She might have accepted her feelings for Kara but she isn’t ready to show any signs of vulnerability in front of these people. Lena clears her throat again, feeling at a loss for words.

 

“I promised her…” Kara intervenes to Lena’s utter surprise and relief. “I promised them the Federation’s full pardon if they were to help us in dealing with Daxam. Which they have. Many times. Sometimes to great sacrifices.”

 

Lena stares at Kara, but Kara doesn’t meet her eyes. Lena nods when Captain Olsen shifts his focus back on her with narrowed eyes. Lena tries not to let any of the surprise show on her face. What is Kara doing? They’ve never agreed to anything...

 

“Only the High Council is in a position to grant pardon,” Captain Olsen says in a grumble, looking to the Admiral who –Lena realizes –has been staring at her all this time.

 

“The High Council put me in that position the second they brushed off my warnings of the Daxamite threat,” Kara says, her voice rising as her hand closes in a fist over the table. “I made allies when the High Council was too busy chasing after ghosts.”

 

“Not that I don’t agree with you Commodore,” Maggie pipes up, “but I wouldn’t call the armada waiting for us ghosts.”

 

Lena holds the Admiral’s gaze but her attention shifts, Kara’s jaw is locked tight and from where she is seated Lena can see her jugular pulse beneath her skin.

 

The corner of Admiral El’s lips lift in a soft smile. Lena grabs her cup and hides her embarrassment by taking a sip.

 

He puts his hand up, putting an end to the rising tension in the room. “What of their movements? Do we know what Lex wants? Besides killing me?”

 

They have no clue, Lena realizes as she listens to them lists Lex’s possible motivations. Kara hasn’t told them what she has told her about Lex’s reason for staying here. A map is laid out over the table. They are now debating the merits of different escape routes. Lena glances at Kara, furtively, only to see her stare right back at her, while everyone is hunched over the map.

 

The message couldn't be any clearer. Kara doesn’t want her to say anything, and Lena doesn’t understand why. In withholding that information, Kara is purposely misleading them…

 

Lena frowns.

 

Don’t, Kara mouths to her.

 

“I can find out,” Lena volunteers despite Kara’s glare. They all look at her and Lena repeats her offer. “I can go and find out what he is waiting for.”

 

“Just like that?”Captain Olsen asks, skeptical.

 

“Why? Would you rather I do it for a price?”

 

“I have never known a pirate to do anything for free.”

 

“Clearly, you haven’t met me.”

 

His demeanor shifts. His eyes soften and Lena looks away, not interested in the sudden curiosity so plainly written on his face.

 

“You have helped us a great deal, already.” Admiral Kal-El sits back down and asks a nearby officer for some paper and ink. “Everything Commodore Zor-El has promised you and your crew is to be granted to you as of today. You don’t have to prove anything to us anymore.”

 

“I know.” The side of her face burns from Kara’s glare but Lena remains steady. She can’t keep running away. Lex is waiting for her, and she has cheated fate long enough.

 

“And you’ll come back and report to us?” Captain Olsen asks, disbelievingly.

 

“Queen Rhea has been as much of a threat to my crew as she has been for you. We could do something about it or stay stuck here, waiting for my brother to make a move.”

 

“Captain Luthor has a point.” Maggie gives her a nod of support and looks around the table. “The longer we stay here, the more time we give Queen Rhea to wreak havoc somewhere else.”

 

A hum of agreement seems to run around the table.

 

A chair scrapes against the floor as Kara stands up.

 

“I’ll go with her.”

 

The room freezes.

 

“Are you out of your mind?” Alex exclaims, getting up of her seat as well. “Lex Luthor tried to kill you.”

 

“She can’t go on her own!” Kara fumes, gritting her teeth. Lena stands up as well, seeing the tension rise once again.

 

“As much as I’d appreciate the company Commodore Zor-El, it would be better if I go alone.” Lena finishes her drink and raises her glass. “Thank you for the wine. Now, if I could have my weapons back?”

 

Her weapons are given back to her. Two other officers carry the chest containing her crew’s weapons out of the room, likely to give them back to her crew.

 

Kara tries to intervene again but Lena is quicker.

 

“You should get ready to get the hell out of here in case it goes sideways.”

 

Lena leaves with a nod and the pardon securely inside her jacket. She closes the door behind her with a soft exhale.

 

*

 

The door slams open.

 

Lena startles as Kara storms out of the room, alone, and makes a beeline for her. Lena stands her ground.

 

“What the hell are you doing?” Kara hisses, advancing on her, her eyes wild and frustrated.

 

“No, what the hell are you doing?” Lena pushes against Kara’s chest to stop her from colliding into her. “I told you why Lex is out there. I don’t know what it means. I don’t know what he wants from me, but it certainly isn’t whatever threats you let them believe.”

 

Kara clenches her jaw, and Lena didn’t want to see it. After their last conversation, she had thought Kara had given up on her, but apparently she had been wrong. Kara still cares…

 

“You don’t want me to go,” Lena trails off, her eyes roaming over Kara’s face and committing it to memory.

 

“If I had told them they would have used you as leverage,” Kara says tiredly. She pinches the bridge of her nose and sighs. “But I was wrong because you did it all by yourself.”

 

Kara is still protecting her… The realization makes Lena smile, but it fades just as quickly.

 

Not if I come to resent her for it.

 

“You are being selfish.” Lena tries not to let the hurt reflecting on Kara’s face affects her. “You are still choosing me. The right thing to do would have been to inform the Admiral I could get the information you need without the risk of conflict.”

 

“And what of your safety?”

 

The earnestness in Kara’s eyes is so poignant that Lena gives in. She cups Kara’s cheek, feeling the warmth of her skin against her palm and the softness of her lip grazing her thumb.

 

“I’ll be fine.” Lena smiles at the ghost of a kiss pressed on her fingertips. “You don’t have to worry about me.”

 

Lena walks away while she still can.

 

I’m doing the right thing, Lena repeats to herself over and over again. Kara deserves to be happy. She deserves to choose someone without loathing herself for making that choice.

 

Tears gather at the corners of her eyes.

 

Her crew waits for her at the railing, ready to leave. Kara’s stare burns the back of her neck, but Lena leaves the El Mayarah, without looking back.

 

***

 

Her crew rows toward the Alexander. Light appears on the deck one by one, now that the sun has set.

 

“Are you sure you don’t want me to come with you?” Luke asks for the hundredth time, making Lena sigh and roll her eyes.

 

“Lex has issued a death warrant against you, so yes, I’m sure.”

 

Luke mutters something under his beard, but Lena is too focused on the familiar sight of the Alexander to give it any thought.

 

The boat knocks against the hull of her brother’s ship and Luke gives her a boost to reach the ladder.

 

For the first time, in more than ten years, Lena sets foot on her family’s ship. Taking in everything in, Lena feels like she is coming home.

 

Mercy is the one to greet her at her arrival.

 

The swell of emotion is overwhelming and Lena can only nod her thanks as Mercy escorts her to the Captain’s cabin.

 

Lena opens the door.

 

Of all the things the universe could have thrown at her, Lena doesn’t expect to find Lex eating with-

 

“Ah! Lena!” Lex rises from the table, wiping the corner of his mouth with a napkin. He stares at the little girl seated next to him who is looking down her bowl with a displeased frown, and sighs. “Being angry with me doesn’t exempt you from greeting your aunt, Lionheart…”

 

The little girl sighs and glares at Lena. She… roars. A tiny, lion-like roar. And she goes back to staring daggers at her bowl of vegetables.

 

Lex shakes his head and gestures toward the empty seat and plate seemingly set for her.

 

“Would you like to join us?”

 

Notes for the Chapter:

Thank you for reading. Stay safe!

15. Part III : 3

Notes for the Chapter:

It's a bird! It's a plane! It's... a new chapter! Kudos to Seriouslyficent for the support and keeping me sane and on track. Thank you to Rebellion_Bear for helping me with grammar and commas and making this chapter way easier to read. And thank you all for the continuous support and love. Enjoy the new chapter.

 

It feels like she’s reached the end of the story.

 

The brine sticking to her skin feels unpleasant when it hasn’t bothered her in years. It makes her want to scrub her body raw and tear off the last decade of her skin. Would it absolve her of her faults? Would she be forgiven for the lives she’s taken and those she couldn’t save? A lot has happened since she’s left the Luthorian Islands, well more like avoided it. She’s been running away from the prophecy for so long; from Lillian, the guilt, and she still finds herself exactly where she never wanted to be. Standing here before Lex, with her brittle hair, dry skin, and broken nails, Lena feels like she is worlds away from the girl she used to be.

 

And Lex isn’t the brother she remembers him to be.

 

They are perfect strangers. Linked by blood and fate.

 

Strangers sizing each other up.

 

Lex invites her to join them like there hasn’t been a decade worth of questioning, absence, and avoidance filling up the room. As if there was nothing wrong. As if the world wasn’t splitting at the seams and unraveling before their very eyes.

 

Breathing deeply, Lena tries to remain calm and collected while watching the kitchen staff move around the room. Her stomach growls, made hungry by the scent of hearty meat and steamed vegetables. It is a rarity to be served this kind of food onboard any ship, let alone so far away from home. A privilege Lena would have indulged in any other circumstances if it wasn’t for the little girl who looks so much like Lex staring at her from the other side of the table. Lena doesn’t know if it’s the shape of her eyes or the way her mouth curls in distaste when looking at the food that makes her look so much like him, but the resemblance is uncanny. The little girl stares at her, puzzled, but full of curiosity and excitement, and Lena is rooted to the floor, dead weight strapped to her ankles.

 

With the blood of your blood on your hands, you will be the last sunrise he’ll see, is what the Silver Banshee had said, and looking at Lex’s daughter, at her head tilted to the side as she stares and completely ignores her father when he tells her to eat, makes Lena want to turn back and run.

 

To the end of the world if she can. To hell, if she must.

 

“What is this?” She asks, harsh and demanding, unable to look at her niece any longer because it feels like her heart is breaking when she didn’t think it could break any further. She was wrong. Pain has no limits. It’s an abyss where the darkness is never dark enough and the fall never-ending. She clenches her jaw at the look Lex gives her, too perceptive and discerning, until his gaze flicks to the open door and he makes one of his kitchen staff close it with a snap of his fingers.

 

“Dinner, of course. Please…” he gestures toward the empty seat once more.

 

Lena doesn’t move. She can’t. The world is unraveling, and everything she’s done for the past ten years has been for naught. Running away from the prophecy still led her to her brother. It is incomprehensible. It is ironic and so unfair…

 

The walls are closing in, and her seat remains unoccupied.

 

Lex, raising an eyebrow at her, finally sighs and chuckles. “See, Lionheart? I told you your aunt was stubborn. Just like you.”

 

It makes the wild little thing shrug, still picking at her plate. She glances up, eyes timid and wondering until a strand of hair obscures her vision and she blows it out of her face. The offending strand of dark blonde hair comes back, and her niece groans in annoyance, pushing it out of her face and messing up her ponytail.

 

“If Aunt Lena doesn’t eat, can I not eat too?”

 

“No.”

 

“Why?”

 

“Because,” is all Lex says. He cuts through his meat, puts it in his mouth, and chews on it, infuriating the girl – her niece – who grumbles and throws down her cutlery in protest.

 

Lena stares, bewildered as her niece screams at the top of her lungs.

 

“You’re mean!”

 

“Eat.”

 

“No!”

 

“Five more bites.”

 

“No!”

 

“Lena!”

 

The name thunders in the room, making the back of her head tingle and the hair on her arms rise. Lena stares at them, confused, but Lex isn’t looking at her. No, he’s looking at his daughter, and his reprimand makes her niece flop back on her chair with a pout like the fight as left her body. Lex turns his attention back to her, and Lena gasps as the realization dawns on her.

 

It can’t be. It can’t be!

 

Why would Lex even—

 

But the look in his eyes confirms her suspicion, and the air gets thinner with every breath she takes. He has named his daughter after her and… a lump forms inside her throat at the warmth cracking through his confident persona. But the prophecy. God, the prophecy!

 

Lena cannot look at her niece and not think of the prophecy. She backs away and grabs the handle of the door. It is what she’s always done. Running away. From her family. From the prophecy. From herself. She lied and killed and— she survived. She did it all to survive, but now?

 

She shouldn’t have come on board the Alexander. She should have stayed hidden. She should have listened to Kara’s warnings and not spied on Rhea’s whereabouts. She shouldn’t have gotten involved with the Federation. They can burn for all she cares. Fate can suck it.

 

Pushing the door open is easy. It’s a matter of survival, and Lena has survived worse than this mockery of a family!

 

The cold night air pushes inside the room, making the lights around them flicker and the little girl cry out.

 

“Are you leaving already?”

 

Looking over her shoulder, Lena sees the frown marring her niece’s features. It pierces her heart in ways she didn’t think it could, but the pain is an abyss and she has spent so much time staring into its depth that it doesn’t take her long to meet her niece’s gaze straight-on, ready to offer some made-up justification to take her leave. But the little thing looks away with disappointment written all over her face. Her tiny body curls against her chair and Lena doesn’t recognize the wild child who greeted her with a roar just moments ago.

 

Lena glances at her brother, helpless and apologetic, but Lex is unreadable. He rises from his seat and kneels next to his daughter. It is strange to see him act so… fatherly. To see him speak so softly to his daughter and coax her out of the sadness she provoked with a smile and a little boop on the nose. Her heart aches at the sight, bittersweet and homesick, as Lex gathers his daughter up in his arms. The little thing nearly disappears in his embrace, hiding her face in the crook of his shoulder.

 

“It has been a long day,” he tells her with a reassuring smile that she doesn’t deserve but acknowledges with a smile of her own. He walks over to her and shows her the mop of dark blonde hair pressed into his shoulder. “Say goodnight to your aunt.”

 

All they hear is a muffled mumble.

 

“Lionheart…” Lex says in warning, rolling his eyes. Her niece finally glances up, and Lena has to smile at the sheepish face staring up at her.

 

“Night, night.”

 

“Goodnight, darling.”

 

Lionheart hides back into her father’s shoulder, hiding the red coloring her cheeks and the pleased smile Lena sees blooming on her face. She doesn’t deserve this, but seeing her smile makes her feel stupidly good about herself.

 

Lex carries his daughter deeper inside the cabin while ordering the kitchen staff to clear the table. He opens the door to the private sleeping quarters and disappears inside the room. She can hear him talk, giving instructions to the governess, and bid his daughter goodnight. But his daughter is a Luthor and it seems like she has him wrapped around her finger for he stays a little longer to tell her a story…

 

Lena waits by the door, motionless, as the room bustles with activity. Lex’s story makes his daughter shriek with laughter, it makes her gasp out loud, and Lena finds herself creeping a little closer to the open door to hear better. To soak it all in. Lex is a father and he loves his daughter just like she loves him. Lena can hear the contentedness in the tiny voice that gets quieter and quieter as the minutes pass. Lena can feel it in the extra seconds it takes him to leave his daughter’s side after she’s fallen asleep.

 

The door of the Captain’s cabin closes, and Lena looks back, only to realize that all the kitchen staff are gone.

 

Lex steps out of the room and closes the door of the private chamber quietly.

 

Frozen by a sudden beat of silence, Lena stares at her brother and feels like she is truly seeing him for the first time. Not the boy from her memories or the man she defied on a burning ship, but someone else. A man capable of both violence and love. It’s such an uncomfortable dichotomy that the silence weighs in just to accentuate how little she knows about her brother.

 

Lena expels a breath, unable to stand the silence anymore. “I didn’t mean to upset her.”

 

Her apology is met with detached acknowledgment. Her brother walks to his desk and pulls out a bottle filled with amber liquid from a drawer.

 

“It’s alright,” he says, uncorking the bottle. The sound of sloshing liquid hitting glass fills the room. Lena doesn’t know what to expect or why she stayed, but she did and she has to face the consequences of that choice. Whatever they may be. Even if it means that she has to force herself to hold her brother’s stare dead-on. “Family can be… disappointing.” Lena tries not to flinch at the jab as Lex downs his drink. He smacks his lips together in appreciation, and Lena narrows her eyes as he shrugs good-naturedly. “It is a good lesson to learn. I do have some responsibility in that, though. She had unfairly high expectations of you.”

 

She doesn’t know what he’s playing at but she doesn’t like the glint of satisfaction in his eyes. It’s as if— As if he wanted to hurt her feelings, by implying he’s talked about her to his daughter and by pointing out her absence from their life… If she’s honest with herself, it does hurt.

 

“What do you want?”

 

He lets out a chuckle and leans back against his desk.

 

His body thuds against the wood. Louder than either of them expected.

 

It’s weirdly clumsy and she watches him cover-up that clumsiness with a careless twirl of his glass on the surface of the desk. The feigned nonchalance is obvious. It makes her scan his body for any signs of injury he might have sustained during their fight, but her scrutiny is met with a glare.

 

“It’s time for you to come home, don’t you think?” He asks, looking serious.

 

Lena scoffs, taken aback by his words. She waits for him to break in laughter and tell her it’s a joke, but he doesn’t. He watches her impassively and Lena crosses her arms over her chest, feeling a chill creep up her spine.

 

With the blood of your blood on your hands, you will be the last sunrise he’ll see…

 

The light flickers around them, painting elongated shadows on the walls.

 

“And if I don’t?”

 

Lex sighs and wipes the side of his mouth after taking a swig from the bottle. He hisses and Lena hears him breathe deeply through his nose from across the room. The muscle in his cheek trembles as he clenches his jaw.

 

“I’ll finish what I started,” Lex says simply, threatening her as one would talk about the weather, but the veins on his temples betray his anger for Lena sees them nearly bulge out. “The Federation has been getting sloppy lately. I don’t mind teaching them a lesson.”

 

“And put your daughter in danger? Do you hate Kal-El that much?”

 

He rounds up his desk and takes a seat. “I’m not sure why you are on their side but I’ll have no qualms opening fire on them to make you come with us.”

 

Looking at him now, Lex seems tired. The light accentuates the shadows under his eyes and—

 

“I thought you’d be happy to see us,” he says. “Lionheart certainly was…” His eyes flick to the closed door of the sleeping quarters and Lena follows his gaze without meaning to. “You will come home, Lena. I’ll be… generous and let you say your goodbyes. I expect to see you tomorrow at first light,” he says, waving her away; leaving no room for arguments.

 

The light vacillates and the elongated shadow on the wall reminds her of a monster from years ago. She doesn’t want to believe in fate but this situation and this ultimatum make it harder not to.

 

“If I follow you, you’ll let the Federation go,” she asks, opening the door and looking at her brother from over her shoulder. “Without harm?”

 

“For once, I’m not here for Kal-El. I paid your debt to the Queen of Daxam. She told me you broke her trust…”

 

The kryptonite powder, Lena thinks in horror. Lex gave Rhea the means to destroy the Federation just to protect her? It doesn’t make any sense!

 

“I hope you won’t break mine,” he continues with a mocking smile that makes Lena step out of the room, unable to stand it any longer. “It was fated after all.” Lena hears him say just as she closes the door behind her.

 

***

 

It was fated after all…

 

Does Lex know about the prophecy?

 

Lena blinks as a droplet of water hits her cheeks, followed by another and another. She looks at the dark, cloudy sky and closes her eyes as rain falls on her. It is light, just a drizzle, but Lena smiles.

 

“Are you okay?” Luke asks, grunting his question as he rows them back toward the Intrepid.

 

Lena hums in reply and keeps her eyes closed and her head tilted up. If she ignores the sound of oars hitting the water, the voices whispering around her, and the wood beneath her, she might be able to fool herself into thinking she’s flying. But she’s scared of height and the image she pictures in her mind is a strange one. Wouldn’t it be nice to fly, high and free above the clouds, held by gentle, unshakable arms?

 

It was fated after all…

 

If Lex knows about the prophecy, does it mean he’ll try to… break it?

 

If yes. How?

 

Is he luring her away from her crew to kill her?

 

The number of people cursed by Silver Banshees was so small; Lena has only heard of two in the last century and read about five in the three centuries before that. There are a few bards who sing of those tales but the stories seem so exaggerated that it is hard to know if they are true. Lena can hardly blame them since tales of Silver Banshees and those they curse make for easy coin. But in all of those iterations, the only element of the story that remains unchanged is how feared those unlucky few become. There are tales of whole villages turning against those who have been cursed by a Silver Banshee. Rumors of public execution. Songs of men and women being shamed and killed for something they had no control over; in fear of a prophecy, no one wanted to wait for it to realize itself.

 

Keeping the scar hidden had been an imperative. Luke had reminded her of it constantly. She already had a target on her back for being a Luthor; she couldn’t let anyone know she was cursed as well. People learning about her being cursed by a Silver Banshee would have made their life drastically more difficult. She might not even have been part of the Singing Nightmare if the crew had known about it… It is a scary thought Lena tries not to think too much about, and Lena is glad that Lillian hadn’t denounced her. She’s glad that she sent Luke to do the dirty job instead, for she’s only alive because of him. Because of that one miscalculation on Lillian’s part.

 

The rain keeps falling. Lena exhales a breath, feeling the water seep into her mouth and gather at the corner of her eyes. It is concerning that Rhea knew enough about her to know to use a supposed meeting with the Messenger of a Silver Banshee to get to her, and Lex might also be in the know… Was it Rhea or Lillian who told him? Head tilted up, Lena keeps smiling as her body turns to lead and dread fills her heart. The more she smiles, the less scared she feels.

 

“It went better than I thought it would,” Lena finally says, meeting his eyes as she lies. “I feel… relieved.”

 

He squints a bit, trying to see through the rain— or her— in the darkness it could mean anything, and Lena is too weary to try to guess.

 

“Can I ask what he wanted from you?”

 

“Information about the Federation, what else? He asked me to arrange another meet-up with Admiral Kal-El. He felt like he was robbed of a fight and he wants to beat him in a one-on-one combat,” Lena lies again. The words leave a bad aftertaste in her mouth as she plays on people’s perception of her brother. “His obsession with the Admiral is as severe and petty as we thought.”

 

Her heart twinges as it works. Luke’s mouth curls into a smug smile.

 

“Their rivalry is a little intense,” he agrees, making Lena look away, unable to look at him. “That is what happens with two brilliant minds. Their antics were tales of legends during my training.”

 

The rain keeps falling. Looking past his shoulder, Lena sees the Intrepid. You can’t save everyone, he’d said and she had listened to him. She had watched members of her crew die during the attack of the frigates. She has led them into a violent battle against Dominators, and though they might have won, it had been hard-fought and a close one. And now, her crew stands between two beasts waiting to pounce and the choice she has to make is easy. Easier than she expected it to be. She might not be able to save everyone, but she can try.

 

She has to try.

 

“Nearly there,” he says, watching her wrap her arms around herself. He orders for the other to go faster. “We’ll be warm in no time.”

 

Lena nods, dropping her chin against her chest and bites the inside of her cheek. She isn’t worthy or deserving of his trust. All he’s done was helping her and she’s lying to him. She’s going to lie to everybody and—

 

The Silver Banshee might not be the monster, after all. She is.

 

The pardon Admiral Kal-El gave her weighs heavily inside her jacket.

 

The least she can do is set them free.

 

*

 

The Intrepid shines like a beacon through the rain. It’s not the ship itself or its numerous lights that Lena finds warmth in. It’s in the silhouettes she can see scattered on the main deck. It’s hearing Jess announce their arrival and see all those silhouettes rush to the railing. Lena rolls her eyes at their shouts and whistles, shaking her head good-naturedly at their welcome. It’s not like she’s been gone for long.

 

But it feels nice.

 

Hector waves at her from the crow’s nest. Lena waves back and sees Eli lean over as the boat bumps against the hull.

 

“Captain, before you say anything…” he says, his voice filled with apprehension. She eyes him curiously as she goes up the ladder. He points at the people gathered at the mainmast. “They came here not long after you left and…” He lets out a long exhale. Lena chuckles at seeing him so worked up. “First of all, I’m so glad you’re back because there’s so much I need to tell you. I told them you had just left so they could go back to their ship but they said they wanted to wait here. So we waited, and I swear something weird is going on with Jess and that Lieutenant,” Eli pauses and juts his chin toward the quarterdeck where Jess is, glaring at the unexpected visitors. “Hector has been acting all sulky because Jess has been staring at the Lieutenant like crazy and I’m not sure if it’s a I want to kill her kind of look or if she wants to bone her. Really, it’s fifty-fifty.”

 

Lena glances at the unexpected guests, standing back behind her crew with Sam who seems to be keeping them company, and sure enough despite all the commotion her arrival has caused, Lieutenant Vasquez doesn’t shy away from Jess’ attention. It makes Lena smile at the new development. Who would have thought? They were ready to blow each other’s heads off not two nights ago.

 

“What is this?” Luke asks as he comes up. “Why are they here?”

 

Eli raises his hands defensively.

 

“I’ve been manning the fort like the Captain asked, but the crew was terrified. ‘Cause fuck, that’s— that’s the Girl of Steel,” Eli says in rushed whispers, pointing his thumb back over his shoulder. Lena snorts a laugh as Luke sighs heavily; her gaze strays past Eli’s effusive retelling and the shoulders of many of her crew nodding along, and lands on blue eyes, turned dark by the low light and the distance, staring back at her. The rain has stopped. The air is warm and muggy, and holding Kara’s gaze feels like playing with fire. It feels like staring at a flame and being hypnotized by the way it moves and breathes. Lena feels consumed with every breath. With every heartbeat…

 

“But I could have taken her on,” Eli finishes; making Lena snaps her attention back toward him as her crew erupts in laughter.

 

“She beat you to a pulp, mate.” One of the crewmembers says, socking the poor boy in the shoulder.

 

“You didn’t last five minutes!” Hector piles on, making his way toward them. Eli shoves Hector, and Hector stumbles back into the crowd, laughing harder.

 

“You fight her and we’ll see how long you last!”

 

“Enough!” It stops any further escalation and Lena throws a pointed look to her crew. “You lot wouldn’t last a minute.”

 

They protest raucously, making bets among themselves as if the subject of their conversation wasn’t standing behind them, listening.

 

“Because you did so much better,” Luke whispers in her ear; a teasing smile audible in the tone of his voice.

 

Lena rolls her eyes. “You don’t fight a beast like her…” she says, looking at Kara and feeling her breath get trapped inside her ribcage, caught in Kara’s gaze like a moth to a flame.

 

“A beast, huh?”

 

Hearing the obvious mirth in his voice, Lena doesn’t make any more comments. She already has a hard time concentrating on the situation as it is, because Kara’s focus is relentless. Her eyes burn a trail all over Lena’s body, and Lena has to look away lest she forgets herself. She catches Sam’s attention and gives her a sharp nod to her questioning look.

 

Sam finally lets the Commodore get closer.

 

It’s hard not to feel intimidated when Kara marches toward them in full regalia, oozing self-confidence. Every step feels like thunder. It’s bold and fearless, and the perfect stillness of her gaze feels like bolts of lightning piercing through her chest. Followed by Captain Danvers and Lieutenant Vasquez, Kara moves like she is the one in charge. It should irk her, but it doesn’t. With her blood pumping inside her ears and the hushed silence falling over her crew as they part ways at the Commodore’s arrival, Lena stands her ground.

 

At what cost? It has only been a few hours since their last talk— their last argument, and it’s been chaos ever since, absolute fucking chaos in her heart.

 

“A beast, indeed…” Luke whispers, taking a step back and leaving her on her own to deal with the juggernaut that is Commodore Kara Zor-El.

 

“Captain Luthor,” Kara says, standing before her, so absolutely still, it’s uncanny.

 

“Commodore Zor-El,” Lena responds, “I thought we agreed on reconvening on the El Mayarah for my report…”

 

Kara opens her mouth but no sounds come out. Lena is immediately taken by the tentative softness breaking through the Commodore’s mask.

 

“Someone was impatient,” Captain Danvers pipes up in a grumble from behind the Commodore’s back, shattering the perfect stillness in the air and Lena’s focus on Kara. Sam giggles and Luke hums contemplatively from behind her. “You haven’t been taking good care of my ship,” Captains Danvers remarks, pointing at the broken bits and pieces in the flooring.

 

A low murmur travels among her crew.

 

“Just say thank you,” Eli exclaims with a shit-eating grin. “Those holes are from saving your asses back in New Kandor.”

 

Captain Danvers rolls her eyes as the crew bolsters Eli’s confidence with whoops and guffaws.

 

“Alright, alright,” Captain Danvers says, conciliatory and raising her voice above the noise. She levels them all with a glare that would have been effective if it weren’t for the smile curving her lips. “You all know it was just a loan, right?”

 

“It was a gift,” Lena retorts without missing a beat. “Wasn’t it, Commodore?”

 

Kara’s eyes widen imperceptibly at having the limelight switch to her. Tall and impassive, the blush creeping up her neck would have gone unnoticed if it wasn’t for the fact that Lena is hungry for those little shreds of emotion breaking through the Commodore’s mask. Little crumbs Lena finds herself collecting and safeguarding selfishly, for the mere sight of them feels like a victory and a defeat all at once.

 

“It was a gift,” Kara confirms after a moment of silence. The crew cheers. Captain Danvers groans and shakes her head as Lena’s crew taunts her playfully.

 

It makes her sigh contentedly at seeing her crew be so at ease with a blue-coat. Captain Danvers might as well be the picture-perfect Federation officer, direct and so lawfully good. It’s nothing short of a miracle to see her blend in with Lena’s motley crew. It is undeniable that what happened in Lian Yu has acted as some sort of catalyst. Her crew isn’t as wary of the Federation as they used to be, they learned to look beyond the uniform. A lesson Captain Danvers reciprocates easily, and the lightheartedness is so contagious that Lena notices a hint of a smile tug at the corner of Lieutenant Vasquez’s lips.

 

“And even if it wasn’t a gift, they did drink you under the table,” Sam points out, nudging Captain Danvers’ shoulder. “I’d say they’ve won your ship fair and square.”

 

“Come on!” Luke steps in. “Let the Captain breathe!”

 

The crew breaks away. Laughing, some resume their duties while others enjoy the night air. Lena sees them linger around the main deck, trying to appear nonchalant while keeping an eye on their visitors. Lena smiles at their cautiousness and their obvious care. Hector tries to make Jess join in the fun, but after a few minutes his endeavor remains unsuccessful. The boatswain seems quite fine staying where she is, observing all of them.

 

“In some cultures, it could be seen as a wedding gift,” Luke says out of the blue. Lena jabs her elbow between his ribs as he laughs. “What?” he defends himself, rubbing his stomach, but Lena sees the teasing glint in his eyes and rolls her eyes, hoping no one heard his silly comment.

 

She looks around and gulps at seeing Kara staring at them, mouth agape, and at a loss for words.

 

“It’s true,” Sam pipes in, detaching herself from Kara’s sister, and looking at her with nothing but mischief in her eyes, “It’s a thing, especially south of the Federation. I heard some Kryptonians fishermen used to do that as well…”

 

“Wait! Hang on,” Eli says, and Lena startles at noticing him standing next to her. Hadn’t he left with the crew? “You and—” His eyes widen and Lena puts a hand over his mouth before he can attract any more attention to them.

 

“Booze’s on me!” Lena shouts, making her crew look at her curiously. The silence lasts for a beat, blanketing the overcrowded deck until her crew cheers. Lena encourages them to open the barrels of rum inside the kitchen and share the food. It’s been a while since she let them enjoy the night and there is no better time to celebrate than when they are actively stopping another war just by standing between two behemoths.

 

She makes her rounds. Checking-in with Luke, they establish a rotation for the night watch.

 

Lena makes sure to get the last round.

 

“Wait!” Captain Danvers exclaims as Hector and Eli drag her with them where music can already be heard playing in the lower deck, “Lex Luthor is right there!”

 

Her protests die down as Hector calls chicken on the Captain. But before she can be dragged down the flight of stairs, she checks in with Kara, and the Commodore glances at Lena before giving her sister the go-ahead.

 

“I’ll make sure the Reign keeps an eye out as well,” Sam says before whistling loudly. She looks at the dark sky and smiles at the sound of fluttering wings. Her parrot lands on her shoulder and Sam walks away, whispering to her bird.

 

Luke squeezes her shoulder before he makes his way toward the lost-looking Lieutenant and shakes her hand. Lena sees them follow the sound of laughter down the stairs. She finally sighs, glancing at the Commodore only to notice her looking past her. Lena glances over her shoulder and sees Eli, at the top of the stairs, staring at them. He wiggles his eyebrow and runs down the stairs.

 

With most of her crew below, the deck falls silent. The sails swoosh with the breeze and the ship rocks imperceptibly with every wave.

 

The Commodore’s composure breaks with an unrestrained smile.

 

“It’s—”

 

“I know.” Lena pinches the bridge of her nose with a laugh. “They’re a handful.”

 

Some of her crew spills back out from the lower deck, looking for fresh air, and Lena grabs Kara’s sleeve.

 

“Come on.”

 

Guiding her toward the back of the ship, Lena eyes the door of her private quarters but hesitates. Looking up, she sees a lone silhouette still standing on the quarterdeck.

 

“Jess!” Lena calls, taking Kara to the quarterdeck. “Take care of our guests and make sure everyone behaves. Please?”

 

“You want me to go down there?”

 

“If it gives you enough incentive to talk to Lieutenant Vasquez, then yes.”

 

Jess scoffs from her perch and jumps off the railing, landing just before them. “I’ll do it but only because I’m a good friend and you seem to need the quarterdeck to yourself,” she says. Her eyes shift toward the Commodore and Lena sighs as Jess winks at her before leaving.

 

They remain silent as they walk up the stairs. With every step taking her away from her crew, Lena feels the chains of fate weighing down on her. Pressing her forearms into the railing, she watches the light dancing below as her crew drinks and jokes around, unaware of Lex’s ultimatum.

 

The clock is ticking.

 

Luke is filling everyone’s cup, making sure everyone has their fair share of food. He sends a deck-hand with two cups brimming with watered-down rum toward them and Lena raises her cup in thanks. Eli is playing the guitar. Lena raises an eyebrow at seeing Sam invite Captain Danvers to dance and she feels Kara’s surprise at seeing her sister accept. They aren’t the only ones dancing. Lena wolf whistles at Hector seeing him dance with their newly appointed powder monkey, a quiet woman with long red hair and freckles on her nose that Lena had to recruit after losing theirs in the aftermath of the frigates’ attack against the Singing Nightmare. He flips the bird at her, making her laugh. Lena startles as Kara nudges her shoulder, only to laugh even more as Kara points at Jess edging closer to the Lieutenant.

 

“That woman— Jess. She’s very...” Kara pauses, licking her lips while trying to find the right word. “Intense,” she finally says, making Lena chuckle at how true the assertion is. “She’s been glaring at us ever since we arrived. I fear she might eat Alex’s lieutenant alive.”

 

Lena snorts at Kara’s cluelessness, making the Commodore look at her, confused.

 

“Oh, I definitely think that is Jess’s intention, but I’m sure the little death will be worth it.”

 

Kara’s brows furrow for a moment, and Lena waits gleefully for Kara to put the pieces together. Lena laughs when she does, watching the Commodore snap her attention back toward the unlikely pair with an expression of surprise.

 

If Lena hadn’t been in love already, she would have fallen right in.

 

“I’m so dumb—”

 

“No, you’re not.”

 

“—When did this happen?”

 

Lena recounts what happened onboard the El Mayarah, the Lieutenant’s apology, and all the incidents leading up to it. In hindsight, something must have piqued Jess’ interest, even at the paroxysm of their conflict, to make her act on the Lieutenant’s apology in such a profound way.

 

“And these two?” Kara points toward her sister and the Captain of the Reign.

 

“I don’t really know how or when that happened to be honest.”

 

Kara hums, contemplative as she takes a sip of her cup. “She was with Maggie until recently. Alex always talked about settling down one day. To live on a farm maybe. To open a shop somewhere nice… Have a kid… I think it made them grew apart.”

 

In this context, her conversation with Maggie takes on a new light and Lena hopes she hadn’t been insensitive with her advice. Ever since she escaped her father’s clutch and the expectations he had of her, Maggie had been hungry for any scrap of freedom and independence she could get. They had been kindred souls in that way. Both trying to run away from the place they used to call home, and Lena can understand how Maggie could’ve seen the idea of settling down as some sort of sacrifice.

 

“Alex has already done so much for the Federation,” Kara continues. “She could retire now if she wanted to. I don’t think the High Council would be opposed to it as long as she’d still be available for major crises…”

 

“What’s holding her back?”

 

Kara sighs, and Lena watches her bite the bottom of her lip as she stares at the bottom of her cup.

 

“I’m the one holding her back,” Kara whispers, voice rough and teary-eyed. “She’s staying because of me.”

 

Down below, Eli sings for the crowd. They’ve changed partners and Kara’s sister is now dancing with Hector. Hands are clapping. Boots are pounding the deck. Captain Danvers twirls and finds herself back in Sam’s arms, laughing uproariously as Sam whispers in her ear.

 

“And you, what do you want?” Lena asks, watching Kara’s profile and seeing the light dance across her face and the breeze move strands of her hair.

 

“I don’t know…” she says with a sad, little smile. “It doesn’t matter. Being the Girl of Steel is more than enough.” She clears her throat and shifts away from the railing, forcing herself to stand on her own two feet. Jaw clenched and posture rigid, the Commodore looks at the crowd below like she is above that kind of lightheartedness.

 

It hurts her to see the amount of restraint Kara exacts on herself. At seeing the way her fingers clench around her cup and how she observes the world below with such practiced nonchalance it betrays her envy.

 

It makes Lena inch closer.

 

A gust of wind makes the canvas flap in the air. The ship sways, and her shoulder is pressed against the Commodore. The wood creaks beneath them. The scent of honey and mint drifts in the air and Lena closes her eyes as it pulls at threads from deep within her soul.

 

It’s comforting in its sheer familiarity. Like the fondness of a forgotten memory, and Kara presses back against her, making the warmth of their body becomes the bridge they agreed not to cross.

 

Not when Kara isn’t sure of what she wants and Lena is held back by a past she refuses to confront.

 

“You don’t have to worry about Lex. He’ll leave tomorrow,” Lena says, whispering the words so faintly it makes her sacrifice feel a little less tangible, a little less real to her ears.

 

“Just like that?” Kara turns away from the party below to look at her. Lena huffs at the skepticism she reads on her face, but Kara doesn’t pry. The Commodore waits, eyes kind and non-judgmental. It leaves the ugly urge to lash out with no target for her anger and desperation. Lena pushes against the railing and makes her way deeper inside the quarterdeck. She takes a seat on the stairs leading to the poop deck and stares at the dark void above.

 

Sailing around the world hadn’t been enough. Staring at the space between two stars, Lena wonders what more could she have done to free herself from the chains of fate…

 

The stairs creak as Kara joins her. Warmth radiates against her side as Kara settles down and gazes at the sky. There’s quiet, peace, and longing written so plainly across her face that it makes Lena wonder what Kara sees when she is looking beyond the edge of the world.

 

“Do you believe in fate?”

 

Kara shifts beside her, seemingly surprised by the question. Silence settles around them like a blanket on a cold night. The distant music and voices sound like the cracking of a fire, nebulous, peaceful, and melodic.

 

“I believe some things are inevitable,” Kara replies in a whisper. Like a secret. A confession. Some kind of hope. It makes Lena wait with bated breath as Kara sighs heavily and places her cup at her feet. “It gives me comfort to think that some things are just meant to be. No matter how lost we feel or the chaos surrounding us… I’d like to believe there is a path for all of us.”

 

Shoulder pressed against shoulder. Thigh against thigh. A sense of vertigo overwhelms her as it feels like those points of connection are the only thing keeping her from falling.

 

Her cup joins Kara’s.

 

“What if the fate you’ve been given is a horrible one?” she asks, not meeting Kara’s eyes. “What if it pushes you to do something you’d never forgive yourself for?”

 

“I’m no expert but I think…” Lena flinches as Kara brushes some of her hair behind her ear and their eyes meet. “Whatever you are bound to do has to have a meaning. I remember you mentioning a Silver Banshee…” Blue eyes flick up toward her scar, but the wounded flesh is hidden by her hat, and Lena leans away from Kara’s gentle touch. “I’m still confused about certain things but it’s the reason you came to National City, isn’t it?”

 

Kara is piecing the puzzle back together and Lena grits her teeth as it shines a light on every dark little secret she kept hidden for so long. It feels too raw, too vulnerable to let go of years and years of anger and confusion and pain, and Lena still doesn’t know how Kara can talk about her past, about the pain, and the heartbreak she went through without breaking into thousands of pieces.

 

“Am I wrong?” Kara asks. “Is that how you got your scar?”

 

The tone of her voice is unbearable. It’s too soft, too compassionate, too understanding. She’s fire incarnate, and Lena feels like her body has been set ablaze as a sob tumbles past her lips. She presses her forearm against her eyes, feeling breathless and lightheaded, as her heart constricts, burns, and turns to ashes. Her body shakes and her mind is a haze. It’s too much. The prophecy, her conversation with Lex, him having a daughter… It’s all too much, and Lena doesn’t know how to stop herself from crying, afraid her crew might hear, afraid it might never stop.

 

Arms wrap around her body. Lena takes a shuddering breath, enveloped in the scent of honey and mint gathered at the crook of Kara’s neck.

 

“I’ve got you,” Kara whispers, but all Lena hears is her blood pumping inside her veins, her ragged breathing, and Kara’s heartbeat, pulsing directly beneath her ear. Slow and steady. Pressing her face against Kara’s skin, Lena lets her pain explode within her embrace.

 

Kara traces slow circles on the small of her back and it makes the ticking clock feels less menacing for a breathless moment.

 

But Lena is still shackled to the chains of fate. They twist around her body like winding weeds and she doesn’t know if she’ll even live beyond tomorrow. She knows she cannot kill her brother. She simply cannot let the prophecy unfold and deprive her niece of her father.

 

And so the path is clear.

 

She needs to break the chains. She needs to pay the price.

 

If some things are inevitable then her sacrifice might be what fate has always asked of her. Not to kill her brother but to sacrifice herself to save him…

 

Will it be enough? It has to be…

 

It has to be.

 

Lena detangles herself from Kara’s embrace, but her fingers stay twisted in the fabric of the woman’s clothes for one more selfish moment, unable to let go. Unable to say goodbye.

 

“No one really comes out of the Phantom Zone,” Kara says, making Lena look into her eyes and startle at the pain living there. “It’s not a place you can leave behind… You asked me how I got out of it. A while ago,” Kara adds with a small smile, seeing her look of confusion. It suddenly dawns on her that Kara is talking about their time locked inside Roulette’s brig. It feels so long ago. Years instead of months and Lena can only admire Kara’s commitment to their unfinished argument. She takes off her hat in response, reciprocating Kara’s trust, and brushes her hair away from her scar. It pulses with the night air. Blue eyes flick toward the raised flesh and Lena nods at the silent question she sees in them.

 

Her whole being trembles as warm fingertips graze her forehead. Lena shudders, closing her eyes, as Kara traces her scar like she is committing it to memory. Memories of moist skin and tangled limbs flash inside her mind as Lena remembers Kara doing the same thing to every inch of her body. It’s reverent and all-consuming, and Lena embraces the absolute fucking chaos blazing inside her chest.

 

“The Phantom Zone is where your mind goes when it breaks. Krypton dealt that punishment to the worst criminals. They would be taken on Captain Jindah Kol Rozz’s ship and be made to jump the plank. The Captain would fish them out of the water before they could drown only to do it again and again. Until they became ghosts of themselves.”

 

Kara presses her forehead against hers and lets out a trembling breath that Lena feels against her lips.

 

“I was lost at sea. For so long… I kept fighting against the tide, against the waves crashing onto me relentlessly. Against a force that was so immense and endless that it just felt... ridiculous. I think it broke me,” Kara says, her voice unnaturally still despite the anguish swirling in the blue of her eyes. “I wasn’t the same when Kal-El found me. He went to an Elder who had survived Krypton’s destruction to try to cure me, but the only explanation the Elder had was that I was stuck in the Phantom Zone.”

 

Lena sucks in a breath, remembering all of Kara’s momentary absences. All those times when her eyes had become glazed with a blankness that broke her heart and made her want to hold on to her as tightly as she could. Kara had been stuck in the Phantom Zone then. She had retreated so far back inside her mind Lena had been unable to reach her for interminable minutes. It is terrifying to realize how close she’s been to losing her. How tenuous the thread is and how tightly Kara is holding on to it.

 

Lena presses her lips against Kara’s cheek, laying kisses on the corner of her lips just to feel her breathe as intimately as she can without crossing too much the lines they’ve drawn. It’s a poor excuse, Lena’s aware of it, but she can be selfish for the both of them.

 

“I saw you come out of it. More than once.”

 

“The more it happens,” Kara confesses, her breath trembling in quiet terror, “the longer it takes me to get out of it. I don’t want to be stuck in there ever again…”

 

Lena holds the trembling body against her.

 

Beyond, there is a lull in the music. The Commodore tenses as her name is called. The change of pace is so jarring. Lena has a hard time processing as time seems to accelerate suddenly. Kara gets up and Lena is unable to move.

 

“I have to go…” Kara says, and lingers. “What can I do?” she asks, glancing at Lex’s armada glowing in the distance.

 

Lena finally gets up, fighting against the daunting sense of vertigo of a world that moves on too fast for them to keep up. She smoothes the crease on Kara’s coat to ground herself in the moment. She sighs contentedly at the warmth radiating from the vibrant fabric. “Nothing I’m afraid. They’ll try to come after me,” Lena says, glancing at her crew. “Promise me you won’t let them come after me.” Kara nods and Lena breathes a bit easier. “Like you said. Some things are inevitable.” She takes out one of her daggers and presses the pommel inside Kara’s palm. “You cannot let Rhea win.”

 

Kara looks over her shoulder as her name is being called again from the main deck.

 

“I don’t want to leave,” the Commodore whispers, making Lena gasp as Kara bridges the gap they’ve been so careful to maintain.

 

They fall into the kiss like it’s all they’ve been waiting for. It’s brief, desperate, and hungry but it is enough.

 

It has to be.

 

Kara walks away, but Lena cannot let her go without her knowing…

 

“Being the Girl of Steel is not all you are,” Lena says, making the Commodore falter in her step. “Kara Zor-El is just as important.”

 

“I’m not sure who Kara Zor-El is anymore,” the Commodore says, not looking at her before her whole body moves forward.

 

“You’ll find her again,” Lena whispers to the wind as Kara makes her way back to the main deck. The clock is ticking. One day, Kara will find that one person who she can be selfish with without it being a sacrifice. Lena is sure of it and she can’t wait for that day to come. It’s bittersweet, but Lena cannot stop the smile spreading across her face as she longs for Kara’s happiness. Watching that incredible woman walk away from her, Lena feels her heart beat and bleed as the world goes on and their time together comes to an end. It had been brief, but Lena is happy to have been part of Kara’s journey.

 

The air becomes perfectly still as Kara lingers at the top of the stairs and stares right at her from over her shoulder.

 

“I’ll find you when I do,” she says, making Lena’s heart thunder inside her chest. “You better stay alive till then.”

 

***

 

It’s not the epic fight she had pictured. It’s not as simple as killing a monster. It’s definitely not the Manichean struggle of good versus evil all the legends had told her to expect and prepare for— it’s worse.

 

The fight comes from within. It’s an intimate struggle Lena is trying to come to terms with, and going back to the Alexander feels exactly like fighting in Roulette’s Arena and expecting to die.

 

She hadn’t been able to say goodbye. She had left the Admiral’s pardon on her desk for Luke to find but her crew had been asleep or too groggy from the celebration to pay her any mind when she climbed down the ladder and slipped away. She’d had to shoo Sam’s parrot away at one point as it had sunk its claw into her shoulder while she had been halfway to her brother’s ship. This is my choice, she told the bird, hoping it will carry her message and stop them from looking for her. It was highly unlikely, but the chaos her departure would bring would be enough for Lex’s armada to be long gone. Besides that little mishap, running away had been easy, and Lena rubs her shoulder as Lex welcomes her back.

 

Lena takes a deep breath, waiting for the sky to fall, and tenses when nothing happens.

 

Lex gives the order the move-out. All of his ships pull their anchor up and unfurl their sails.

 

Her brother leaves her there, standing on the deck, and ignores her for the whole day.

 

And the days after that.

 

Lionheart jumps at her the first time she sees her and Lena catches the child instinctively. Her niece stays glued to her side, talking her head off, and pointing at every little thing she finds of interest. Lena spends her days with her niece and barely sees Lex. Her brother keeps his distance, not only from her but from his daughter as well, which is strange and uncommon judging by the daily tantrums Lionheart throws at not being able to spend more time with her father. Lena tries her best to reassure the distressed child by spending time with her, reading her books, and by telling her stories of the Singing Nightmares’ adventures. Sometimes Lena would catch her brother watching them with a wistful expression on his face until he noticed her looking and he’d turn his back on them.

 

Days pass and nothing changes. Routine settles in. It lures her into a false sense of safety Lena tries hard to fight against every single day.

 

She wonders what her crew is doing— if Luke will ever forgive her, if Jess resolved the tension with Lieutenant Vasquez, or if Eli stepped up to become Captain. Now that she’s gone, Luke would have been forced to hold an election. By abandoning the ship, Lena had renounced to all her duties and privileges of Captaincy. They wouldn’t be able to function without a leader and Lena hopes Eli steps up to the occasion… He deserves it. She had given him more and more responsibilities ever since the loss of the Singing Nightmare for this exact reason.

 

A loud thump rouses Lena from her sleep.

 

Sounds of footsteps thunder on the deck upstairs. Lena tenses. She follows the sound of footsteps and panicked voices to the Captain’s cabin and stares at the people coming in and out of her brother’s private quarters.

 

“What’s going on?”

 

Mercy whips around, holding a crying Lionheart in her arms. “It would be best for you to go back to your room,” Mercy says, standing in her way while she tries to soothe the distressed child. The governess comes running up the stairs and Lena uses that momentary distraction to push past Mercy and slip through the door of the Captain’s cabin.

 

Lex is slumped on his desk, deathly pale despite the numerous physicians fussing over him.

 

“What is going on?” Lena demands, her voice trembles as Lex startles and glares at her.

 

“You caught me on one of my bad days…” He rises from his seat, his movements jerky and clumsy as he begins to shout and push the people around him away from him. “Out! Out!”

 

The physicians file out of the door, throwing her a look Lena is not sure how to interpret.

 

“That fucking prophecy,” Lex says in a rasp. The veins of his neck and face seem to bulge out with how pale he looks and his eyes are so bloodshot it looks like they are bleeding. Lena observes it all with mounting horror as he knocks back a drink and hisses as the alcohol travels through his body. Some color rushes back to his cheeks but it isn’t enough to hide the tremors in his hands or the sweat pearling on his forehead.

 

“Lex…”

 

Her brother lunges at her.

 

Lena sidesteps the attack.

 

Lex growls and lunges at her again, toppling chairs as he tries to get to her.

 

“You were right,” Lena growls and pushes him away, seeing for the first time the glimmer of a dagger hidden in his sleeve. “Family can be disappointing.”

 

With the blood of your blood on your hands, you will be the last sunrise he’ll see…

 

The prophecy slithers inside her mind and Lena forces herself to be still. Not to fight back. Lionheart is crying outside the door and Lena won’t play in the hand of the Silver Banshee. She’ll break the chains of fate. She’ll pay the price…

 

“Come on,” Lena forces out, eyeing the dagger and feeling her heart pound inside her chest. “Do it.”

 

She clenches her jaw when Lex laughs at her face.

 

“Me killing you wouldn’t change a thing,” he says, staggering away from her, completely winded, and slides against the wall. Bone-tired. “I’m already dying.”

 

The blade clatters on the wooden floor.

 

Lionheart has stopped crying and Lex’s ragged breathing fills the sudden silence. The sound of waves crashing against the hull barely masks the breathlessness and sickness Lena hears coming from his lungs. His eyes flutter open and the pretense is gone.

 

She kneels at his side and grabs his hand.

 

It trembles between her palms.

 

The chains of fate are like winding weeds she cannot escape from, and in a moment of horrifying clarity, Lena understands that Lex is dying regardless of what she might do to prevent it.

 

Lex is dying, and she’s reached the end of the story— or as close to an end as it can be.

 

 

Notes for the Chapter:

Thank you for reading. Stay safe and healthy! <3

16. Part III : 4

Notes for the Chapter:

It's nearly the end... Thank you all for the support! And thank you to Rebellion_Bear for making it easier to read!
As a note: This chapter will heavily reference the other stories in this series. Enjoy the new chapter!

 

Some things feel inevitable– fated to happen, like it has been written in the stars.

 

Lena stares at her brother and wonders if all her choices, good and bad, have led her to this. To stare at her brother and to feel his life slip through her fingers. To watch him die no matter how many times she tries to save him.

 

A laugh shatters the tense silence causing Lex to wrench his hand away from her.

 

“I didn’t think you’d find this funny.” He wipes the corner of his mouth, smearing blood all over the back of his hand, and glares at her. “To mock a dying man… I didn’t think you had it in you.”

 

Overwhelmed by another fit of giggles, Lena grabs his arm.

 

“I’m sorry…” she apologizes, but it’s hard not to laugh when Lena feels like her life has been one giant fucking joke. She knows she’s being insensitive and tactless, and it has cost her friendships and alliances in the past, but Lena can’t stop the laughter bubbling inside her chest, just like she can’t contain it even though Lex stares at her like she might’ve grown a second head. Lena feels like… She feels like if she were to try to contain that uncontrollable laughter, her ribs might crack. One by one. Just from the sheer pressure of this absolute travesty. “I’m sorry…” she repeats, taking a deep breath and letting it out slowly. Her eyes land on the swaying chandelier and the overturned chairs and the papers scattered around the room, and she bites her lip as tears gather at the corner of her eyes. It’s just not fair…

 

Lightheaded, she presses her hand against her forehead. Her scar pulses against her palm, and she feels defeated. Slumping against the wall, Lena surrenders to the will of the universe. She’s tired of fighting, of trying to understand a prophecy that has robbed her of ten years of her life. She’s tired and—

 

“All these years, I stayed away to protect you—” She looks to the ceiling, eyes stinging with tears, and it isn’t laughter bubbling inside her chest anymore. It’s pain; pure, unadulterated pain that her body doesn’t know how to deal with. “All the sacrifices I’ve made… All the things I never allowed myself to have…” She could have stayed home. She could have led a whole different life and the outcome wouldn’t have changed? What a waste. What a giant fucking waste… It is so monumental Lena can barely breathe. “It was all for nothing…”

 

There’s a surprising kindness in Lex’s eyes as he watches her have a complete meltdown, and Lena has to look away. She cannot bear to look at him when he’s the one dying and she’s the one who has wasted so much time running away. Her eyes land on the upturned chairs and broken glass, on the carpet, bunched up against the legs of the table, and it’s… oddly comforting. The ship dips down with a wave, making several bottles topple from the cabinet. Lex curses as some break while others roll on the floor.

 

The chaos is comforting.

 

“I was ready to…” she whispers, but her mouth snaps shut as she begins to see a pattern in the chaos. Not the chaos reigning inside the Captain’s cabin of the Alexander, but the chaos reigning within herself. It’s like holding a tight ball of yarn and watching it unravel as she pulls steadily on a single thread. It’s reliving her memories and understanding why she had been so quick to abandon her crew, why she’s been so resolute to push Kara away, and why it has been so easy for her to sacrifice herself, every step of the way.

 

She just…

 

She just didn’t love herself enough to believe anyone would want her to stay.

 

She didn’t love herself enough to believe she deserved to be saved.

 

When the Silver Banshee came, there had been no doubt in her mind that she was the one to blame. She never questioned Lillian’s interpretation of the prophecy. She didn’t even entertain the thought that it could be anything but her fault— her, the bastard child who became an orphan, and who had to be taken in by a father who was never around and a woman who showed nothing but disdain toward her. She had been so quick to believe the worst of herself; eager to be judged for sins she had yet to commit. She needed an explanation, some sort of justification, or a reasonable cause as to why she felt so out of place inside the Luthor’s Manor and why Lillian never gave her an ounce of affection despite her best efforts to be what Lillian wanted her to be. She needed punishment and shame and guilt because the only memory she has of her mother is a haunting and incomprehensible one. It lives inside her heart and stands beyond forgiveness. After all, who watches their mother die and don’t scream for help? Who watches their mother disappear beneath the waves but only feels the water tickling their feet?

 

A monster.

 

She has believed herself to be a monster for so long that she had been ready, begging even, to pay the price, whatever that price may be, in atonement.

 

It didn’t matter that Luke spent a decade protecting her, or that Jess opened up to her when she kept everyone at arm’s length. It didn’t matter that Eli looked up to her, or that her crew kept following her into battle without questions. It certainly didn’t matter that Kara kept saving her and kept fighting for her, putting her reputation on the line, her life, her heart, time and time again. All of it didn’t matter because Lena just couldn’t see why they would.

 

All of their efforts— their love— had been in vain because she had been blinded by the pattern of self-destruction she had so readily embraced.

 

A hand pats her head. One. Twice. The gesture is unexpected and awkward, and Lena can’t help but jerk away from it, staring incredulously at Lex as he gives her a half-hearted shrug.

 

“You were crying…” he points to her face and Lena finally notices the rivulet of tears running down her cheeks. “And quite hard I might add,” he says with a small smile. His attempt at comfort makes her snort. “I just didn’t want you to choke on your own spit, that’s all.”

 

“Of course,” Lena shakes her head, amused by his lame explanation. Lex’s smile grows wider, and amidst the mess and the chaos, it feels like… It feels like the day they met and Lex introduced himself, without an ounce of hesitation, as her new brother. It’s odd and endearing that, even after all this time, he would try to comfort her. It’s odd and Lena still isn’t quite sure what’s his motivations are. “Thank you for looking out for me,” she says, her voice dripping with sarcasm as she plays along.

 

“That’s what brothers do, is it not? Or is it too late?”

 

Their eyes meet. Lena gasps at the sincerity and at the shimmering vulnerability she sees in his eyes. It’s not what she expected coming here. She had been prepared for Lex to kill her to prevent the prophecy; she never would have thought that he would…

 

It’s too late to be my brother.

 

I guess, it is.

 

Lena shakes the voices out of her head and the images that come with them. A sprawling city. A body that is meant to fade away…

 

The silence lingers, and Lex clenches his jaw and looks away.

 

Nudging his shoulder, Lena listens to the thought, some kind of regret, echoing inside her mind. It feels like they’ve had that conversation before. Many times over, but circumstances never permitted her to say what she had always wished she could say.

 

Until now.

 

“No,” she expels in a breath that seems to belong to a multitude of voices that all resemble her own, “it’s not too late.”

 

“You’re crying again,” is all he says, and Lena rolls her eyes and uses her sleeve to wipe her tears.

 

“You’re a horrible brother.”

 

“Family can be disappointing,” Lex reminds her, completely deadpan, and Lena laughs, genuinely, as a smile dangles on the corner of his lips.

 

A bottle slides past them. Lex snatches it on its way back. He uncorks the bottle, gives it a little sniff, and takes a huge gulp. Snatching another bottle as it passes by them, they drink in silence, letting the rocking motion of the ship be their new equilibrium.

 

“Are you afraid?”

 

Lex remains silent, his jaw clenches, as he tips the bottle to his lips.

 

“I always thought you would die because of me.” Her head thumps back against the wall as she watches the lights flicker on the ceiling. “I spent all these years trying to come to terms with death if it meant saving you.”

 

“And yet, your first instinct was to fight back…” he points out, and Lena looks at the dagger lying on the floor a few feet away from them. She did fight back, on that very first second when she saw him lunging at her. It had been instinctive. Primal. It had felt…

 

“I…” Blue eyes appear in her mind eye. A silhouette stands at the top of the stairs. Wisps of golden hair float in the wind. You better stay alive till then.

 

The floorboards creak as Lex crawls toward the cabinet and grabs his pouch of tobacco for a smoke. “It’s not a reproach.” Fumes come out of his nostrils as he breathes out. “To be afraid of death is only natural. We’re animals, Lena, and survival is engraved in us. It’s what has motivated humanity to evolve and progress. We built better tools to feed ourselves, weapons to protect our lands, and to claim more in an endless desire for expansion, for power, for survival—even at the detriments of others. It’s that selfish, innate instinct that makes life such a thrill.”

 

“Is that why you stayed on that burning ship?” Lena asks, recalling Lex’s trail of destruction and the gleeful expression on his face as he tried to kill the last members of the House of El. “You stayed just for… For the thrill?”

 

“Don’t be so judgmental. Of course, I stayed for the thrill. I didn’t know Kal-El would be there, but if he got captured by someone like Rhea then I don’t see why I wouldn’t toy with him a little bit and—”

 

Lena stares at her brother in consternation as he goes on and on about the humiliation he would have wanted to inflict on Kal-El. In a way, she had been right when she told Luke that Lex was obsessed with the Admiral. It’s just so blatant, that even in the crux of his illness, Lex has yet to let go of this rivalry they seem to have cultivated for nearly a lifetime. Listening to him talk and gloat, Lena is ashamed to realize that she had, for a moment, forgotten how immoral her brother could be. He did provoke a war for the sole purpose of controlling the supply of Kryptonite and Lena has a hard time reconciling the brother she knows with the destructive actions the world knows him for.

 

“You could have killed them,” Lena interrupts his tirade with a growl as she remembers the fear on Kara’s face at the thought of losing another member of her family. “You could have started a war you wouldn’t even be alive to see!”

 

“Oh, come on!” Rolling his eyes skyward, Lex kicks a bottle toward her. “Relax, take another drink.” Lena kicks the bottle right back at him, fuming at his flippant attitude, at his poor choices, and egotism. The bottle spins on the wooden floor and bumps against his leg. Lex sighs. “I wouldn’t have killed them. Injure them, yes. Take them down a peg, certainly.” Lex coughs with smoke sputtering past his lips. “Did I tell you how much the Federation has been getting sloppy lately?”

 

“You’ve mentioned it.”

 

“Well, if you think about it, I was doing them a favor. They will now get their shit together with how scared I made them. Hopefully, they will organize in time to put up a fight against Rhea.”

 

“You’re insufferable.”

 

He gives her a smug, little smile that Lena can’t help but roll her eyes at. The scent of tobacco permeates the air and Lena is reminded of their father’s study; of the old mahogany desk and deep red curtains, of the smell of coffee, and the crackling sound of burning wood coming from the fireplace.

 

“Anyway,” Lex coughs, making Lena tense in worry as a bit of blood splatters in the inside of his palm. He wipes it off against his pants and wave away her concerns. “That bit of fun might have cost me weeks of the time I have left.”

 

He rubs the right side of his chest and groans as he cracks his neck. Remorse submerges her as she remembers how hard she had tackled him to the ground, unaware of his condition.

 

“You should be more careful from now on. Don’t risk it.”

 

“And miss out on all the fun? No, thank you. I’d rather fear death than be lumped with those who are afraid of living.”

 

Lena clenches her jaw, her body stiffening at the remark.

 

“Is that what you think I’m afraid of?”

 

“Why didn’t you come home, Lena?” he asks instead, his gaze unrelenting. “I tried to bring you home. I sent so many ships looking for you, and all you did was sent my ships back to me with half of their crew members beheaded.”

 

“Lillian—”

 

“Yes, I’m aware, she bribed a few of them from under my nose to kill you. But nothing was stopping you from trying to reach out to me…”

 

Lena knows why she never tried to make contact, why she had rather run away than confront Lillian and take the risk of going back home knowing of the prophecy. It is for the same reason she pushed Kara away and didn’t fight, like Kara had been, for them to be together. If we weren’t who we are. Would we have had a chance? Kara had asked, full of hope, and she had said no, only because—

 

“I didn’t want to take the chance,” Lena finally replies, feeling the tears threatening to spill again as Lex nods, in that all-knowing way Lena hates so damn much. And it’s strange, because now that she thinks of it, no longer blinded by the lies she believed about herself, Lena can actually believe that it wouldn’t have mattered who they were or the world in which they were stuck in, Kara would have found her and they would have found a way to be together.

 

“It seems like Fate has forced our hands,” Lex heaves a sigh as he stands up. “You being in Almerac at that exact moment couldn’t have been a coincidence.” He coughs again, bending against the table as he is barely able to take a breath before another coughing fit hits him. The ship dips down with a wave and Lex Luthor is too weak not to succumb to the motion. He stumbles, not even able to right himself, and Lena rushes to his side to stop his fall. “I must have done something truly terrible in a past life or something to deserve this,” he mumbles and swats her hands away, making a point of being able to stand on his own. “She cannot see me like this,” he says, taking a deep breath as he straightens his posture and keeps his head high.

 

They both glance at the door.

 

It breaks Lena’s heart, knowing that somewhere beyond that door a little girl is having the time of her life sailing on her father’s ship. Lena has only spent a few days with them and already Lionheart has found a place inside her heart. There hasn’t been a day in which the little beast hasn’t made her break in laughter with her antics and buoyant energy, dragging her around the ship and showing her how to help the deck-hands in their tasks— as if Lena had never been on a ship before— when she wasn’t busy chasing them with her wooden sword. To think of the tragedy awaiting them, of the sadness and the grief Lionheart will have to face as her father’s life only hangs by a thread, is unbearable.

 

But Lena is ready to hold onto that thread for as long as she can even if the outcome is inevitable. Even if it ends in heartbreak.

 

“Maybe fate is being merciful.” Lena catches her brother’s eyes, feeling again like they might already have had that conversation before, or were about to, or maybe left it unfinished. “Maybe, in this life, fate is allowing us to say our goodbyes.”

 

Lex visibly flinches at her words despite the nonchalance of his expression. He hums, sounding unconvinced, despite the unmistakable sorrow in his eyes.

 

“Come with me.”

 

They leave the Captain’s cabin.

 

Despite the early hour, Lena sees every crew members stand on deck. Officers stand vigil by the mainmast while a physician shares a look with Lex as they close the door. All the lights are lit, and for the first time since she came on board the Alexander, it finally hits her.

 

Lena realizes why she’s here and why Lex has been so adamant for her to come back home.

 

Crew members and officers part ways with a reverence that makes Lena choke up with tears. Every salute, every smile, and every look is imbued with some kind of acceptance that Lena has yet to reach, and Lex bears it with the poise of a true Luthor. With a clap of his hands, they all scatter from the main deck to go back to their post. Lena glances over her shoulder and finds Mercy, at the helm of the ship, staring back at them. From this distance, Lena doesn’t know if the woman’s tense posture is a product of wariness or worry. It’s been a lifetime since she’s spent time with one of the Graves siblings and the striking teenager Lena remembers looking up to has become one unreadable woman. Mercy points them to the spot Lionheart has taken refuge. They find her sleeping among a bed of ropes, wrapped in blankets while the governess watches on quietly.

 

The child sleeps peacefully, oblivious of the heavy silence settling around her.

 

“We have one week left before we reach the shore of the Luthorian Islands.” Lex crouches down and brushes a strand of hair out of his daughter’s face. “I arrested Mother for conspiracy with the Daxamite Queen not a month ago but she fled our prison and I fear she might be planning a coup. Otis is holding the fort for now and I paid Rhea more than enough not only to stop coming after you but also to cease any assistance she has been giving to Mother.” Lex sighs and looks to the dark sky.

 

It’s a quiet moment in which Lena’s understanding of fate begins to change. What she thought were chains weren’t chains at all. Kara’s words spread inside her mind like the warmth of a bonfire. No matter how lost we feel or the chaos surrounding us… I’d like to believe there is a path for all of us. The prophecy has never been a curse to fight against or to break. It was a path. A path they’ve been set on from the very beginning, and maybe the only power they have, the only influence they hold in the face of fate, are the choices they make within that path.

 

Lex closes his eyes and takes in a deep breath before focusing back on his daughter. “I’ll give you a rundown of everything you need to know, everything you need to be prepared to take on the Luthorian Islands’ leadership in my stead… and in my daughter’s life,” he adds quietly.
