She grips his shoulder and feels him shudder. Lionheart grunts in her dreams, making her father chuckle. “I don’t know how fate is being merciful… because it is one hell of an agonizing goodbye, my sweet child.”

 

Around them, the sky begins to change color.

 

“I’ll take care of her,” Lena promises, tightening her hold on her brother’s shoulder as it shakes. “Like my own flesh and blood.”

 

With one last swipe of his thumb against his daughter’s forehead, Lex rises to his full height, looking devastated but determined.

 

“Then let this… handover be our goodbyes.”

 

And for the first time, Lena’s very first instinct isn’t to run away. For the first time, she walks the path she’s been set on and dares to see where it leads her.

 

With tears running down her face, Lena clasps her brother’s hand in a binding promise.

 

***

 

It’s not the great sacrifice she had pictured and romanticized ever since she learned about the prophecy. It’s not the total devastation she had pictured it to be. It’s worse. It’s like asking farmers to plant crops in dry earth and barren lands. It’s like asking a mason to build a house on ruined foundations. What fate is demanding of her is to take a chance, to take that first step, to build and build when all this time Lena thought her fate had been one of destruction.

 

Looking at her hands, at the calluses on her palms, at her broken nails and hardened skin, Lena isn’t sure if those hands are made to build. But she doesn’t have time to linger much on that thought before Lionheart bursts through her door, demanding attention, sometimes hugs, but most of all a listening ear.

 

All of those, Lena is all too willing to give.

 

Every day, Lionheart regales her with stories she’s heard or made on the spot, dragging her around the ship on a great adventure as she most oftentimes than not tries to hide from her governess. In those escapades, Lena learns more about her niece than her heart can bear.

 

“Aunt Lena?”

 

“Yes, darling?”

 

Lionheart grabs her hand and they flee down toward the kitchen as the governess calls her name. Once the poor woman is out of sight, Lionheart glances at her with a shy little grin. “We have the same name, did you know?”

 

Her heart clenches at the way her niece hangs on tighter to her hand, her eyes wide, and unsuspecting.

 

“We do have the same name. Do you know why?”

 

Lionheart bounces on her feet with excitement as she nods frantically, making Lena laugh, and she frowns in confusion when the child shushes her, eyes gleaming with mischief. Motioning Lena to stay at the door, Lionheart creeps inside the kitchen toward the unsuspecting cook. The burly man makes no move to turn around despite the loud giggles her niece can’t stifle on her stealth mission. The cook shifts and Lionheart freezes mid-step, Lena catches the glimpse of a smile on the man’s face before he turns his back on them again. Lionheart resumes her prowl, staying low to the ground as she advances with careful, tiny steps, until she reaches the table and roars. The cook, bless him, makes quite the display on being frightened, and the potato he was peeling flies in the air to the delight of the little beast.

 

The potato rolls to her feet. Lena picks it up and tosses it back to the cook.

 

“You have to be more careful,” Lionheart admonishes him, “you have to open your ears or you are going to be scared again.”

 

“Of course, miss, I’ll be more careful.”

 

“Good.”

 

Lionheart climbs up the bench and peers at the food on the table. Lena shares an amused look with the cook.

 

“Can I have an apple, please?”

 

“Most certainly.” He takes his time looking at all the apples, weighing them one by one before presenting one to the child. “The finest apple in all the land, my lady.”

 

Lionheart squeals in delight.

 

“Thank you, sir. Here’s my money.” Lionheart slaps a piece of rope onto the table and makes her way back to her, showing her the apple. “I’ve got the best apple.”

 

“You did,” Lena says, stifling a laugh and nodding to the cook. Lionheart ambles ahead, aimless, and Lena follows dutifully. “I bet it’s delicious.”

 

Lionheart stops dead in her track, mid-bite, and looks at her apologetically before her eyes darts back toward the kitchen. She pats her pockets and groans. “I don’t have any more money.” Her eyes widen when Lionheart holds up the apple. “We can share if you want.”

 

Lena’s heart melts. “I’m alright, thank you.”

 

“But it’s the best apple. You can have a bite.”

 

Lena surrenders to the toothy grin and sticky fingers. She bites into the apple and hums contentedly, humoring her niece.

 

“Delicious?”

 

“Delicious,” Lena confirms. A few minutes after, as they hide beneath a staircase, Lionheart tells her all about the story of the lost princess from whom she got her name; a princess whose family stood steadily in darkness while only she fell into the light.

 

“We’re princesses,” Lionheart proclaims as her story ends.

 

“We always fall into the light?”

 

Lionheart’s only reply is a contented hum as she munches on her apple, and Lena didn’t think her heart could bear more. But— she uses her thumb to wipe her niece’s face of bits of apple— her heart grows, and it makes her wonder if with a little patience and love dry earth and barren lands could be made into gardens.

 

*

 

It takes her five days to refresh her memory on fifteen years of political history and memorize the names of each member of the different House on the Luthorian Islands and those of the preeminent figures of the merchant guild. She spends those long hours inside the Captain’s cabin, listening to Lex talk endlessly about every decision he’s made while governing the Luthorian Islands. Long hours in which she has to put up with his attitude, his arrogance, and his biased view of the world. Long hours in which Lena is the first to witness her brother’s declining health.

 

His body no longer fills his clothes due to his lack of appetite. His temper rises like a tempestuous sea at a moment’s notice, and Lionheart doesn’t understand why her father cannot spend time playing with her like he used to. She doesn’t understand why she’s only allowed inside the Captain’s cabin at lunch and dinner, and it breaks Lena’s heart to hear, every day, the little girl pound her fist onto the door while Lex squeezes his eyes shut, trying hard to stay on task.

 

By the fourth day, his train of thought becomes more and more muddled and by the fifth, it is Mercy who takes on explaining the conflict with Lillian.

 

“I don’t know if it was love,” Lex mumbles, out of the blue, as they are looking at maps of the five islands forming the Luthorian Islands. He smoothens the crease of his sleeves repeatedly, looking tired and wistful.

 

Mercy clears her throat, looking uncomfortable, and points at the west coast of the third island. “This is where Lillian made contact with Queen Rhea a year and a half ago. With Lex’s declining health and Lionheart being so young there had been talks of finding him a successor and since a Luthor has always been at the head of our Islands Lex had been adamant that we were to find you. Lillian hadn’t liked that at all.”

 

“She told me of the prophecy— but I was already feeling weak…” Lex explains, scratching the side of his face as he faces her, his eyes not quite meeting hers. “She said the only solution for me to heal was to kill you and for a while, I’ve considered it.”

 

“What made you change your mind?”

 

Lex falls into a breathless silence.

 

“This is where we believe Lillian is hiding,” Mercy, taking over and pointing at a little dot west of the Luthorian Islands. “But we’ve been too busy thwarting the attacks of her accomplices on the five islands to go after her.”

 

“Is that why Lionheart is here with you?” Lena asks.

 

Lex slams his fist into the table.

 

“She is safer with me,” he utters with difficulty as if each word were hammers rattling inside his brain, “than back home where Mother… wouldn’t hesitate to kidnap her to use her against me. She already tried to take her from me when—” The table shakes from his anger. It swirls in the room like a dark cloud that feels all-encompassing and heavy until Lex grimaces. “I couldn’t let her have another chance to try.”

 

The chair behind him falls to the floor as he is bent in half by a coughing fit. Lena rubs his back as droplets of blood splatters on the various maps.

 

“Stop it,” he hisses, “I’m not dying yet.”

 

He jerks away from her and Lena watches him leave bloody handprints on the table as he uses it for support while he gets away from her.

 

“You should sit.”

 

Lex ignores her. Rolling her eyes, she pushes him into a chair while Mercy fetches him a glass of water.

 

“Drink.”

 

His nose scrunches at the glass of water. Lena waits, crossing her arms over her chest and Lex finally sips at the glass of water.

 

“I kept thinking…” He coughs again before taking another sip and clearing his throat. “Lionheart would be left on her own if killing you didn’t miraculously… cure me.”

 

Lex falls silent again, his gaze far away. Mercy gives her a small, sad smile.

 

“We read every book there was on Silver Banshees and their curses. We even managed to capture and interrogate one only for her to laugh at our faces. We went to priests and fortune readers but… it was too much of a risk and not a gamble we were willing to take.” Lena cocks her head at the use of “we” and at how integrant to Lex’s plan Mercy has been when she had been staying in the shadows ever since Lena stepped on the ship. “The balance between the noble houses of the Luthorian Islands was already precarious before Lillian’s betrayal,” Mercy continues, “with no one at the head of the Luthor House, Lionheart would lose everything.”

 

“Surely the other Houses aren’t waiting to pounce on a child!”

 

“I can only speak for the Graves in this matter and say that we’d protect her with everything we have but the others might not feel so inclined.”

 

“Attracted to the scent of blood, the lot of them…” Lex murmurs. Lena bites the inside of her cheeks as he throws his glass against the wall and slumps in his seat. “My head is killing me…” he groans. With a wave of his hand, he dismisses Mercy out of the room. The woman rolls her eyes, and gives them a sharp nod, before exiting the room.

 

“Are you two…?”

 

“We used to…”

 

“Is she Lionheart’s mother?”

 

He shakes his head, chuckling to himself, and sweeps his arms toward the table.

 

“What are you looking for?”

 

“Take a guess,” he grunts, eyes half-open. Lena sighs, eyeing the cabinet and the bottle of alcohol. She pours him a glass and sets it on the table. “Ha!” He gulps it in one-go.

 

“Tell me about her mother. Do I know her?”

 

“You did. It was Abelard Del Portenza’s daughter. Erica.”

 

Vague memories of a beautiful girl come to her mind. She remembers her always stepping through the mansion door, flocked by cackling aristocratic girls. “Didn’t she decline your invitation to the annual ball once?”

 

“Must you remind me of that?”

 

Lena laughs at seeing him so embarrassed, even after all this time.

 

“Winning Erica over was the hardest part.” His eyes close and a faint smile stretches his lips. “I think she knew the reasons I was courting her were only… political. At first.”

 

“And she agreed to marry you?”

 

“I’m charming.”

 

“And humble…” Lena quips. Lex grunts, disgruntled, despite the small quirk of his lips.

 

“There had been a ball before you disappeared and apparently, you told her that one day you would explore the world because there is more to it than the eye can see… She liked that. Years later, when I asked if we could name our child after you she didn’t even think twice about it. Mother hated it of course. She kept trying to make us change our minds, but we didn’t. We settled for Lionheart as a nickname to stop her from nagging us constantly.”

 

Little footsteps thunder on the quarterdeck above them.

 

“I don’t know if it was love,” Lex says, wiping the blood at the corner of his lips and getting ready to greet the little beast running down the stairs. “She died a few days after she gave birth to Lionheart and… it hurt.”

 

The door of the Captain’s cabin slams open, revealing a little silhouette holding so much of Lena’s heart already.

 

“So maybe it was…” Lex finishes, concealing any signs of discomfort to face his daughter with a mock expression of terror as Lionheart roars at them. “My… my… What a terrifying little beast, you are!”

 

Lionheart giggles.

 

“Can Aunt Lena come and play now? Please?” The little beast adds with a big smile and soft pleading eyes.

 

Lex relents to his daughter’s whims.

 

*

 

She’s been out in the ocean for so long— traveling nowhere, completely aimless— that now her destination is fixed Lena nearly wishes she didn’t have to leave the ocean behind. Standing at the bow of the ship, Lena stares at the horizon and remembers being fourteen and watching the shore of her home disappear behind the fog, terrified. It’s funny. Ten years have gone by and Lena is still terrified, but the fear she is feeling now as the Alexander sails toward the Luthorian Islands blends seamlessly with a nervous kind of excitement.

 

Looking over her shoulder, Lena realizes— now that the anger has abated— that the time she has spent away from home might not be such a waste after all. It’s early morning and Lena helps the deck-hands in their tasks. It’s not what is expected of her but she finds the work relaxing, smiling to herself as memories of being on the Singing Nightmare resurface. She had been of no use then, falling to her knees with every wave and vomiting her guts out on the first days. She had been so uncomfortable with the world and in her own skin that finally getting her sea legs had been an achievement that still fills her with pride. Deck-hands salute her as they get to work. Lena assists the riggers in releasing the sails, checking the rigging as the wind pushes them forward. The work has become so familiar that Lena relishes in feeling her hands and muscles burn from pulling ropes; tasting the salt on her lips as the ship dips and brine sprays their face. If she had stayed home, she might not have had that experience. She might not have met Luke and found in him a confidant she didn’t think she needed. All the people she’s met and all the adventures she’s lived had been because of her choices and Lena cannot find an ounce of regret within her soul at having shared her pains and joys with her crew. Without a doubt, Lena wouldn’t change a thing, for her choices, good and bad, might have just led her to the love of her life.

 

She stares at the horizon, feeling her lungs expand with every breath.

 

“Us, Luthors, we are drawn to beautiful things,” Lex says, joining her at the bow. They watch the sunset paints the sky in golden hues. “I wouldn’t be surprised if you were drawn to her…”

 

“Who?”

 

“Don’t think the way you defended her has escaped my attention. I kept waiting for you to say something but you haven’t. I did share personal things with you…” he says, arching a sly brow.

 

Lena rolls her eyes and presses her forearms into the balustrade, leaning forward. She takes off her hat and fiddles with the edges, feeling the wind caress her skin. “Were you drawn to Kal-El?” She asks instead, full of mischief, as she looks at his unimpressed face from over her shoulder.

 

“I was,” he replies, without an ounce of sarcasm, to Lena’s surprise. It never ceases to surprise her how open her brother has been during this journey, how willing he has been to help her, and how accepting he has been of his fate. Lena has to blink back tears at the thought of losing him now that she has had a glimpse of what their dynamic could be, of what it would be like to be back within the fold of the Luthor family. “I just loved hating him,” he emphasizes with glee. “My hatred was only fuelled by my admiration of him.”

 

“I don’t hate her,” Lena confesses, pressing her shoulder against her brother’s side as he pats her shoulder.

 

“Well,” his lips tug into a smile, “you were always the smartest one.”

 

Behind them, Lena hears Lionheart creeping on the stairs, waiting to pounce on them while the first sights of land appear on the horizon.

 

“Welcome home.” He squeezes her shoulder and catches the little beast before she can jump at him. Lionheart squeals in delight and Lena watches them make their way to the Captain’s cabin where dinner awaits.

 

She joins them not long after that, leaving her hat at the door.

 

***

 

The news of her return spreads like wildfire. Everywhere she goes, Luthorians stare at her as if they’ve seen a ghost. They whisper and point at the scar Lena doesn’t bother hiding anymore, but Lena walks with her head held high as she settles into the political arena of the Luthorian Islands.

 

There are advantages to being known as a ruthless pirate. Members of the other Houses freeze anytime she glances at them, and though it doesn’t stop them from trying to undermine her position as the newly appointed Head of the House of Luthor, it never fails to make the siblings share a smile.

 

Lex’s health is on a slow decline and Lena has to step up more and more each day. It comes to a point where she finds herself making executive decisions on military expeditions.

 

“We received confirmation,” Otis announces gravely, “Lillian has taken over the mine.”

 

Lex stifles a cough, pressing a hand against his lips. He grimaces at the sight of blood on his fingers and stumble to his vanity to rinse them. “Lena,” he grunts, catching her eyes through the mirror.

 

“Show me the map.”

 

Mercy unfolds the map.

 

“The safest bet for her would be to hold up inside the fort.” Mercy points to the little castle symbol. “She’ll have a good view on the coastline, and because of the Kryptonite gunpowder she can keep us at bay twice farther than any normal cannons would.”

 

“It’ll be hard to set foot on the island but it can be done,” Otis says, “we can overwhelm her forces by going all in.”

 

“We’ll lose a lot of ships,” Mercy points out.

 

“And people,” Lena adds.

 

Otis shrugs. “But at least, some of us we’ll be able to take the island.”

 

“No,” Lena asserts. Otis scoff and turns to Lex, making Lena clench her jaw as he waits for Lex to challenge her decision but Lex doesn’t. He mentions for them to proceed and Lena hides her relief by focusing back on the map. Lillian having the control of their supply of Kryptonite is a disaster waiting to happen and time is of the essence to stop her from shipping the deadly gunpowder. “Is there a way to get to the island by the north?”

 

“No,” Lex says. “I laid traps there and there. The only way is through this bay. Otherwise, our officers will be blown to smithereens with one wrong step.”

 

She looks at her brother, trying to gauge if he’s being serious or not, but Lex is being deadly serious. The more she thinks about it, Lex trapping half of an island just to have control of the only Kryptonite mine known in the world isn’t such a stretch, but it makes taking back the island much more difficult.

 

It makes the situation feel all the more real and dire. Lena is momentarily deprived of her breath as the weight of it all overwhelms her.

 

A knock on the door makes them all stiffen.

 

“We’ve stopped a ship from entering the territory at the southern outpost,” says the officer, looking intimidated as all eyes focus on him.

 

“You have my authorization to open fire,” Lex grumbles, frustrated at the interruption, and dismisses him.

 

The officer clears his throat and fidgets nervously. “They… they claim to be Captain Lena Luthor’s crew, sir.”

 

“Tell them to leave,” Lex says through gritted teeth.

 

“Lex…”

 

Lex slumps back in his seat at her warning and sighs. “Fine, let them in.”

 

The officer gives them a sharp nod before exiting the room.

 

“I’ll be back,” Lena declares, sending an apologizing look to the Graves siblings. “It won’t take long.”

 

“It’s getting late anyway,” Lex says, scratching the stumble on his cheeks. “Lionheart must be starving. We’ll continue after dinner.”

 

The Graves’ sibling nod and get out of the room. Lex stops her before she reaches the door.

 

“Don’t be a fool, Lena. You told me yourself,” he says, leveling her with a stare, full of pity and kindness. “Goodbyes can be a small mercy.”

 

Having her words thrown back at her, Lena cannot leave the manor fast enough to stop the dread building inside her heart. Is she what she should do? Say her goodbyes? It doesn’t feel right. Things are definitely changing and though she has agreed to help Lex, she isn’t ready to renounce on any other part of herself.

 

She doesn’t have time to ponder more on it as the Intrepid docks in the harbor. The bustling activities around the port do nothing to calm the beating of her heart. Whispers and stares follow her as she battles her way through the crowd to finally reach the pontoon where the Intrepid finally put down its anchor.

 

The anchor plunges into the water. Ropes are being fastened to beams. The gangplank slides against the wood. It slams into the pontoon.

 

No one comes out of the ship. The sky is darkening around the port. The low activity on the docks makes the strange behavior of the Intrepid even more suspicious to the officers standing by and Lena has to physically interpose herself between them and the ship to stop them from embarking.

 

“Wait here,” she orders. It’s jarring how they listen to her without much of a fuss. Lena still isn’t used to having the Luthorian army be at her command. The weight of responsibilities crushes her shoulder momentarily as she remembers why she’s been entrusted with that kind of power in the first place. Looking at the Intrepid, Lena wonders if she could run away from it. Because if she is honest with herself, Lena is terrified. She doesn’t know what to do with all the duties that have befallen on her as the newly appointed leader of her House. She can’t even stomach the guilt of having to approve of a plan that could potentially send hundreds of Luthorians to their death, and the political game she has to participate in every day just to be heard has been an endless source of anger and frustration. It makes her even wonder if Lex handed her the reigns early so that he could be rid of the headache-inducing meetings she has to have every morning with the other leaders of the Houses. But the most terrifying of it all; the responsibility that makes her wake up at night, drenched in sweat, is wondering how she is going to take care of her niece once Lex is gone. She never took care of a child before. She’s never really been around children. Eli doesn’t count; he was nearly a teen when she met him and she never really had to take care of him. On the rare occasion, Madi would let him get on the ship with them, Lena had just let him hang around and their bond was made naturally. She never really had to think about it, but with Lionheart? Lena feels completely out of her depth here.

 

“I’ll handle it,” she finally says, taking in a deep breath as gets onboard the Intrepid.

 

The welcome is frigid.

 

The twenty-five remaining members of the Singing Nightmare stare at her with their arms crossed. It brings tears to her eyes to think that she’s spent the past ten years with them and to feel the bond they have has started to break. If eyes could kill, Jess would have killed her three times over. Eli is standing in front of the crew as if he had to protect them from her. And Luke… Her eyes scan the crowd for familiar reddish hair and find him, standing at the far back, not even looking at her. Somehow, it hurts worse than their silence and their anger.

 

“Have you elected your new captain?”

 

The crew bristles at her question, but it is a necessary one. Fate might have lighted the way but it has been their choices that have given shape to it. Standing on the deck of the Intrepid— not the Singing Nightmare— and holding the stare of those who have become much more than a crew but a family, Lena feels ready to let go.

 

Because in the end, it’s all about choices. Some are obvious and some feel unexplainable.

 

“I won’t—” Lena wipes the tears she feels falling on her cheeks, and wishes, foolishly, she could go back to the start. Nights when they would drink themselves to stupor and laugh themselves silly. To endless expanse of water and raging wind. But Lena knows it’s her fear talking and her crew doesn’t deserve to have the last act of their captain be one of cowardice.

 

“I won’t try to defend my actions,” she declares, loud and clear and steady. “I know I was wrong. I made you believe everything was alright when it wasn’t. I lied and I left without a word.”

 

The crew grumbles like a raging storm. Lena raises her hands to appease the shouts that resound like thunder in the night and decides to lay it all on the table. Once and for all. “Lex never wanted to have another meeting with Admiral Kal-El. I made that up,” she confesses, looking apologetically at Luke. He scoffs and that sole reaction is enough to hush the storm long enough for her to explain, to tell the truth from the very beginning.

 

“What he wanted was for me to follow him,” Lena says, walking closer, and pushes her hair back so that her scar is fully on display. “Maybe I should begin with that. I know most of you think I got it in a fight. I’ve heard the stories—” Lena looks at Hector pointedly making him startle and shrink inside the crowd. No one has been more eager to speculate on her scar than him. It did help create her persona wherever they went so Lena didn’t stop him from doing it. And, from everything Lena had heard Hector spout, he never once got close to the truth.

 

“I was cur-” Lena stops herself. Was it a curse? Lena isn’t so sure anymore. “I was marked by fate,” she says instead, hearing the crew gasps at the revelation. Hurt flashes in Eli’s eyes as he realizes how much she hid from him and Luke finally meets her eyes, challenging her to say more, to commit to the path she’s taken. “A Silver Banshee’s messenger came to me one night and the Silver Banshee gave me this scar, along with a prophecy. With the blood of your blood on your hands, you’ll be the last sunrise he’ll see,” Lena repeats. It has lived inside her heart for so long Lena feels like it has become part of herself. A manifestation of a loss she couldn’t accept and one she’ll have to face. Maybe that’s why people call it a curse. No one wants to live with the insight of what’s to come next. They would rather live ignorant than be crushed by a truth they cannot accept.

 

“I didn’t know what that meant,” Lena whispers, shrugging helplessly as they all stare at her. “I just knew that I was in pain and that something terrible would happen to my brother and I would somehow be involved. I confided in my step-mother, thinking she had answers, but she only confirmed my worst fears. She told me my brother would die because of me. So she made me leave. Well,” Lena amends, “she wanted me dead, but Luke saved me.” The crew turns back to Luke who only sighs heavily and looks down at his shoes as he rubs his eyes. “I wouldn’t be here without him. He abandoned his life in the Luthorian Islands when he chose to save me.”

 

The murmur traveling among the crew isn’t a raging storm anymore, but her vision is blurred by tears as there is a kind of liberation at finally being able to open herself in that way.

 

“For years, I have run away; persuaded I would be the one to cause my brother’s downfall. I ran from him like the plague despite his many attempts to bring me back. I ran away for so long that it became instinctive and when Lex asked for me to follow him I thought… I thought he wanted to kill me to end the curse. I didn’t want you to get in the crossfire of that. I thought I would make you safer by leaving.”

 

A violent shove makes her nearly lose her balance, after wiping her tears, Lena finally sees Eli, with tears running down his face, standing before her. “Leaving someone behind isn’t protecting them. It’s just letting them fend for themselves.”

 

“You’re right,” Lena amends as he grips her jacket with that same kind of desperate anger he had when they came back to Lian Yu to tell him that his mother had passed away. “I’m sorry,” Lena says, patting his cheek.

 

He pushes her hand away and slinks back to the crew.

 

“What does he need from you?” Luke asks; always the voice of reason, always the one to keep them on track.

 

“Lex is dying,” Lena tells them, heart heavy, as the whispers get louder. She hears them speculate, still not understanding how that loss will affect all of them. But Luke knows, as their eyes meet and Lena finds quiet support in his gaze. And it’s not the conclusion she would have liked to happen. Some part of her wished she didn’t have to quit their way of life and leave the ocean behind as she focuses on governing a nation. But she can’t ignore this anymore. She’s run away long enough and her journey as the Captain of the Singing Nightmare has to come to an end. There’s nothing more to be found beyond the horizon for them. As they were. And Lena cuts through the noise to inform them of her decision. A choice she makes without regrets. “I chose to protect his legacy.”

 

“So you’re staying?” Jess blurts out, incredulously.

 

“What about us? It’s over? Just like that?” They all ask. Among the doubts and the grief, Lena is quick to remind them that though the life they’ve had for the past ten years has come to an end it doesn’t mean it is the end of them.

 

“We’re still the mismatched family Madi put together! We’re still the Singing Nightmare,” Lena promises. “Some things are just… going to change. Admiral Kal-El drafted a pardon for all of us. You could do anything. Be anyone. You could have the life you’ve always dreamed of.” They don’t look convinced and Lena feels helpless but she doesn’t push. Change is scary. She would be a hypocrite if she didn’t acknowledge that for most, it takes time to accept any type of change. She has had a hard time with it and all Lena can do is giving them time. Time to think. Time to get back on their feet. “I might have chosen to stay in the Luthorian Islands but you’ll always find a home with me,” Lena promises, again. “Nothing is going to change that.”

 

But they aren’t listening to her. They have all turned to Luke for guidance and Lena sighs. She wouldn’t have it any other way. Making her way back down, Jess stops her before she can step on the gangplank.

 

“What’s with all the defenses?”

 

“Ah,” Lena looks down at the officers waiting for her down below. “I will leave shortly to stop my step-mother from—” she throws her hands in the air just at the thought “—starting another war, I guess. You’re welcome to stay. As long as you need.”

 

She goes down the gangplank, and with one last look to the people inside the Intrepid, Lena makes her way toward the officers waiting for her.

 

“Captain!”

 

She turns back on instinct despite not being their captain anymore. But a small smile tugs at her lips at seeing most of the crew lean over the railing as Jess shouts to her.

 

“I think we deserve at least one last mission under your command, don’t you think?”

 

The officers look at her, perplexed, as she is hit by a fresh wave of tears.

 

*

 

Standing at the helm of the Alexander, Lena looks around her and cannot believe she is at the command of a dozen warships, with the Intrepid amongst them. The last four days have gone in a blink between strategizing and arguing with the leaders of the other Houses as she had to present her strategy (It’s all a formality, Lex kept reminding her, because, in a crisis like this, the Luthors always have the final say) and just waiting to get to the island where the mine is.

 

It doesn’t help her nerves that with the arrival of the Intrepid, news of another front has run amok within the ranks. A battle is raging back in the Federation. Several ships and cities have been lost and it is said that National City might be next. Its fate solely rests on the shoulders of the Girl of Steel.

 

Lena can’t help but think back on the conversation she’s had with Luke before they left. He had found her wandering the port and invited her to the nearest tavern. Just the two of them. Like old times. It had been hard to convince him to relax, to stop looking over his shoulder like someone might stab him at any moment. She had managed to make Lex revoke the death sentence he had on Luke, but habits die hard and her oldest friend still maintained his vigilance.

 

“It’s always good to be prepared,” he had said, piling his plate with food as a tankard of ale were brought to the table.

 

Lena had rolled her eyes, but she kept from complaining because it felt good to have him back.

 

“I was angry,” Luke had said. “I couldn’t understand why you didn’t tell me anything, but the Commodore stopped us from doing something we would have regretted.”

 

“You wanted to attack Lex’s armada.”

 

“It did cross our minds…” He had smiled and Lena couldn’t help but notice how tired he looked, like the years have finally caught up to him. “She told us to have faith in the path you’ve chosen. To trust and hope. I’m paraphrasing, but you know us, we’re a cynical bunch.”

 

“And yet you’ve listened to her.” She had eyed the last piece of chicken and when she reached for it, Luke beat her to it.

 

“She can be very persuasive and she makes good speeches.” He had munched on that piece of chicken with an air of victory and Lena had known that it was his payback for lying to him.

 

“If you think that’s the right path I’ll support you,” he had said a bit later in the night.

 

“I don’t know if it is the right path, but I’m committed to it.”

 

“Good.” He had raised his tankard. “We’ll see where it leads us, then.”

 

Lena had clinked hers against his, relieved that not all things had to change.

 

Some things are as certain as stars in the sky.

 

The Intrepid sails to her right, and Lena gives Luke a nod as he meets her eyes.

 

“We’re close,” Otis announces.

 

Lena looks to the horizon and sees the first thin shred of a landmass ahead. She motions for the ships to slow to a stop and have the infiltration party get ready. This is another change Lena has to get used to. Not being able to be in the thick of the battle. Don’t die, Lex had told her too weak to even come out of his chambers. Delegate, he had reminded her. Even if your decision might kill them. That is the burden of power.

 

Otis and several other officers disembark from the Alexander to get on board the Intrepid.

 

Luke gives her a nod before the Intrepid disengages from the fleet to head east.

 

This was your plan, Lena has to remind herself as she orders the fleet to go on forward and prepare the canons. She is the distraction. The bait. And if it all goes well, Lillian wouldn’t notice the Intrepid coming from the other side of the bay.

 

Lena closes her eyes, feeling for the wind, and clenches her jaw.

 

“Send in the boats.”

 

An officer relays the order and dozens of boats, loaded with tar, are rowed as close to the shore as they can before the Kryptonite canons from the fort can hit them.

 

An alarm rings out in the distance.

 

All her ships present their portside as the first gleam of green flashes from the fort.

 

The boom of cannons shatters the silence. Water sprays high in the sky as it misses one of the boats.

 

“Abandon the boats!” Lena orders as her officer rings the alarm and deck-hands from all ships throw ropes in the water to help get the officers back on the ship safely. As soon as they get back on board Lena motions for those waiting on the crow nest to fire at the tar.

 

Bullets are fired. Lena hears the faint sound of breaking glass before the tar catches fire. Billowing smoke invades the bay, heading east.

 

Lena smiles as everything goes according to plan. She sets her eyes on the coast where several of Lillian’s ships prepare for a riposte.

 

“Fire!”

 

The ship rocks from the firing of their Kryptonite canons. Lillian might have taken the mine but Lena makes full use of Lex’s reserves as the smoke around them takes in a faint green hue. Lena doesn’t hear anything but the sound of canons. The air is saturated with green smoke. It tastes like salt, iron, and something Lena never felt before. Something that makes the hairs on her arms rise with an impending sense of danger.

 

“Take cover!”

 

Officers scrambled to their feet, throwing their arms over their heads as Lena sees a flash of green headed straight at them.

 

The cannonball sinks thirty feet in front of them, spraying them all in water. It won’t be long before they adjust their aim, Lena thinks, and just as the thought crosses her mind the ship next to them is pierced by a cannonball. The ship tears in two from the impact, making the officers on it call to abandon ship.

 

“Come on,” Lena grits her teeth as her ship prepares another salvo. She orders the deck-hands to fish out the Luthorians as she waits for the signal. An officer nods at her and Lena screams, “Fire!”

 

Pieces of wood fly in the air as one of Lillian’s ships is destroyed.

 

“Tell them to aim for the fort!” She orders her officer who signals four of their ships to concentrate fire on the fort. It won’t reach it but hopefully, it will distract those in the fort long enough not to notice the Singing Nightmare’s crew and Otis’s team.

 

It feels like it goes on for hours. Blood is running down her arm from a cut made from a jagged, flying piece of wood. Her whole body thrums with the booms of canons, and though they’ve managed to take down the ships at the bay, they still can’t get close enough because of the fortress canons. Their range is incredible and frightening. They aren’t even close to getting inside the bay and the fort has already sunk five of their ships. Lena doesn’t want to think about those they’ve lost to the sea despite their efforts to rescue every sailor. Lena doesn’t want to think about those she sent up the hill, braving the traps Lex had left several years ago, just to get inside the fort from the back.

 

Her friends’ lives rest solely on Otis’ memory of the land and his guidance once inside the fort. Lena prays for them to be alright. She hopes—

 

An explosion shatters her train of thought.

 

Lena looks around and sees all the officers doing the same. “Was it one of us?” They all shake their heads, as Lena counts the number of ships in her fleet. “Hold your fire!” Lena barks and looks up the hill. The smoke clears enough for them to see the fort racked with another explosion.

 

Utter silence falls on the bay as they watch evidence of the battle raging inside the fortress. Walls are being blown up. A tower is crumbling. And Lena gives the order to get inside the bay. They fire at the beach where a few canons remain, scattering the people manning them.

 

It is not long before Lena sets foot on the island and sees a white flag flailing in the wind. The trek up to the fort is arduous for the sole reason that Lena has a hard time keeping her legs steady and her heart from tripping over itself at the thought of what she’ll find once she’ll get inside.

 

Cheers erupt as she enters the courtyard. The stronghold lay in ruins and Jess smirks at her, hands on her hips.

 

“Took you long enough!”

 

Eli gives her a nod, sporting a bloody nose and several scratches across his arms. Otis’ arm is thrown over Eli’s shoulders as the Luthorian gives her a mock salute, having the use of only one of his legs to stand upright. Several officers salute her, each bloody but all right. Several crew members of the Singing Nightmare give her a nod. Lena looks for reddish hair in the crowd and exhales a sigh of relief at seeing him behind Lillian.

 

Her step-mother watches her approach with her hands bound and a streak of blood running down her face. Standing tall, nearly regal. Even in defeat.

 

“Hello, mother.”

 

“I take it this is your doing?” Lillian chuckles when Lena doesn’t reply. “You have exceeded all expectations. I truly believed Rhea would have killed you.”

 

“You told her about the prophecy,” Lena states, clenching her jaw. She already had suspicion that it was Lillian who sent Rhea after her, because not many knew of the prophecy, but having Lex reveal that their mother has been working with Rhea all along has been a confirmation Lena didn’t think would cut so deeply. It makes her realize that even after all this time, even now, looking at her and seeing nothing in her eyes but smugness and disdain, Lena had hoped that Lillian would maybe express some regrets at having cast her away from home. “Why?”

 

“To save my boy,” Lillian replies, looking at her as if she’d gone mad. And it is such a clear dismissal of everything Lillian had put her through. All the assassination attempts, the fear, and the grief. It makes Lena scoff, then angry at herself for having a reaction in the first place. For letting Lillian get to her even after all these years. For believing she could have remorse. For thinking Lillian could love her as her own child. “You can’t blame a mother of trying to save her child. Rhea and I surprisingly bonded over that.”

 

“Rhea is going to lose the war,” Lena says, gritting her teeth at yet another jab. “Lex is dying, mother, I had nothing to do with it!”

 

“Yes,” Lillian admits with that smug smile Lena hates so much. “It seems I have backed the wrong horse…”

 

She’s heard enough. Lena gives the order to take Lillian back on the Alexander. She orders the mine caved in before she leaves, despite Otis’ protests. But Lena doesn’t care. Kryptonite has caused enough tragedy as it is. The explosions still echo through her body even hours after the battle has ended. Lena can barely imagine what it must have been like for Krypton. To have a whole landmass burst from the inside because of Kryptonite, shattering the earth and unleashing the rage of the volcanoes nearby. There’s already too much Kryptonite powder circulating in the world because of Lex anyway. If she is to be blamed for the disappearance of the last known mine then so be it.

 

She leaves three ships behind to keep watch on the island until she can send more to relieve them.

 

“You’ve won,” Luke tells her, on their way back to the Luthorian Islands. “How do you feel?”

 

He grimaces as Lena pours alcohol on his back and begins to close up the gash there.

 

“I’m not gonna lie. I feel pretty great.”

 

*

 

They’ve won.

 

That feeling of victory doesn’t leave her until they get back home and Lena sees Lionheart run toward her, arms wide open, only to fall, stumbling on her feet. She rushes toward her niece and checks her body for any injury as the little beast cries and holds on to her neck.

 

A scraped knee.

 

Lena stares at the blood staining her fingers. Her heart lurches inside her chest.

 

With the blood of your blood on your hands, you will be the last sunrise he’ll see.

 

Scooping the little beast into her arms, Lena rushes back to the manor, listening distractedly as Lionheart recounts everything that has happened in her absence. Her heart pounds in worry as the manor comes into view from behind the trees.

 

At her arrival, a housemaid tends to Lionheart’s scrape while Mercy motions for her to follow her.

 

Lex’s breathing is shallow. His skin is so pale it looks grey.

 

Lena kneels by his bed and takes his hand. “Lex, it’s done. We won.”

 

He hums in reply, lips chafed and blue.

 

Lena looks at the physicians, all looking impassive as they stand to the side.

 

“What are you waiting for? Help him!”

 

A hand grips her shoulder. Lena looks up and sees Mercy shake her head.

 

“There’s nothing they can do. I’m sorry.”

 

Lena stares, uncomprehending at the sight before her. Of course, she knew that it would happen. She has watched his health decline dramatically in the last two weeks. She had stayed with him every step of the way, taking the brunt of his anger and his frustration on the bad days and listening to his surprising wisdom on his not so bad days. She knew it would happen and yet Lena feels completely helpless. Her mind becomes blank and Lena wonders if she is even breathing because there is pain inside her chest that just keeps on building and building and Lena doesn’t know what to do with it. Or what to do next.

 

“Does Lionheart know?”

 

Mercy shakes her head.

 

“She has to know,” Lena says as Lex shakes his head. “She needs to know…” Lena insists as the memory of her own confusion at her mother passing haunts her in this moment. Of seeing her head disappear beneath the waves and of her not understanding what was happening. Of her standing paralyzed and not being able to react or to say goodbye as her world shattered before her very eyes. “Please, Lex. Let her say goodbye.”

 

“Not here,” he whispers finally, giving his assent. “Take me outside. Make it seem like a grand adventure.”

 

The news spreads quickly. Night falls and thousand of lights appear on windowsills as they hike up the cliff that overlooks the city and the harbor.

 

Lena holds Lionheart’s hand as several officers lower Lex’s reclining chair to the ground.

 

“Let’s build a fire,” Lena suggests and smiles as Lionheart’s eyes light up.

 

A diffuse kind of pain travels through every part of her body. It feels like walking against the tide. Trudging through the mud. Every movement has a cost. Every movement demands effort. Walking, speaking, breathing. And Lena can barely look at Lex as she hears him guide Lionheart in building her very first fire.

 

The fire is lit. Mercy brings them blankets and she isn’t alone. Glancing over her shoulder, Lena sees Luke build another fire, not too far away. He gives her a nod and Lena has to stifle a sob as the Singing Nightmare’s crew joins him, settling in their spot in quiet support.

 

She knew it would happen. She had spent her life trying to prevent it. And maybe it had been foolish to waste time trying to prevent the inevitable when she could have tried to work on accepting it. Maybe it would have hurt less. Maybe it wouldn’t make her feel so ill-prepared and helpless.

 

Time moves with Lex’s every raspy breath.

 

Lionheart is throwing sticks into the fire and Lena steadies her breathing as she plunges into the unknown. She licks her lips, glancing at her brother before calling her niece.

 

“Darling,” Lionheart perks up at her voice, “come here. I need to tell you something.”

 

But she isn’t prepared. She isn’t prepared for the blank look Lionheart gives her, nor is she prepared for the way her niece scrambles out of her arms to cling to her father as Lex smiles weakly at his daughter. Lena winces at hearing her cry; her heart breaks at hearing her scream and plead her father to stay. What do five-year-olds understand of death? Lena certainly didn’t know a thing when she lost her mother, and she hopes, maybe that by giving Lionheart the chance to say her goodbyes, maybe, maybe…

 

The little beast falls asleep as the stars shine bright. Lex holds his daughter a little longer before giving her back to Lena. Bundling her niece in warm blankets, she lays her on the cot next to her.

 

“It feels like the end, doesn’t it? The end for me,” Lex whispers. “Taken away by a disease we don’t know how to cure. It’s pathetic. But you know what comforts me?”

 

“What?”

 

Lex glances at the other fires burning behind them. “To know it will be just another beginning for her. And for you.”

 

“Lex…”

 

“Don’t worry. I made peace with it.” He looks to the great expanse above and smiles. “I’ve done some good in this life, haven’t I? If my life ends with this one, I’ll be happy.”

 

Lena stares, uncomprehending, as his voice gets quieter and quieter and the light fades slowly from his eyes. 

 

Lex Luthor takes his final breath when the sun breaks on the horizon, his unseeing eyes cast to the sky, as Lena checks for his pulse and finds none.

 

“Rage, rage, against the dying of the light,” Lena whispers solemnly as a goodbye, the same goodbye they told their father so many years ago, and closes his eyes.

 

The funeral is a blur.

 

Lena finds herself to be exceptionally good in the moment of crisis but no one has prepared her for the aftermath. For when the master of the house is gone and the staff walk the halls looking lost. For when she hears cries in the middle of the night of a distraught daughter who doesn’t understand why her father isn’t there to kiss her goodnight. It’s the small things that make the loss feel so big. Like a void, no one will ever be able to fill. Lex has always been a polarizing figure, but in this world, he was a father. A loving father, and his daughter missing him makes the loss devastating to their world.

 

It takes days and days for things to even begin to quiet down. From the preparation to the visits from officials, Lena doesn’t have a minute for herself. She doesn’t sleep very well either, and thus she is immediately awake when she hears footsteps inside her chambers.

 

Lionheart comes to her bed, hair tousled and blinking rapidly.

 

“What is it, darling?”

 

“Does it hurt?”

 

Lena exhales shakily as she feels little fingers trace over her scar. “No, it doesn’t hurt anymore.”

 

“It hurts here,” Lionheart points to her heart and Lena engulfs her in her arms.

 

It’s moments like these that make her feel so ill-prepared for what might be coming next for them. But it’s moments like these that make her feel she’s made the right choice.

 

Several weeks pass and Lena pours herself into the work of leading a nation. She revises every trade agreement, expanding some and revoking others. She strengthens her ties with the other House leaders. She sends letters, organizes hearings with the different guilds, and makes changes inside the Luthorian Islands.

 

No one is more surprised than her to see Olivia Marsdin and Maggie Sawyer standing at the port of the Luthorian Islands, one morning with a proposal. An alliance between the Federation and the Luthorian Islands. To which after hours upon hours of deliberation, all House leaders accept.

 

“Our partnership hasn’t been without challenges but I truly believe you are going to change this nation for the better,” Councilwoman Marsdin says before departing. “The High Council agrees with me. It’s not everyone who can change the mind of stubborn politicians like us.”

 

“I don’t think I can take all of the credit for it.”

 

“True,” Councilwoman Marsdin nods with a small smile, “it certainly helped to have someone like Admiral Zor-El on your side.”

 

The councilwoman walks up the gangplank, leaving Lena perplexed.

 

“Admiral?”

 

Maggie squeezes her shoulder as she passes her by. “Kara got promoted after winning the battle of National City. She’s been overseeing the reconstruction of the city and its surroundings while also drafting the peace treaty with the remaining daughters of the late queen of Daxam.”

 

“How is she… doing?” Lena asks before Maggie boards the ship. She holds Maggie’s gaze despite the amusement she can see in her eyes. What? She’s curious. She only catches bits and pieces from their informants and the passing merchants, and more often than not the news she receives is political. But surely Maggie would know more. That’s what she hopes at least.

 

“From what I heard she’s doing fine,” she says kind of dismissively. Cryptic. “It’s the Girl of Steel, you know. She’s one busy woman.”

 

“Of course,” Lena says, trying to keep the unease and disappointment from showing on her face as she lets her go.

 

The gangplank slides back inside the ship.

 

“We will send our ambassador as soon as we can. Are you sure you don’t want a fellow Luthorian to represent you?”

 

“No, I believe Samantha Arias will do quite well as our ambassador,” Lena says, waving them goodbye. There is no one Lena would trust more than someone who has no interest in politics. Besides, Sam had already established a life in the Federation with Ruby well before any of that happened. It’s a win-win situation if she can have her work there on their behalf and then have her come to the islands every few months for a social visit.

 

The ship leaves the coast, but Lena only needs to walk to the next pontoon to deal with another departure.

 

“A message for me to forward?” Eli asks her as he hugs her. Lena holds him close for a few more seconds before letting him go. “In case I might be running into some blue-coats.”

 

“Besides that message to Sam, no. Not right now.” She hugs Jess next. “I’ll expect you back in a month. You promised. If not I will send every ship in the harbor to look for you.”

 

Eli rolls his eyes at that. “Don’t give me orders, I’m the Captain now.”

 

Jess puffs out a laugh and socks him in the shoulder. “I’ll make sure he doesn’t get into trouble he can’t handle.”

 

Feeling an arm settle over her shoulder, Lena smiles as Luke shoos them away. “Come on you lot! Get on the ship! Let me enjoy my retirement in peace!”

 

The crew groans and it’s not long before the Intrepid— renamed Madi’s Song— leaves the port as well.

 

“In a month,” Lena reminds them.

 

“Yeah, yeah,” Eli shouts back as Luke huffs out a laugh.

 

“I give them two weeks before they come back,” Luke says.

 

“A week and a half.”

 

“Deal.”

 

Time stretches and sometimes passes in the blink of an eye. Luke wins the bet as the Madi’s Song returns to the Luthorian Islands two weeks later with the Reign in tow. Lionheart grows more and more every day, and Lena settles in her new role with the help of the Graves siblings.

 

Lex was right. This isn’t an ending. Just another beginning.

 

Sometimes, Lena makes the trek up the cliff to think. It becomes her spot. If she isn’t in her office or with Lionheart or Luke, people can find her overlooking the city, looking pensive but serene. It is hard to explain, but looking at the world, Lena doesn’t feel as lost or as alone anymore. For the first time, Lena looks ahead of her and doesn’t feel dread. For the first time, Lena feels excited to see what awaits her, knowing full well that she’ll never be fully prepared for it. But it doesn’t scare her.

 

She isn’t afraid of living anymore.

 

*

 

One night, Lena wakes up before dawn and makes her way up the cliff, not really knowing why. She holds her cape closer as the wind raises goosebumps on her skin.

 

The sun rises, and far on the horizon, a ship appears.

 

Majestic. Familiar.

 

Some things truly are inevitable- fated to happen, like it has been written in the stars, and Lena exhales shakily as the El Mayarah draws nearer to the coast.

 

Notes for the Chapter:

There will be an epilogue after this, but the main plot is over. I hope I managed to answer the questions you might have had. If I didn't, fire away in the comment section and I'll do my best to hopefully make it clearer.
The epilogue is just gonna be fluff, maybe a bit of smut, a lot of feels, and it's really just going to be domestic supercorp.
Thank you for reading. Stay safe!

17. Epilogue

Notes for the Chapter:

This is it! I'm kind of speechless and emotional right now. It has been such a long time coming. I wouldn't have been able to write this story without Seriouslyficent's support, listen_to's insight, and without Earthling3 and Rebellion_Bear's help. Thank you! And thank you all for the continuous support and love you sent me for the past three years. Let's finish this journey together!

 

Beyond the horizon, the sun rises with bright and fiery colors. It doesn’t wait for Lena to compose herself or give her time to calm her wildly beating heart. The sun has a will of its own, and it might be the Luthor blood running through her veins that makes her run back to the city in some sort of daring defiance. Kara, just like the sun, is an uncontrollable force of nature; a fire Lena had been fascinated and burned by. And it is definitely her hubris that makes her want— crave— to meet that fire head-on. She’s running so fast it feels like she’s flying and Lena laughs loudly in the early morning, watching flocks of birds fly away from nearby trees. Her lungs are burning. Her legs are on fire. She could be a ball of flame right now and it wouldn’t surprise her. Running her hands across the tall grass, Lena imagines herself scorching the earth in her path and soaring into the sky, high, high to an inevitable collision.

 

She might burn her wings on the way up. She might fall. But the possibilities of what that collision may bring far outweigh the risk or the pain it might also involve, and Lena doesn’t want to live in fear anymore.

 

The wind snatches her cloak in her mad dash back to the city. Breathing heavily, Lena jogs down the streets, watching the lights appear inside windows and fumes sputter out of chimneys. A rooster crows in the distance and the warm scent of bread fills the streets, making her mouth water. She slows to a stilted, dignified walk when she crosses paths with the baker.

 

Labellemiche, now in his late fifties, comes out of his shop looking worriedly down the street while clutching a rolling pin. “Is there anything I can do for you, Chancellor?” He asks, glancing at where she came from and tightens his grip on his makeshift weapon. “I saw you running like a Banshee from my window… You just say the word.”

 

“No, Labellemiche,” Lena quickly reassures him, grabbing his arm before he whacks anyone. She laughs as he insists. It’s endearing how more than a decade has gone by and the baker still steps out of his shop, like an old reflex, to defend her. It had been from children back then. Children from aristocratic families who had nothing better to do than chase after the bastard child of the Luthor House. Labellemiche had been frightening back then; towering over them like a giant. A buttery-smelling giant who filled her pockets with pastries and loaves of bread. Now his hair has gone grey. His belly has become rounder with age and hearty food. And kindness still radiates off of him like the heat of his ovens on a cold morning. She pats his arms and walks him back to his shop. “Everything is quite alright. A ship is nearing our coast and… I got a bit carried away.”

 

He stares at her, blinking owlishly, as she gives him an awkward smile, and he points to his hair. Frowning, Lena runs her hand through hers and finds a leaf in there.

 

“Right,” she chuckles, feeling strangely weird and clumsy today. “Thank you.”

 

He hums, adjusting his apron around his waist and glances down the street once more before his stance softens when he realizes no one is chasing her. It is the heat. It’s definitely the heat coming from the bakery that makes her cheeks redden at the shrewd look he gives her. “You were heading for the port then?”

 

“I was…” Lena replies, returning a smile to the wool seller pushing his cart to the marketplace. “But I—” She runs her fingers through her hair once more as Labellemiche raises an eyebrow at her. “Maybe I should go back to the Manor instead. Make myself more… presentable?”

 

His booming laugh startles her.

 

“You do that.” He gets inside the bakery and comes out a few seconds later with a cloth filled with pastries that he dumps in her arms and a warm cloak that he fastens around her shoulders.

 

“Oh!” She laughs, tightening her arms around the sweet-smelling bundle of joy. The warm scent of bread fills her nostrils and Lena inhales deeply at the sweet memory of them sharing bread over the counter on rainy days. “Thank you. You are the best baker in town,” she says, nudging his shoulder as he waves her compliment away with an embarrassed laugh.

 

“Come visit soon we have plenty more inside.”

 

“I will,” she replies and steps away from the old bakery she spent so much time in. The building hasn’t changed much since she left. The stones look a bit older but peering through the window the inside is still as warm and as welcoming as she remembers it. “Thank you,” she says, lifting the bundle in her arms and makes her way back to the Manor.

 

She stops in her track as Labellemiche calls out her name.

 

“Must be someone special to have you running like that,” he shouts with a teasing grin in his voice that has Lena rolling her eyes. She has to stop introducing Luke to the people she used to know. His bad influence has clearly rubbed off on them. No more late nights drinking at the Woodsman’s Corner reminiscing about their old days out on the sea either, Lena decides, whirling around and winking at him.

 

His booming laughter follows her down the street. Clutching the cloak tighter around her body, Lena watches the city bustles with activity as the sun gets higher in the sky. She walks to the rhythm of hooves hitting the cobble street, sellers shouting their prices while showing their wares, and laughing children running to school. Now that the surprise and novelty of her return has dwindled a bit, Lena can take a stroll in the city like she used to as a child. Well, not exactly. People greet her now. They stop her mid-walk for a quick chat and show her their products. They give her samples of their butter, honey, jams, and dry meat whenever she goes by the marketplace. Lena knows her popularity is due to her influence with the Merchants Guild and to the trade agreements she drafted and signed with Councilwoman Marsdin. More ships have come to the harbor in the past few weeks than in the last year and the ports of the Luthorian Islands have never been as busy. It’s not what she had expected when she took on Lex’s legacy. Prevent a war? Sure. Putting an end to his shady deals? Done. Managing the economy of a struggling country? That had been fairly low in her expectations. But now, the demand for their wool and wheat has doubled while commissions for their stained-glass have experienced a definite increase. It has been quite common to see Durlans come to the once secluded Islands to admire the architecture of the city or enjoy their homebrew ale in one of their numerous taverns. There have even been excursions organized for travelers to enjoy one of their hot-springs for the modest sum of two gold pieces. Once words go around, Lena wouldn’t be surprised to see an uptick in visitors in the oncoming years.

 

There are still a few people who look at her and whisper. Her past as a pirate is no secret and Luke— her eyes and ears— often reports to her the latest gossip being spread about her or the Luthor House in the seedy parts of the city. It doesn’t surprise her for he always had a knack of getting his fingers on the pulse of any city they stopped at. He knows how to stay hidden and gather information inconspicuously. A thing Lena wasn’t that great at and one of the reasons why he often left her on her own in the early days of their escape while he scoped out the city. She isn’t sure what he’s doing now exactly. To her knowledge, he has reacquainted himself with former contacts, and though some of the gossip is hilarious, Lena makes sure none of it impacts Lionheart directly. It’s another side of Lex’s legacy she hadn’t expected. To feel so protective of a child she could move heaven and earth so that no harm comes to her.

 

She heaves a breath as the Manor seems to appear out of the fog and morning mist. She might still have an hour left before the El Mayarah docks in their port, and Lena is sure Mercy is already waiting for her to report the sighting of a Kryptonian ship on their shore. A chill runs down her spine. The sun isn’t high enough to offer any warmth from her cooling sweat.

 

She goes around the side to get to the cellar. As her hand pushes against the dark wood, Lena wonders if that is what fate wanted her to do by stepping up after her brother’s death. If those hands, which have dealt death and worn blood as gloves, can lay strong enough foundations for Lionheart to thrive.

 

There are nights she wakes up in fright after dreaming of countless lifeless eyes staring back at her and of fields of grass drenched in blood. Sometimes she would dream of long, underground corridors, lit only by flashing red lights, and more often than not, Lena would stumble out of bed to find her chamber pot and spit out the feeling of blood sitting heavily on her taste buds. It’s always on those nights that Lena can’t shake off the disturbing feeling of hearing Lex’s last words, over and over again. In different circumstances. Sometimes strung with different words.

 

Closing the door behind her, Lena greets the kitchen’s staff. The room is bustling with activity already. Lena informs them that she is expecting guests and that the food they usually serve for breakfast might not be enough to feed them. The cook laughs it off but Lena remains deadly serious while giving her the loaves of bread Labellemiche gave her.

 

“How— how much more?” She asks, her eyes round and glancing at the bubbling pots she already has on the stove.

 

“Double it.”

 

Lena laughs at hearing the poor woman send a prayer to the sky while ordering the staff around. We’ll make a feast worthy of a god, Chancellor, is all Lena hears before the clanking of pans and pots increases in an organized chaos.

 

Kara Zor-El is no god.

 

But she is no ordinary woman either.

 

Mercy greets her at the bottom of the stairs and makes her report while they walk to Lena's office. Two ships are approaching the coast according to Mercy. One is Kryptonian while the other seems to be a vessel from the Federation. Smaller, compared to the Kryptonian one, but could still take on any of their ships in a fight.

 

“The House leaders are nervous,” Mercy adds in a monotonous tone that does nothing to hide the disdain she feels for them. “Their main concern is that no Kryptonian ships have gotten close to our shore since the War.”

 

Lena opens the door of her office while acknowledging their concern with a hum of understanding. She has spent so much time around Kryptonians, as unbelievable as it may sounds, that it makes her forget that Luthorians might not be as accustomed to them as she is. She unfastens the cloak from around her neck and drapes it on the back of her chair, making a note to herself to return it to Labellemiche.

 

“On a scale of one to ten,” Lena opens her wardrobe and stares at all her formal attire and dresses she keeps in her office, “how would you rate their unease of the situation?” She takes one of her most intricate dresses out of the rack, admiring it quietly, but puts it back after seeing the look on Mercy’s face.

 

“Not enough to organize a coup, but enough to make your life a living hell at the next meeting.” Mercy huffs at her second choice and nudges her aside to peruse her wardrobe. “I’d say a solid seven. Are we trying to impress someone?” The question is asked innocently enough that Lena nearly utter an undignified yes before she catches herself in time at seeing the way Mercy’s mouth curls into a smirk. “Because if we are, I’d strongly suggest this one.” Mercy chose a deep blue dress with gold and white motifs around the waist, holding it up to Lena's neck with an approving smile. “It’ll show off your… assets,” she finishes with a low husk in her voice as her eyes travel down to her chest in such a suggestive way that it makes Lena blush.

 

“Right! Well… Thank you,” she says, clearing her throat, and grabs the dress from Mercy’s hands. “I’ll deal with the other House leaders at the next meeting. Make sure Luke is among those who welcome our guests at the port. He’ll tell you if there is anything to worry about.”

 

“Will do!”

 

Lena rolls her eyes at the smug smile lingering on Mercy’s face as the woman closes the door behind her. What is happening? Has Luke shouted the details of her affair to the whole city or is she that transparent?

 

Her cheeks burn to the touch and Lena smiles disbelievingly. She is that transparent.

 

Damnit.

 

Kara Zor-El is no god.

 

But Lena feels like it would be less daunting to impress a god than to face the woman who has stolen her heart.

 

*

 

Her heart leaps inside her throat as she is startled by a knock on her door. Has the El Mayarah arrived yet? Where has the time gone? Lena takes a moment to check her reflection. She adjusts her dress and her hair before opening the door.

 

She frowns at seeing Luke’s eyes widen.

 

“What?” She grumbles, straightening her posture self-consciously.

 

“Nothing,” he replies and yet looks like he’s dying to say more. “You look nice.”

 

He doesn’t say anything else, but Lena doesn’t like the glint in his eyes. “I’ll have you know that I like to wear dresses now and then.” His lips curl into a slow smile, making her groan out loud. “This one is quite comfortable,” she adds, smoothing the creases on her dress as Luke snorts.

 

“I assume you are aware that the El Mayarah has docked in our port?” He asks, changing the subject, and motions for her to follow him out of the office.

 

“I was informed, yes,” she replies, trying to sound nonchalant as she follows him and holding onto his arm to quiet her nerves.

 

Luke side-eyes her and Lena resists the urge to elbow him.

 

“They’re waiting downstairs.”

 

“Anyone we know?” Lena asks, wishing he would just confirm Kara’s presence. It is Kara’s ship after all, but what if Kara isn’t there and she is getting her hopes up for nothing?

 

Luke doesn’t bother to hide the mirth in his eyes. “Oh, you know… Some blue-coats. The uniform makes it hard to distinguish any of them.”

 

Lena narrows her eyes at his shit-eating grin. Standing on the top of the stairs, she takes a deep breath, steeling herself to face whoever awaits her downstairs. “Shall we?”

 

“Don’t look too excited.”

 

“Shut up.”

 

The walk to the parlor where the Federation’s delegation is waiting feels like sailing on an endless ocean. It’s excruciatingly long and slow, and in a complete discordance to the beating of her heart. For all the insolent confidence she projects, Lena isn’t that brave when it comes to matters of the heart. It is easier to erect walls and stew in the hurt than rise above it. It is easier to be angry at the world than try to forgive it, and Lena had been so angry and hurt that it took the quiet unraveling of the Girl of Steel to show her one could be full of wounds and still be standing on their feet.

 

A warm hand settles on the small of her back, making Lena breathe shakily while staring at the closed door of the parlor. She can hear voices beyond the wood; quiet, indistinct, and muffled by the thundering of her heart.

 

“Ready?”

 

Lena nods as Luke opens the door.

 

The rustling sound of clothes fills the sudden silence. Cups of tea knock softly against saucers and half-eaten biscuits clatter against the plates as silhouettes rise on their feet at her arrival.

 

Lena barely focuses on any of it as her eyes lock on blue ones.

 

Blue eyes she had first seen on one fateful night in National City. They had pierced her soul from across the bar and invoked feelings so big inside her chest, so beautiful, that it ached. And Lena knows, stripped of all illusion and deceit, she had started falling in love with those blue eyes right there and then. Long before she even knew who they belong to.

 

“Breathe,” Luke whispers, guiding her to her seat. Lena smiles gratefully for her legs feel quite shaky at the moment from Kara’s unwavering gaze on her.

 

Everyone settles back on their seat, but Kara is all her heart can focus on.

 

“I heard of your promotion,” she says, her voice faltering slightly at the softening she sees in those blue eyes. “Congratulations, Admiral.”

 

“No more impressive than yours,” Kara counters easily, leaning ever so slightly forward so that Lena holds in a breath at feeling Kara’s striking presence wash over her. “Chancellor.”

 

Blue eyes scorch a path down to her lips, the column of her neck, and going as far down as to the top of her breasts before they snap back up to meet her gaze, hazy and unsettled.

 

Lena cannot say what would have happened if there hadn’t been several small tables full of biscuits and teacups separating them. If there hadn’t been several people who must be gawking at them right at this moment.

 

She winces as she swallows a scalding hot mouthful of tea, but the pain is enough to jostle her out of Kara’s gravity and delay the collision. She tries to hide her surprise at seeing that Captain Danvers and Captain Olsen are also in the room, munching on their biscuits while staring at Kara like she’s completely gone off the rails.

 

The tension lingers for a few more moments until Lena composes herself enough to send a warning look to Luke and Mercy not to say a thing.

 

“You caused quite a stir arriving here unannounced,” Lena says deciding to look at Captain Danvers this time, lest she lose herself in fantasies where she would cross the room and straddle the Admiral’s laps to kiss her senseless. “To what do we owe the pleasure of your visit?”

 

Captain Danvers clears her throat loudly and nudges Kara not so gently. Kara stares at her sister, uncomprehending, before realization washes over her. She smiles and rubs the back of her neck, looking nervous and hesitant all of sudden.

 

“Well,” Kara pauses, seemingly looking for words as Lena hangs on from the edge of her seat. “It is my understanding that Councilwoman Marsdin informed you that the High Council would be appointing an ambassador to represent and strengthen the alliance between the Federation and the Luthorian Islands?”

 

“Yes,” Lena trails off, suddenly feeling unsure and uneasy and confused at the way Kara holds her gaze in such a dispassionate way that it feels like they are no longer separated by flimsy tables they could jump over but by an entire ocean.

 

“The High Council chose…” Kara pauses, licking her lip, and looks at Captain Olsen, her eyes seeming to beg for… something. But Captain Olsen remains silent despite the subtle frown settling over his brows. “Captain Olsen, here, has been appointed to be the Federation’s ambassador,” the Admiral declares with a clench of her jaw, looking at her dead in the eyes. Lena doesn’t know what unsettles her more. The High Council appointing Captain James Olsen, a known skeptic of the Luthor name, as their ambassador or feeling Kara completely shut her off so suddenly. “It is an unprecedented position and we were sent here,” the Admiral continues, gesturing to herself and Captain Danvers, “to oversee that it all goes well. For both the Federation and the Luthorian Islands.”

 

To say Lena is disappointed would be an understatement. She swallows around the lump inside her throat and chances a glance at Luke who looks as confused as she is. What did she expect? Lena holds the Admiral’s gaze and finds nothing but some sort of quiet indifference. The room seems to sizzle with unspoken tension but Lena isn’t sure of what she is feeling anymore. She might as well be projecting...

 

“Alright then,” she exclaims, slapping her palms against her thighs to jostle herself out of her thoughts. It seems to startle the Admiral as well, but Lena doesn’t want to linger any more than necessary on the chaotic feelings Kara invokes in her. She’ll deal with them later. Alone. And preferably with a glass of whisky. “I guess I should give you a tour, Captain Olsen. Show you a glimpse of what your life will be like from now on and what the Luthorian Islands have to offer.”

 

“Yes,” he replies, sounding oddly uncertain. “Thank you.”

 

Lena motions for him to follow her, but he hesitates. She eyes him curiously as he seems to be waiting for the Admiral’s permission. The Admiral nods, tight-lipped, as Captain Olsen turns back toward her and gives her a small contrite smile.

 

“Lead the way.”

 

“Actually,” Luke exclaims, making her glance at him from over her shoulder. “Why don’t I show the good captains here around? Miss Graves can come with us and we’ll show them the barracks and all that.”

 

Mercy shrugs, seeming not to care either way.

 

Luke clasps Captain Danvers’ shoulder and guides her out of the door. “Don’t I owe you a drink? And you, Captain Olsen? Wouldn’t you like a fresh beer after such a long journey?”

 

“That early in the morning?”

 

“We have beers so clear it looks like piss, but I can promise you it is the best beer around,” she hears Luke say as they all walk away.

 

The rustling of clothes from behind her makes her close her eyes and breathe unsteadily. Every step Kara takes as she gets closer is like the warmth of a fire burning her back, and yet Lena can feel the hesitancy in each of one of them. Looking at her hands, Lena wonders if those hands are made to build. If they haven’t been covered in too much blood to love.

 

The world stands still as Kara stops moving.

 

It would be easier to face a god, Lena thinks distractedly as she turns around and familiarizes herself again with shoulders that never seem to relax, even in time of peace, with corded muscles straining with every emotion on a proud and statuesque neck, and lips always curling into a smile, even when they tremble with infinite sadness.

 

She’s falling.

 

“You look well,” Lena whispers, afraid to disturb the silence that has always seemed to grant them some sort of privacy. Away from the world and its expectations. Her smile falters as she notices a faint, jagged line on the side of the Admiral’s neck, going up to her jaw. Lena stops her traitorous hand from reaching out at seeing Kara’s breathing tremble. “I’m sorry,” she says, bringing her hand back against her chest, feeling her heart quiver beneath her skin.

 

Kara seems disappointed and Lena doesn’t know what to feel anymore. The room feels charged. Charged with anticipation, with something waiting to happen, something inevitable... Lena doesn't know where to look, what to say, what to do, but Kara doesn't seem to know either and it makes Lena breathe a little easier knowing they are both equally lost. Waiting, wanting...

 

“Chancellor!” A strained voice calls out to her, cutting Kara off in whatever she was about to say. Lionheart’s governess hastens toward her, uneasy and anxious. Kara takes an authoritative step forward and stands directly behind her; her hand settling protectively at the small of her back.

 

“Chancellor, I’m sorry,” says the poor woman. “She refuses to let me in.”

 

“It’s okay,” Lena tells her with a smile, while reaching for Kara’s arm to appease the tension she can feel pulsing at her back. “I’ll make sure she gets ready for breakfast,” she looks at the clock, “late breakfast,” she amends with a wince. “Will you accompany Admiral Zor-El to the dining room? You must be hungry after that long travel,” Lena smiles, squeezing Kara’s arm once more. “I’ll join you shortly.”

 

Lena hurries out of the door and stops mid-way up the stairs at hearing Kara call her name.

 

The Admiral apologizes profusely to the governess before catching up to her. “Would it be alright if I were to come with you? It seemed serious…” Kara trails off, her brows knitting in a frown.

 

“Sure.” Dismissing the governess fidgeting at the bottom of the stairs, Lena grabs Kara’s hand and laces their fingers together. Kara becomes absolutely still, hard as stone, making Lena fear she might have crossed some boundaries until Kara squeezes her hand back in return. Lena steers her toward Lionheart’s room. “Let’s face the little beast together.”

 

“The little what?” Kara asks, making her smile at how apprehensive she sounds.
