But Lena doesn’t have time to elaborate for she hears her niece crying down the hall and hurries to the closed door. She motions for Kara to wait outside before opening it. It budges but only slightly as Lena realizes Lionheart is blocking it from the other side.

 

“It’s me, darling.”

 

A little body scurries away.

 

Kara observes her curiously as Lena takes a deep breath. Giving Kara a tentative smile, she pokes her head inside the door.

 

Light filters through the partially opened curtains. Pillows litter the floor while the sheet hangs from the bed, half on the floor. Her eyes immediately zero in on the trembling little lump, wrapped in a blanket, in the middle of the room. Lena kneels by her niece’s side and rubs her back; smiling when dark blonde hair pokes out from the blanket.

 

“Hi,” she whispers, lying down next to her niece, and props her head upon her hand. “You scared off your governess. Again.”

 

Lionheart doesn’t reply. Only sniffles.

 

Lena keeps rubbing her back, feeling the tension seep away from her little body. “Wanna sleep a bit more?”

 

The little beast crawls inside her arms without a word and Lena holds her close, waiting for her to wind down.

 

From what her governess had told her, Lionheart always had difficulty getting ready in the morning but the tantrums never lasted more than five minutes, just the time for her to wake up properly. Lionheart wasn’t a morning person, which Lena understands. It only happened twice, but some of the tantrums have now turned into full-blown screams and cries since Lex’s death. The governess had been so startled the first time it happened that she had come running to her door, asking for help. Lena isn’t sure if what she is doing is helping, but so far, it seems like having twenty more minutes of peace, quiet, and a good cuddle has been enough to make Lionheart ready to start the day.

 

She lifts her head off the floor at hearing someone shuffle at the door. Her eyes lock with Kara’s who leans against the doorframe, watching them with unreadable eyes.

 

Lex’s daughter, she mouths. Kara nods and points down the hall. Lena closes her eyes, smiling appreciatively, as she listens to the sound of her fading footsteps.

 

A grumbling sound disturbs the relative silence after a while, making her focus back on Lionheart who shifts inside her arms.

 

“I’m hungry,” she whines, making Lena laugh. She brushes the hair out of her pouting face and gets on her feet.

 

“Should we get ready for breakfast?”

 

Lionheart nods, seeming in a better mood as she picks up the pillows off the floor and throws them on the bed, laughing when they bounce off. Lena laughs while watching her wrestle with her clothes.

 

Walking down the hall, Lena sees Kara waiting for them at the bottom of the stairs, and slows down her pace.

 

“There are guests who will be eating breakfast with us today. One of them is standing right there,” she says pointing at Kara who waves at them. “She is very special to me.”

 

“Okay,” Lionheart replies timidly, shuffling a bit as they get down the stairs.

 

Kara introduces herself, crouching to Lionheart’s level with her hand outstretched. Lionheart doesn’t roar, nor does she bite Kara’s hand like she did with Luke on their first meeting. She hides behind Lena's legs instead, but Kara’s smile doesn’t waver.

 

Lionheart hides even more as her stomach grumbles again.

 

“I’m so hungry,” Kara bemoans suddenly, flashing Lena a smile that has her swooning on the spot.

 

“Me too.” Lena hears Lionheart mutter.

 

“Race you to the dining room?” Kara challenges them, making Lionheart sucks in a breath.

 

Lena huffs a laugh. “You don’t even know where the dining room is.”

 

“I know!” Lionheart pipes up, bouncing on her feet.

 

“Oh yeah?” Kara’s eyes light up. “How about you show me the way and we’ll race Lena there?”

 

“Okay!” Lionheart shouts as she runs down the halls.

 

“You are trouble,” Lena accuses her, watching Lionheart roar at the maids while she runs around them.

 

“No more than you.”

 

The corners of Admiral Zor-El’s mouth twitch upward, seeing Lena blush.

 

Lionheart runs back to them and grabs Kara’s sleeve. “Come on! Let’s go!”

 

Lena laughs as Kara, bent in half, is dragged down the hall.

 

*

 

The day has gone by faster than Lena expected. Easier.

 

Kara does have a knack to make friends wherever she goes. She shouldn’t have been surprised to see the Admiral mollify the hard-headed House leaders with a few words and a smile when she befriended a rat on Roulette’s ship less than a year ago.

 

Lena sighs, watching the dark sky. The delegation has planned to stay three more days before James Olsen would take his functions, but she doesn’t know how she is going to survive three whole days with having Kara looking at her like that and stand so far away. She doesn’t know where they stand either. A lot can change in three months. Kara certainly seems like she came back from hell.

 

Lena whirls around at hearing someone trudge through the tall grass.

 

“Hi,” Kara announces herself sheepishly. “Luke took everyone out for a drink and I saw that you weren’t there so… He told me I could find you here.” Kara’s gaze lands on the fire, on the log she’s sitting on, and the heavy cloak wrapped around her shoulder. “But I can go,” she offers quietly, pointing her thumb back to where she came from, looking ready to bolt at any indication that her presence isn’t welcome.

 

“I wouldn’t mind the company,” Lena says, shuffling a bit to give Kara space to sit. Their interactions have been so tentative, so fraught with nerves and indecision that Lena wishes she could just— make it better somehow. She nudges Kara’s shoulder and smiles when the Girl of Steel startle. “Yours is always welcome.”

 

With a brief and relieved smile, Kara looks at the flames and holds her hands out closer to the fire. It crackles softly, like a purring cat under the care of their master. Lena smiles at the ridiculous thought while watching the flames dance in a slow, languorous seduction. But it’s not enough to sway Admiral Zor-El into letting Lena have a peek at what turmoil she’s hiding. Everything is kept close to the chest. Everything is concealed under quiet confidence and a small smile. The only tell betraying the Admiral is a little crinkle between her brows, and all Lena can do is wait, speculate, and try to guess what’s brewing inside the woman sitting by her side.

 

Kara stares at her hands and flexes her fingers, maybe asking herself if those hands are made to build.

 

Lena grabs one of those hands and presses it against her body, feeling it tremble between her palms as Kara stares at her slack-jawed.

 

“You don't have to hide from me, Kara. I know,” she whispers, pressing their hands deeper into her chest. Lena knows what Kara hides behind her smiles and what she leaves unsaid. Lena knows of the anger and the sadness, and she’ll wait. She’ll wait for when Kara is ready to share those thoughts with her.

 

“This is where Lex died,” Lena confesses, changing the subject and sharing a bit of herself like Kara had done when Lena had needed it the most. “He died peacefully. I think. Surrounded by his family.” Lena clears her throat as she holds on to Kara’s hand for strength, support, or warmth. She doesn’t really know. All the above maybe. “I saw him watch his last sunrise,” she says with a painful but grateful smile. It doesn’t stop her from wondering if there are things she could have done better, or things she could have said that would have made his passing easier… “He—” Lena shakes her head and smiles apologetically. “Sorry. I know he was despicable to you…”

 

“No,” Kara hastens to say, inching closer. “I mean yes. He did try to kill us. But… he could have been worse.”

Some stories unfold in unexpected ways…

 

Sitting at the top of the cliff, bundled in blankets, and warmed by a bright fire, Lena holds Lionheart close to her and smiles.

 

It had been worth it. All of it.

 

Her smile gets even bigger at feeling the sun settle against her back.

“You’re just in time.”

 

“I didn’t want to miss watching the sunrise with you,” Kara whispers as they stare at the line where the sky meets the sea.

 

Beyond the horizon, the sun rises.
